# Changelog

Generated by o-inventory from the snapshots in ~/hub/inventory: one entry per snapshot, newest first, only the concepts that changed. Every line but Commits is derived from the diff; `fixed:` lines quote `fix:` commit subjects.

## Unreleased

- Fixed a blank outline row drawing its box or bullet after the open line
  (far right of the column) instead of before it: with no label, the whole
  first cell is the line's prefix. Consumer: matomeru's task outline (a new
  task's `[ ]`).

## v0.126.0 (2026-11-11)

- The `?` help follows the bindings. Any change to a binding table (the
  help's own bind, a hand-edited `bindings.json` read by `binding.reload` or
  `config.reload`, a default set from code) bumps the bindings generation
  and the open help reads its views again after the next update
  (`Runtime.syncHelp`), so the table's rows and the here and pick views show
  it without reopening; before, only the help's own bind refreshed it.
- The help's `b` captures a sequence. A key that starts a bound sequence
  (`g`, with `g g` bound) goes on to the next key instead of asking at once;
  a key that starts nothing still binds at once. `enter` binds the keys so
  far, `esc` cancels, `backspace` drops the last key, `ctrl+v` takes the
  next key as it comes (so `enter`, `esc` and `backspace` can be bound); all
  are rows of `bindings/help.json` in the capture mode. `B` captures keys
  until `enter` (three keys, or a key that is bound alone as the first). The
  keys so far show above the list. A key that would cut a bound sequence
  short, or sits behind a shorter bound row, asks to replace and names the
  sequence. The row written is the sequence row a hand-written file has. New
  verbs `bindings-help.bind-start`, `bind-finish`, `bind-drop`,
  `bind-literal`, `bind-cancel`; `bind-key` takes `literal`. Setting
  `help.bind-sequences` (default true; off: `b` binds the first key pressed
  at once, as before, and `B` is not offered).

## v0.125.0 (2026-11-11)

- Outline rows reorder the way the rows are drawn. `ochazuke.outline.move`
  takes a `count` (`3J`, `3K`; the bindings' `from {count}`) and stops at
  the edge, and `to` (`first`, `last`; `g K`, `g J`) goes to the first or
  last place among the siblings. New setting `tree.move-spill` (Lists,
  default off, which keeps the old stop): with it on, moved past the last or first sibling
  a row steps out of its parent to follow or precede it, unless the
  application refuses that move (`Fixed`, `Tree.Refuse`). Over a visual
  selection `J`/`K`/`g J`/`g K` move every selected row as a block and one
  undo step and the selection stays. Consumers: matomeru's task outline
  (picks it up at the next bump).

## v0.124.1 (2026-11-11)

- Fixed a key or palette line asking for a subject the focus already supplies
  (a regression in v0.124.0). A key on a focused row whose noun names the
  verb's noun by an alias (a mounted `decision` row running `question.accept`
  through `decision.accept`) opened the args form for the verb's optional
  target; it acts on the row again. A thing on the focus path of the verb's
  noun, or of a noun that names that noun's actions by an alias, now stands
  for an optional subject of either noun, from keys and the palette alike
  (`subjectStands`); a naming noun the verb also takes as a parameter of its
  own stays that parameter's value. The ask remains for what nothing at the
  focus supplies.
- Fixed a path ask losing the path picker's source keys (a regression in
  v0.124.0, which made the ask's sources scopes): `ctrl+o` and `alt+o` step
  its sources again, the footer offers `ctrl+o` while typing, the title lists
  the sources with the current one in brackets, and the source last used is
  remembered. The ask is the path picker it was with the relation walk as one
  more source, last; `S` and `alt+s` step the same list (`PickerSpec.ScopeKeys`:
  the scope keys on an unscoped picker's sources). The file system sources
  keep their order, names and labels (v0.124.0 put typed entry last and
  labelled each `on disk · NAME`); an application's source named like the
  walk still reads `on disk · walk` and is remembered under its own name
  (`PickerSource.RememberAs`).
- Fixed a path ask's title naming one scope on the search line (`on disk ·
  parents`) where the path picker named it among its sources (`[parents]`), a
  regression in v0.124.0. With the sources that existed before v0.124.0 alone
  (`picker.scopes` or `subject.ask` off) the title, the sources and the keys
  are the path picker's own; with the walk offered the title differs by that
  one name, last (`… [parents] … walk`), and the search line carries no scope
  tag.
- Fixed `subject.ask` off not restoring what each route did before the ask (a
  regression in v0.124.0, which refused every noun, path, `Focus` and `From`
  parameter with the setting off). A palette line missing a required path
  refused ("missing file (path)") where v0.123.0 opened the path picker; a key
  missing a required noun refused where it opened the args form; a handler's
  `Ctx.Need` for such a parameter was refused where it asked. Off is now
  v0.123.0 exactly: a missing required argument opens the args form (the path
  picker for a lone path) from the palette and a key, `Ctx.Need` asks, the
  palette asks for the verb's own `Focus` target (`palette.reach`), and the
  subjects the ask added are left to the verb. `Runtime.mayAskParameter` is
  gone; `declaredSubject` reads the setting. The test that asserted the
  refusals (`TestSubjectAskOffRefusesPaletteKeyAndNeed`) is replaced by the
  v0.123.0 matrix, `TestSubjectAskOffIsTheBehaviourBeforeTheAsk`.
- Fixed `thing walk` failing with `unknown argument "prop"` on `enter` over a
  path or link value of a noun that declares its own `open` verb (v0.124.0).
  The walk ran `NOUN.open --prop PROP` whatever that verb was; it now runs it
  only where it is the free verb (`Operation.Standard`) and otherwise opens
  the value through `opener.run`, leaving the noun's own `open` and its
  arguments alone.
- Fixed a stale preview or side column replacing the destination's after a
  picker moved to another source or place (v0.124.0). Cycling the source, a
  source claiming the query and going into a row reset the preview and side
  state, generation included, so the next load reused the old generation and
  a result still in flight for the place it left was accepted. The
  generations now only move on (`Chooser.dropColumns`), and a navigation
  drops the side column as well as the preview.
- Fixed the relation walk hinting and answering the path picker's folder keys
  (`~` home, `g /` root, `n` new folder and their `alt+` rows), which mean
  nothing over things (v0.124.0). `picker.goto` and `picker.new` are now
  available only where the current source can do them: guards `picker-goes`
  (the source has a `Goto` and, when it declares `PickerSource.Places`, the
  place asked for is one of them) and `picker-makes` (it has a `Make`). The
  walk declares its two places, the first column and the deepest one visited
  (`0`, `$`); every path source keeps every key.
- Fixed a key answering for a verb that is unavailable at the focus because
  the verb could ask for a subject or a path (a regression in v0.124.0). A key
  that repeats, one row per place it means something, ran the first row's verb
  everywhere and asked for its path, where v0.123.0 passed the key on to the
  next row or level. Availability is decided first and by the verb alone
  again (`rowAvailable`; `canAskSubject` is gone): only an available verb
  asks, and the footer, the help and `binding list` offer a row exactly where
  its key answers. The palette still reaches a guarded verb and asks for its
  subject (`palette.reach`), as before.
- Fixed a key pressed on a focused thing asking for its verb's subject (a
  regression in v0.124.0). An application's own verb under a core row's name
  on a stored noun adopts the key subject (`adoptFieldParam`), so a key bound
  on the thing under the cursor (a table cell's `r` running `value.edit`)
  opened the args form for the value's id instead of acting there. A row that
  answers at a thing's level is that thing's key (`level.holds`): its request
  acts for the thing, no form opens for a subject and none marks one
  required, as before v0.124.0. A missing required argument and a handler's
  `Ctx.Need` still ask. Only a key of no thing (an `app` row) and the help's
  enter are asked for the subject the focus does not supply.
- Fixed `esc` in a path ask with `modal.esc-closes` on (a regression in
  v0.124.0). The ask was given a normal mode of its own, which released the
  search line's pin, so `esc` only left typing where the path picker closes
  in one press. The ask has no normal mode of its own again: `esc` does what
  it does in the path picker under either value of the setting (off, the
  default: the first `esc` leaves typing and `q` closes, as before), and the
  walk enters normal mode by itself.

## v0.124.0 (2026-11-11)

- Adopted Oden v0.5.1 in the framework, story runner and examples. Explicit
  namespace aliases take precedence over discovered executables, so mounted
  extensions keep ownership of their configured namespaces.
- Fixed mounted custom creation verbs disappearing when a host disables their
  choice source but still accepts free values. Inactive dependency metadata
  no longer invalidates the verb; mounting also preserves its `Creates` mark.
- Named text registers a-z now persist in private, atomically replaced files
  under the app state directory. Host callbacks and standalone CLI writes
  retain output across processes, and live reads refresh external writes.
  `register.persist` defaults to true; false stops disk access without deleting
  existing files. Stored text is unencrypted and has no automatic expiry.
  Special registers, macro recordings and yank history remain memory-only.
- Setup verbs type their `home` override as a directory and setup items type
  `path` as a file. Catalog consumers get shared path choices and the setup
  noun gets the standard path verbs without application-specific overrides.
- Fixed the standard history verb requiring a writable store. Readable facts
  now expose their recorded per-thing action history without gaining mutators.
- Fixed mounted open/reveal/open-with handoffs being killed when their
  extension exited successfully. Intentional OS opener processes now have a
  separate Unix process group; captured worker cleanup remains unchanged.
- Missing declared subjects now ask where from consistently from palette commands,
  bound keys and `Ctx.Need`. Noun asks keep the argument form first; choosing its
  missing field offers nearby candidates, the noun's list and the relation walk.
  Ordinary record-choice editing and populated fields retain their sources.
  Path asks keep disk sources and their explicit start hints, adding the graph
  walk before typed. Initial nested path pickers retain their field ownership.
  `subject.ask` defaults to true; false restores automatic refusal. Hints, help
  and binding listings include ask-capable keys. Optional creation/copy inputs,
  plain optional references and focus-local component actions stay unchanged;
  subject checks respect normalized prop aliases and object arguments.
  Archived/all scope teasers retain their counts beside the walk without doing
  source IO during rendering.
- The relation walk uses the existing columns picker to browse nouns, things,
  values and related things, including mounted nouns. `walk.columns` defaults to
  3; `picker.scopes=false` leaves the walk out of argument pickers.
- Catalog verbs can declare typed reciprocal actions on another host noun,
  forwarding both directions to the original operation and its checks.
- Mounted pages can open a requested record, including records outside the
  current list scope, and fetch only the selected record's missing detail data.
- Resolve accepted store selectors to the returned record's durable key.
  Hosts can migrate legacy typed references and relation ends when a mutable
  human selector is resolved; generated timestamps now publish the time type.
- Namecheck distinguishes explicitly typed local string value domains from
  dispatch-name strings; primitive string constants and aliases remain checked.
- Publish explicit CRUD store capabilities so an overloaded custom record-read
  action can supply mounted show, forms and path actions without losing its
  original CLI parameters.
- Persist durable history subject identities separately from private record
  values, so a moved record's history still resolves after a restart without
  restoring redacted before/after data.
- Resolve reciprocal receivers from their addressed rows, migrate surviving
  detached relation endpoints, and preserve invocation sequence order across
  deferred history completion and bounded retention.
- Keep explicitly typed dispatch keys visible to namecheck while preserving
  separate local UI-value domains.
- Honor covering input parameters when the palette and args form ask for an
  implicit subject, matching the operation's existing required-argument rule.
- Publish collection scope for generated operations that take no existing
  subject, so mounted lists do not inherit a selected record target.
- Keep native index opens in the index's existing collection, without a
  separate row cache or repeated-open duplicates.
- Fixed a palette regression from v0.123.0: `edit` and `open` are verbs again,
  not bare aliases of the Open line. With a thing in focus, `:edit --field
  @label` opens that thing's one-field form (v0.123.0 opened the Open line
  prefilled with `--field @label`), and bare `:edit` and `:open` run its own
  verbs as in v0.122. Removed the `edit` and `open` alias rows; `:e`,
  `:e PATH` and the `g o` key still open the Open line.
- Fixed derived index columns gaining a `created` column once the generated
  timestamps became time-typed: of the stamps only `updated` is drawn, last.

## v0.123.0 (2026-11-10)

- Added normal-mode keys `g o` for the Open line and `g .` for focused
  actions. Record nouns with free path verbs get hidden `g F` open and
  `g X` open-with rows, which yield to declared bindings. Existing opt-out
  settings remain in force.
- Added palette-only `e`, `edit` and `open` aliases for the Open line.
  Explicit nouns retain their own edit/open verbs.
- URL parameters now accept text in the palette, argument forms and user
  actions.
- Expanded focused actions to every level of the key walk: the thing and
  its value verbs, component, page and app, including operations bound
  from other namespaces. Filterable origin groups show full action names,
  keys and unmet needs instead of hiding unavailable verbs. Selection
  runs from the original focus, including inside viewers and modals.
- Kept the new `g o` and `g .` shortcuts inside the editor's pending `g`
  mode for text inputs, preserving counted `g g` and yank-history `g y`.

## v0.122.1 (2026-11-10)

- Replaced 1,062 individual story files and 182 full-screen goldens with twelve
  area composites. They retain the KEEP behavior from the 2026-11-09 value
  report, plus newer scaffold, fill-in, grouping, path picker and wrap
  regressions. Three KEEP cases that require persistent state across a true
  restart remain ordinary tests. Incompatible starting fixtures are isolated
  checkpoints advanced through the existing host reset.
- Composite checkpoint controls also work on pages created during a walkthrough,
  including generated noun indexes and Settings, rather than only the fixture's
  initial pages.
- Prefer state and targeted frame assertions over full-screen snapshots.
  Contributor rule: extend the area's composite or write an ordinary test;
  add a new story file only when the owner asks.
  Help-pick checks assert the chosen noun/mode, fallback operations and selected
  key instead of description wrapping or a highlighted row's physical height.
- `make stories` runs only the composite tests, plain and uncached. Binding
  validation and typing-shadow checks now observe that replay instead of
  replaying the suite again. Removed the generated minimum-verbs README table
  and its inventory-only test.
- Removed unused demo-only fake segments, peer-loading fields and verbs,
  mode-tone setup and the legacy pending-mode shorthand. The sandbox,
  recording and snapshot trimming remain available.

## v0.122.0 (2026-11-10)

- Added `tree.wrap`, a boolean defaulting to `true`: tree and outline rows
  soft-wrap with space-only continuation indents measured from nesting,
  fold markers, and cell 0's prefix before the editable label. Trailing
  cells and suffix badges stay on the first line. The inline editor uses
  the same wrapping, keeps its cursor and selections on the wrapped text,
  and preserves the row's display prefix and badges. `false` restores
  single-line clipping and sideways editing.
- Wrapped outlines count display lines for viewport scrolling and page
  motions, reveal tall rows by the half-page rule before leaving them, and
  cache row geometry for large outlines. `Tree.HeightAt(width)` counts
  wrapped lines; `Tree.Height()` retains logical-row sizing.
  Stories: `tree/wrap-40`, `tree/wrap-80`, `tree/wrap-edit-40`,
  `tree/wrap-off-40`.

||||||| bc933566

- Fixed: generated create props with a source, noun or enum complete their values instead of prop names on the CLI. The runtime and palette share the pair/value completion rule, preserving sibling source arguments, empty source answers, and prop=value completion for create, set and edit.

## v0.121.0 (2026-11-10)

- Fixed: empty generated indexes and other empty lists keep their declared or remembered row noun on the effective key walk without inventing a row or subject. Generated `n` now opens the existing creation form or creates immediately as the noun declares; subject-required verbs still ask or refuse normally. Binding listings, help and hints share the fix, collection motions and populated-row precedence remain unchanged, and `keys.noun-defaults` remains the opt-out. Story: `nouns/index-empty-new`.
- Fixed: a verb's subject parameter can use `From: "row:<field>"` to derive its subject before the palette asks which thing. This also works for an application's override of standard edit, args forms and catalog-mounted verbs. Explicit subject arguments and subject targets win; missing fields still ask, and targetless CLI/API/MCP calls never read UI focus. Existing `Parameter.From` is the declaration; no new guard or handler bypass is needed.
- Fixed (windowed grouping): `From` lists push the effective `GroupBy` and `GroupSort` through bounded `Store.Query`, `DocQuery` and mounted-store list requests, retain source group order and labels, and show counts of matched rows on the current page. `collection.group-fold` folds a group to one navigable collection heading without reading more rows; folds survive continued groups and `j`/`k` cross folded page boundaries, including remote completion. No default fold binding or new guard was added; empty `collection.group-by` remains the opt-out. Story `lists/windowed-group-folds`.
- Fixed (grouping verification): folded table headings span the row width instead of being truncated to the first column, including all-folded pages. Folded headings and ordinary table rows use one row-layout helper. Remote window and noun-count reads keep their original token input across stepped replays, so token/list/archive awaits retain their sequence. Local sorting stays inside the existing arrange stage, below the windowed early return. Story `lists/windowed-table-fold-width`.
- Changed (behavioral tests): removed the root `TestOperationTraitsKeepClassification` static operation-trait inventory, its unused `opTraitLines` helper and `testdata/op_traits.txt` pin instead of repinning it for a new verb. Existing behavior regressions remain. Classify `group-fold` as an action in the declarative lexicon vocabulary.

- Fixed: standard `create` accepts a `props` object alongside named props and `prop=value` words, with shared alias, role, type, duplicate, and required-prop handling on API, CLI, and palette routes. A noun's prop or alias owns its name; colliding create control parameters gain `create-` prefixes. A relation-like `from` prop now accepts `from=project:x`, while `create-from` still copies an existing record. Standard duplicate uses the same escaped copy parameter. Quoted palette arguments accept escaped quotes and backslashes, and declared flags remain available before a rest argument starts. Stories: `verbs/create-props-collision`.


- Added `ShowFormSpec.Related`, an optional `*RelatedSpec` for a hand-built page's complete related stack. The show form copies it and replaces its target with the selected thing; app settings, hooks, custom section views and history remain on the existing related mechanism. Omitted specs retain the native stack, and `NoSections` still leaves all sections out.
- Fixed show forms with both `List` and `Target` so the explicit target selects the same thing in the form and its sections. Explicit `Data` controls reloads when supplied.
- Fixed related history ordering so history stays last even when an order setting names it first or referrers are registered later.
- Fixed related sections rebuilding after an enabled toggle or target-noun change so externally placed views return to their original owner before a section is removed. Their rows survive instead of being recursively destroyed with the section; focus settles to visible content and the view can be reached again when shown.
- Fixed `AddShowForm` classifying its wrapper as the existing framework detail noun instead of registering every placement ID as an application noun. Explicit `Kind`, `Parent` and `FocusOnClose` options remain supported.

## v0.120.0 (2026-11-10)
- Changed (integration of noun scaffold E with fill-in): the generated `a` (relate/associate) with no other end and `annotate` with blank text answer `Ctx.Need` (`to`, `text`), so a key or the palette asks on that field (the noun's picker, every scope) instead of refusing; the CLI and agents still read "to is required: ..." / "text is required: ...". Tests: generated record keys never answer inside the path pickers or the palette (`integrate_keys_test.go`); `show` at the UI asks for the id, then opens the index with the opener's tab still shown.
- Fixed (tab row): while a page out of the tabs is open (a noun's generated index page `nouns/NOUN`), the tab row marks the tab it was reached from (the tab visited last: the Nouns page, or the page `show` ran on), not the first tab. Story `nouns/index-tab-marker`.
- Fixed (noun kinds): `ochazuke.markdown-view`, `ochazuke.message`, `ochazuke.terminal` and `ochazuke.timeline-body` declare `Kind(NounKindPart)`: they are drawn parts, so an app's Nouns page no longer files them under commands (checked over every framework noun: none derives as a command now).
- Added (noun scaffold E, mounted nouns): the standard `token` verb on every noun whose store keeps a change token (the store capability `changeToken` in the catalog): it answers `Store.ChangeToken`. A mounted noun whose catalog has it gets a forwarding `ChangeToken` on its proxy store, so the host's index and the Nouns page's counts read a mounted noun again only when its token moved: a remote store answers its token inside the read's own job (`jobToken`), and an unmoved token reads nothing more. A catalog without the verb (an older extension) mounts as before, with no token.
- Fixed (Nouns page counts): a noun whose store narrows an omitted list argument to a default (mtm's action-item `state=open`) was counted by its default list only, so its archived count read 0. Counts now ask `ListQuery.Every` (new): a store with default list arguments skips them when it is set (filters still apply), and counts the archived things through an explicit archive filter. A store declares nothing; one that applies defaults reads `q.Every`.
- Added (scaffoldcheck, D'): checks `verbs` (show, yank, history; duplicate where it creates; restore where it archives; associate where relations are kept), `keys` (the generated key block has a row for each verb it has, unless the noun declines it) and `show` (the history section reads, the relation sections relate). Changed: `delete` passes when an agent can run it only with the owner's confirmation (`confirm` in `agents.rights`, as pane and session close); a plain `allow` still fails. The check reads `Runtime.AgentRight` (owner rows first).
- Added (noun scaffold sc-show-form): `ShowForm` (`Runtime.AddShowForm(id, ShowFormSpec{List | Target, Data, Form, NoSections})`, `ShowFormOf`, `Sync`, `Block(text, width, height)`, `Form`, `Related`, `Holds`): the selected thing's edit form with the relation sections (one per referrer and relation kind: the v0.116 Related stack) and its history under it, following a list's selection or a target. A generated index page's detail is one; a hand-built page opts in and draws it under its own text (`form-under-text`; o-story `detail.showForm`). `RelatedSpec.History` adds the history section last (`Referrer.History`), read again when the history grows; `Runtime.ThingHistory(noun, key)`. Setting `detail.show-form` (default on) turns it off.
- Added (sc-show-form, catalog): detail kind `record-form` (`cli.DetailRecordForm`): a tool or mounted extension asks for the show form by data (`"detail": {"kind": "record-form"}`); section kind `history` beside `related` for a record detail.
- Fixed (sc-show-form): the show form read the selected thing five times per selection change (a target probe, the get, the form's own watch baseline and the watch's first event's reload); it reads it once: the caller's key is known (`knownTarget`), the form keeps no watch of its own and reloads once when its list reads the row again with other data; show forms sync again after the watches refresh, so a selection that moved (an archived row hidden) gets its form.
- Fixed (o-story demo): a story store's change token moves on create and delete too.
- Added (noun scaffold sc-standard-verbs): every stored noun gets `show` (the props; at the UI its index opens with the thing selected and the focus in its form), `restore` (the archive role set false; back from the R1 purge), `duplicate` with alias `dup` (a live copy through create's copy, props given replace), thing `yank` (`as`: label, key, ref, json), `history` (the thing's history entries, rows of `ochazuke.history-entry`) and `annotate` (a new thing of a noun with an about prop, `RoleAbout`, naming it), rows of the legacy verb table, each where its needs are met; `relate` gains the alias `associate` on every noun. The scaffold rows yield to a prop of their name (`standardVerb.YieldsToProp`): such a name is no longer reserved from props. A free verb the table generated yields to an alias the program declares under its name (`page.show`). `restore` and `duplicate` left `removed_verbs.json`.
- Added (sc-standard-verbs, generated keys): `bindings/generated/noun-defaults.json`, the default key block every record noun gets in normal mode for the verbs it has: `n` create (new), `r` rename (`edit {field: @label}` where a label prop is writable), `v` and `enter` show, `d a` archive, `d r` restore, `d d` delete `{archive-first: true}`, `u` undo, `a` associate, `g d` duplicate, `y y` yank, `y p` yank-path, `R` reveal, `z A` the list's archived rows (where the noun archives). Rows are `Binding.Generated` (never in a file; `bindings defaults` names the source) and step aside wherever a declared row on the walk binds their keys or a prefix: a user or application row whatever its guard, a framework file's row where it answers now. Setting `keys.noun-defaults` (default on) turns the block off; `NounBuilder.DefaultKeys(false)` (`Noun.NoDefaultKeys`, catalog `noDefaultKeys`) declines it for one noun (the outline, the Settings rows). Off the footer (hidden); `?` help lists them.
- Added (sc-standard-verbs, archive-first delete): `delete` takes `archive-first` (default the `delete.archive` setting): a live thing is archived with no question, an archived one is deleted after one question that names what goes with it (`Runtime.DeleteGoesWith`: things of each referring noun that name it, relations kept as unassociated; the `ochazuke.confirm-delete` prompt).
- Changed: an index page keeps `z a` as a hidden extra row beside the `z A` hint (the related section's `z a` fold is inner and keeps its meaning).

## v0.119.0
- Added (fill-in-and-scopes, picker scopes, `picker.scopes`): a picker for a parameter is never a wall around its values. `Scope{Name, Source, Fixed, Needs}` is data on `Parameter.Scopes` and `PickerDef.Scopes` (narrowest first; `Fixed` sets the source's arguments and `"{name}"` reads a sibling; `Needs` leaves a scope out while that sibling is unset); a noun that archives gets `all` then `archived` (the list asked to include them) with no declaration. The picker opens on the first scope (never the one last used) and `S` in the picker's normal mode, `alt+s` anywhere (`ochazuke.picker.next-scope` and `previous-scope`, guard `picker-scopes`) steps through them; the search line's tag names the scope and what the widest holds beyond it (`project matomeru · 212 more in all`); a picker with one scope shows no tag. New: `ChooserSpec.Scoped`, `ChooserSource.Count`. Off with `picker.scopes` (default on). Stories `scopes/*`.
- Added (fill-in-and-scopes, the safety net): a verb that finds it lacks a value asks for it, wherever a person ran it. New error `MissingParameterError{Param, Reason}` (the missing required argument of every route, `Ctx.Need(param, reason)` for a handler that finds it late, and the standard verbs' "no thing is focused") opens the args form on that field with the values given, the field required and the reason shown; confirming runs the same request again, esc runs nothing, several needs are asked in turn (`askNeed`: keys, the footer, the help's enter, the palette and a job's late failure all go through it). A standard verb guarded off because no thing of its noun is on screen runs headless when a person names its thing (`runsAway`, with `palette.reach`), and asks for the thing when it does not; a program's request is still refused. The refusal is `NotApplicableError{Key, Held}` (same text, the false guards in `Held`). The CLI and MCP still answer with the plain error. Off with `palette.missing-opens-form`. Stories `need/*`.
- Added (palette-which-key-board, palette.style): a second palette style, the which-key board (`palette.style` `board`; default `classic`, switchable live). The first two words are chosen on a board of cells, nouns with their verb counts then verbs with their noun counts; letters narrow by prefix, the shortest unique prefix of each cell is bold and underlined (`palette.prefix`), the one cell left commits itself (a typed word that continues the committed one takes it back), a word that is a noun and a verb offers both readings, a noun is followed by only its verbs and a verb by only its nouns, and a help line describes the current cell. New verbs `ochazuke.palette.cell {to: left|right|up|down|first|last|first-col|last-col}`, `ochazuke.palette.step {direction}` (up and down: the board's cells; classic: the completions while the line has some, else the history) and `ochazuke.palette.back`; rows in `bindings/palette.json` (arrows, home, end, alt+left, alt+right, backspace, esc goes up a layer). The board's line stays in insert, so esc is `palette.cancel` rather than leaving insert. Compared with Vim: `:help wildmenu` (README, Command palette styles).
- Changed (palette focus line, `palette.marker`): the focused completion is a full-width line in the new `palette.focus` tone (reverse bold where the theme gives none) instead of a `>` marker, in both styles; setting `palette.marker` (`line`, the default, or `arrow`) brings the arrow back. `up` and `down` are now `ochazuke.palette.step` (the completions while the line has some, else the history as before), `shift+tab` is `palette.move -1`, and `ctrl+n` and `ctrl+p` are hidden rows (still bound).
- Changed (shared matcher, prefix first): `fuzzyScore` (choosers, the path picker, list filters) adds a tier for a query spelled at the start of the text or of a word of it, wider than any scattered score, so `ta` ranks `tag` and `task` above `attach` and `con` ranks `context-item` above anything that only holds c, o, n in order. Fuzzy hits remain, below. The palette's own completions were already prefix-only; the board never lists a fuzzy hit.
- Added (palette.reach, default on): the palette offers every registered action from wherever it opens: a verb whose guard is false at the focus is no longer left out of the list or the line's reading (`offered`); a bare alias that shadows a verb (`set`) still reads the nouns that have the verb here (`offeredHere`), so `set NAME` reads as before. Choosing such a verb runs it at a live thing of its noun (`Palette.reach` sets the request's target to the first live instance, by id, where the action is available, or the instance a target parameter names). A verb disabled, behind a setting that is off, or whose store cannot do it is listed with its reason (`PaletteEntry.Reason`). A prop a guard hides is offered too. Off restores the old lists.
- Added (missing target is a parameter): a verb whose subject parameter (`Parameter.Focus` naming the verb's own noun) nothing focused fills is no longer refused with "key is required: no X is focused": the line is missing that parameter, and enter opens the args form on it, as a required one, with the noun's filterable picker; the pick runs the request. `Runtime.targetParams`, `missingTargets`, `Palette.missingTarget`, `askedTargets`. Keys and the help's enter are not changed (see the lane report). Setting `palette.reach` off restores the refusal.
- Changed (settings): `palette.style`, `palette.marker`, `palette.reach`. Binding guards `setting:NAME=VALUE` hold while a choice setting has that value (`setting:palette.style=board`). Chrome tones `palette.focus` and `palette.prefix`.
- Changed (tests): palette fixtures that assert guard-hidden verbs run with `palette.reach` off (`reachOff`); `fixed-root-refuses` sets it off; the four param goldens lose the hidden `up` hint and the settings goldens count three more settings.
- Added (path-pickers-hybrid-columns, styles): the setting `pickers.directory.style` (`list`, `hybrid`, `columns`; default `list`, the picker as it was) decides how every path picker is drawn, read at each open and switchable live from the Settings page; no caller branches on it. `hybrid` is one list in sections (Recent, one for each application source that declares `PickerSource.Section`, Browse), `]` `[` between them (`picker.move {sections}`), headings never take the highlight (`PickerRow.Header`/`Section`); `columns` is parent | folder | preview (`PickerSpec.Columns`, `PickerSource.Side`, `Chooser.Side`). Both open in normal mode (`PickerSpec.StartNormal`) and take the browse keys of `bindings/picker-browse.json`; `pickers.directory.section-limit` (5) caps a section while the search line is empty. See `docs/PATH-PICKER.md`.
- Added (path-pickers-hybrid-columns, never confined): every path source goes up and into folders (`browsable`: `PickerSource.UpFrom`, `CanOpen`, `Goto`, `Landing`), so going up from any row of recent, walk, find, typed or an application's source reaches `/`, and a typed or pasted path always works. Changed: going up from a folder opened out of the recent source (or any source) no longer returns to its list at the first step; it goes to the folder above, and `picker.goto {to: own}` (`alt+down`) returns to the source's own rows. The typed source goes up from `~` and from an empty query.
- Added (path-pickers-hybrid-columns, verbs): `picker.goto {to: ~|/|first-col|last-col|own}`, `picker.mark {all}`, `picker.new` (name and make a folder: `PickerSource.Make`, `MakeLabel`), `picker.toggle {name: hidden}` (`PickerSpec.Toggles`), `picker.move {sections}`, and on the highlighted path `picker.open-with {with}` (the opener table when `with` is empty; an agent confirms), `picker.reveal`, `picker.yank-path`. `picker.pick {current}` takes the highlighted row when a source has no current row (the file under the highlight in a file pick). `PathChooserSpec.Many`/`ChooseMany` and `PickerSpec.Many`/`ChooseMany` mark several rows (adding to a list field's paths marks several folders at once); keys `alt+` for each in the `list` style.
- Added (path-pickers-hybrid-columns, frecency): `Runtime.SetRecentUses(func(PathPick) []PathUse)` gives the recent source and the hybrid Recent section each place's use count and last use; places rank by `PathBoost` (tuios's applist brackets, capped at 32). `Runtime.PathAutoListable(dir)` exports the `path.no-auto-list` test for an application's own scans; `Chooser.Settled()` says nothing is loading, for a test to wait on.
- Fixed (path-pickers-hybrid-columns): the recent source no longer stats its paths on the loop in a file pick (the stat is work off the loop and skips `path.no-auto-list`); a folder pick's recent rows read no file system.
- Added (noun scaffold sc-doc-store): `DocStore(table DocTable, noun, hooks...)` is a noun's whole store over a table of JSON documents, built from the noun's props at run time: get, list, create (a given key, or the noun's first letter and the next number), set, delete, archive and undo, `Query` run in the table (`DocQuery`: conditions, `Hide` for the archive hiding, sort, `Text` over `TextFields` (the key, the label and writable plain text props), the page) and the table's `Version` as `ChangeToken`. `DocHooks{BeforeDelete, AfterWrite, ConfirmFor}` carry a store's side effects. `MemDocTable` is the reference table (`Reads`, `Queries` count what it answered); `TextMatches` is its text search. Package `doctest` is the contract (`doctest.Run(t, newTable)`): an application runs it against its own table layer.
- Added (text search): the standard list takes `search` (every word in the key, label or a text prop, case-insensitive): a store's `Query` gets it as `ListQuery.Text` (a windowed index's filter text now searches the whole store), a store without one is searched in memory.
- Added (mounted paging): a mounted noun whose catalog says `store.query` gets a proxy `Query`: the extension's list is asked for one page with `where` (equality conditions), `sort`, `search`, `page`, `per-page` and `archived`; a condition those words cannot say runs over the whole list here instead. Not forwarded: `ChangeToken` (no extension verb answers a token yet).
- Added (free verbs from value kinds): prop type `url` (`PropURL`, a link: stored as text); a noun with a path or url prop gets `open` and `yank-path` for it, a path prop also `reveal` and `open-with` (as before), from the `openableTypes` table by the prop's type, never by noun. A noun with a single-thing reference prop (`Parameter.Noun`, or the any-noun `*`) gets `follow {prop}`: opens the referenced thing's index with its row selected. Agent rights unchanged: open and open-with confirm, reveal, yank-path and follow allow. Time props have no free verb (no common action beyond the edit field).
- Added (windowed lists, pass 1 carry-overs): `j` on the last row of a windowed list's page reads the next page and selects its first row, `k` on the first row the previous page's last row (`windowEdge`); only at the store's ends does the move spill to the next stop. On index pages `]`/`[` run `collection.page-next`/`page-prev`. The Nouns page recounts after a change while it is open (stores whose change token did not move are not read).
- Added (noun scaffold sc-rights-and-conformance): package `scaffoldcheck` checks every record noun of a registry (an application's, with its extensions mounted) is served by the generic scaffold from its declaration: index (List or Query and a key), label, form (edit), new (create or a verb that `Creates`), set, archive (an agent may archive), restore (a writable archive prop), delete (never an agent's), the CLI words get and list, and its value kinds' free verbs. `Problems(Config)` lists the failures; `Check(t, Config{Runtime, Baseline})` holds them to a baseline that only shrinks (`SCAFFOLDCHECK_UPDATE=seed|1`). ochazuke's own framework record nouns are its first baseline (`scaffoldcheck/testdata/scaffoldcheck.txt`).
- Added (noun scaffold sc-show-form, first part): an index page's detail is the edit form over the selected thing (`EditSpec` placed under the page, made again when the selection names another thing, after the request, never while drawing): every prop, read-only ones muted, `i` edits a field, each change written as typed, `u` undoes; `}` moves into it from the list. Setting `detail.show-form` (default on; off draws the props as text). A keyed watch (one thing's, the form's) reads that thing with `Get`, never the whole store. Story `nouns/show-form`. Not yet: relation sections and the history section under the form, catalog detail `kind: record-form`, adopting it on hand-built pages.
- Fixed: a leftover merge marker in the v0.116.0 section of this file.

## v0.118.1
- Changed (CLI help, needs oden with `Command.Namespace`): a tool's default help (`BIN`, `-h`, `--help`, `help`) lists its own commands and nouns, the generic CLI commands and one line per namespace (the framework's `ochazuke` group with its noun count) instead of every framework noun, with brief CLI notes and no shadow lines. `BIN help --all` (also `-h --all`, `--help --all`) prints everything as before; `BIN ochazuke` lists the namespace's nouns, internal `__` ones left out. Machine output (`catalog`, `commands`, `state`, completion, MCP) is unchanged. `Mount` marks each top-level group with `Namespace`/`NamespaceHead`/`Hidden`; no setting needed (`--all` is the opt-out).

## v0.118.0
- Added (noun scaffold sc-catalog-data, C1 noun kinds): every noun has a kind, `Runtime.NounKind(name)` / `AllNounKinds()`: `record` (a collection store that is not facts), `singleton`, `facts` (`Store.Facts`), `instances`, `command` (no store, an application verb) or `part` (no store, only interaction verbs: drawn rows and widgets). A storeless noun may declare `part` or `command` (`NounBuilder.Kind`, `Noun.Kind`), and a stored noun `facts` where its store is not marked so (a mounted extension's facts over a proxy store). Constants `NounKind*`, `NounKindList`, `StoredKind`, `DeclaredKind`.
- Added (C1, C8 catalog): `CatalogNoun.NounKind` (`nounKind`), `Facts` (`facts`) and `Store` (`store: {query, changeToken, watch, undo, remote}`), and `CatalogVerb.Creates` (`creates`). Backward compatible: a catalog written by v0.110 to v0.117 has none of them; `CatalogNoun.KindOf()` derives the same kind from the rest of the entry (store kind, a standard verb unavailable as a fact, application verbs), a missing `store` reads as a store that lists whole (no paging, no token: today's behaviour), a missing `creates` as only the standard create creating. `MountNouns` declares the published (or derived) kind on the host noun where the host's own reading would differ (a storeless noun's proxies are all application verbs; a proxy store is not marked facts); nothing else about a mount changes.
- Added (C9): `Operation.Creates` and `NounBuilder.Creates()` mark a verb that makes a new thing of its noun (the standard create has it; `lane.define`, `question.ask` declare it).
- Added (C5, time props): prop and parameter type `time` (`PropTime`): stored as RFC 3339 in UTC, read from RFC 3339, `2026-11-10 09:30` (local) or `2026-11-10` (`ParseTime`). The CLI, palette, args form and edit form read it; text that does not read is kept in the field and marked, never written.
- Changed (C6, JSON field kind): `array` and `object` parameters are form fields now (multi-line JSON text, read as JSON of that shape; text that is not is kept as typed and marked), so a required object parameter no longer fails the args form; an `object` prop's edit field is several lines high. `TestParamKindsPinTodaysRoutes` pins the new form and editor routes. Actions.json parameters may be `time`.
- Added (noun scaffold sc-nouns-page, the `ochazuke.noun-browser` component): the Nouns page (`NounsPageID` `__nouns`, title Nouns) lists every noun, the application's and its mounted extensions' (from their catalog alone), grouped by kind in `nouns.kinds` order (default record, singleton, facts, instances, command: drawn parts are left out), each with its count, archived count and store (`local`/`remote`, `pages` for a `Query`, `token` for a `ChangeToken`); command nouns list their actions. Detail: the noun's kind, store, count, props and verbs. `Model.Init` registers it last (`EnsureNounsPage`), so it is the last tab. Verbs `ochazuke.noun-browser.open` and `.refresh` (key `r` on the page, `bindings/nouns.json`).
- Counts are read when the page opens and on refresh, never while a frame draws: a paged store answers two one-row pages (total, archived total), a remote store's read is a job off the loop, registry facts and live instances are counted where they are, and a store that can only list everything is not read (`–`) unless `nouns.count-lists` is on (default off). A store whose `ChangeToken` is unchanged is not read again. `Runtime.NounCount`; the `ochazuke.noun` facts gain `kind`, `store`, `count`, `archived`.
- Added (page tab settings): `Page.TabSetting` names the setting that puts a page in the tabs (a boolean on, or a list holding the page id); a page out of it stays out of the tabs and ctrl+j/k even while current, so opening and leaving it never changes the tab row. Setting `pages.pinned` (default `[]`) is that list for opt-in pages (a noun's generated index page). The Nouns page names `nouns.page` (default on; off takes it out of the tabs, `noun-browser.open`, the page picker and the palette still open it). `Runtime.QueryAll(noun, things, query)` runs a list request over things held in memory (a store's `Query` over its own rows).
- Settings added (Pages section): `nouns.page` (true), `nouns.kinds` (record, singleton, facts, instances, command), `nouns.count-lists` (false), `pages.pinned` ([]). o-story: page `nounsPage`, store `generate`, `paged`, `token`; story `nouns/page`.
- Added (noun scaffold sc-index, the `ochazuke.index` component): `Runtime.AddIndex(id, noun, opts...)` is a list From any stored noun in `Runtime.IndexColumns(noun)`: `index.columns.NOUN` (declared when an index is built, and at startup for every noun `settings.json` names) when it names props, else the noun's declared columns, else the label (else the key), then up to six short choosable props (enum, boolean, reference, number, time, duration), then `updated`; never a secret, computed, multi-line, list or object prop. Verb `ochazuke.index.columns {noun, props}` writes the setting. A catalog list `{"kind": "index"}` (`CatalogList.Kind`, `cli.ListKindIndex`) is that component over the host's mounted noun, filled from its proxy store; a catalog without the field reads every list's row source as before, and an unknown kind does too.
- Added (index pages): `noun.show {noun, key}` (`enter` on a Nouns row) opens `nouns/NOUN` (`IndexPageID`, `Runtime.OpenIndex`): the index and the selected thing's props beside it; every stored noun gets one, a hand-built page or not; a command or part noun answers what it is and its verbs. Index pages name `pages.pinned` as their `TabSetting`: never a tab unless listed there, so opening and leaving one (or a mounted extension's) leaves the tab row unchanged.
- Added (windowed From): a list From a noun whose store has `Query` holds one page of it (`collection.window`, default 200; 0 reads the whole store as before): the list's filters, sort, text and archive toggle go into the query, the store answers the page and the total ("page 2/100 of 20000" on the filter line), `collection.page-next`/`page-prev`/`page-first`/`page-last` read that page, a view change reads page 1 of the new query once the request has run (`readStaleWindows`, never inside a refilter), and a store whose `ChangeToken` and the query are unchanged is not read. Its watch reads nothing itself (`Watch.notify`): no snapshot of the store. `Collection.Windowed`, `WindowTotal`. A From list over a remote store reads in a job owned by the list in the slot `window-read` and draws its loading line meanwhile (`Placeholder.LoadingJob`), then the empty line: never the empty line while loading.
- Added (archive toggle): `collection.show-archived {show}` shows or hides a list's archived rows (`Collection.SetShowArchived`, `ShowsArchived`); hidden, the filter line says "N archived hidden" (counted by the store for a windowed list), `collection.archived-count` (default on) turns that off. Key `z a` on index pages (`ochazuke.index/normal`): bound on every list it took `z a` from related sections' fold and made `z` a prefix where lists bind `z` keys, so other lists reach the verb from the palette and `bindings.json`. Settings `collection.window` (200) and `collection.archived-count` (true). Stories `nouns/index-open`, `nouns/index-archived`, `nouns/index-sort-filter`, `nouns/index-big` (20,000 things: one page of 200 read, no list).

## v0.117.0
- Changed (cpu-follow, watch change token): `Store.ChangeToken func(Ctx) (string, error)` is an optional cheap token for a store (a version counter, sqlite `data_version`, a write generation); equal tokens mean nothing changed. A fallback watch keeps the token its last snapshot saw and, while the store's token is unchanged, skips the snapshot (no List, no record build, no JSON, no hashing), so a change-kind request on a 20,000-row store costs one token call instead of a full reload on the loop. `""` or an error means unknown and snapshots as before. A store without a token behaves as before: one snapshot per watch per refresh pass, and a request makes one pass. `Runtime.Changed` honours the token too. Consumers: matomeru's `contextStore` (and any store over a large table) sets `ChangeToken` to get the saving; nothing changes for a store that does not.
- Changed (cpu-follow, segment backoff): a status segment whose answer (text, tone and error) repeats backs off: the wait doubles on each refresh up to the row's `max-every` (new `Segment.MaxEvery`, JSON `max-every`, default `DefaultSegmentMaxEvery` 60s; at or below `every` means no backoff) and returns to `every` when the answer changes or `segment.refresh` runs. An exec segment that says "link down" now spawns about once a minute instead of every 2s. Setting `status.segment-backoff` (Status bar, default `true`; this changes how often segments update) turns it off so every segment refreshes at its `every`. The segment refresh doc comment states interval, cost, idle behaviour, backoff, pause and the settings. Not done: pausing while the terminal is unfocused (the runtime does not handle `tea.FocusMsg` or ask for focus reports); a segment hidden by `status.segments`/`status.segments.<id>` is already not refreshed. Tests `TestSegmentBacksOffWhileTheAnswerRepeats`, `TestSegmentMaxEveryInSegmentsJSON`.
- Added (cpu-follow, nothing changed): `Ctx.NothingChanged()` lets a change-kind verb say this request changed nothing. The runtime then leaves no history row and no undo record for it, does not count it as a change for the open surfaces and does not refresh the watches (a request that ran another change verb still refreshes). It applies to the request the handler runs in; a job a handler returns is judged when it completes, as before. A verb that does not call it behaves as before, so a writing verb need not be marked a fetch to avoid the refresh. Test `TestNothingChangedSkipsRefreshHistoryAndUndo`.

## v0.116.0
- Added (relations-lib E2, relation model): a relation is one row of a many to many with two any-noun ends (`Relation{Kind, From, To, Text, Data, Position, Archived, FormerFrom, FormerTo}`; ends are `noun:key`, `AnyRef`), kind any word (default `relation`), free text, a data object and a position. ochazuke models and enforces; it never persists: the application (or a tool, in its own file) gives the runtime a `RelationStore` (`Relations(RelationQuery)`, `Put`, `Remove`) with `Runtime.SetRelationStore`, `MemRelations` is the in-memory one for tests and o-story. Operations: `Runtime.Relate`, `Unrelate`, `SetRelated`, `RelationsOf`, `DetachRelations`. `RelationKind` rows (`Runtime.DeclareRelationKind`, catalog `relationKinds`, fact noun `ochazuke.relation-kind`) add rules to a kind: `from`/`to` nouns, `maxFrom`/`maxTo` with `onFull` `replace` (default) or `keep`, `ordered`, `exclusive` data flags (at most one relation per From thing holds a flag such as `default`: the first made takes it, setting it moves it, unrelating its holder promotes the next by position), and the `prop`/`inverse` words. An undeclared kind has no rules.
- Added (E2, relation-backed props): `PropSpec.Relation` backs a list prop (`Items.Noun`) or a string prop (`Noun`) with relations of that kind: the thing is the From end and the value is the To keys in position order. The noun's store is wrapped, so `get`, `set`, `create`, the edit form, undo and the related sections see the prop without a special path; the noun's own store never holds it. A runtime with no relation store reads an empty value and refuses a write.
- Added (E2, list item edits): `set PROP+=x` and `PROP-=x` (also `props: {"PROP+": x}`) add or remove one item, or a JSON array of items, of any list prop, not only relation-backed ones.
- Added (E2, verbs): `NOUN.relate` and `NOUN.unrelate` on every noun with a store once the runtime has a relation store (legacy rows of the standard table, absent otherwise). Both ends have them and share one handler: `to` makes this thing the From end, `from` the To end (any-noun `noun:key`), `kind`, and for relate `text` and `data`. Under `delete.archive` unrelate archives first and unrelating an archived relation removes it; relating again brings an archived one back with its text and data. Relate and unrelate are history entries that undo (the row put back, the side effects too); deleting a thing keeps its relations, the end moving to `FormerFrom`/`FormerTo`, and undoing the delete puts them back. `Runtime.Referrers` lists relation-backed props (`Referrer.Relation`, `Multi`) and the sections declared kinds add (`Outward`).
- Added (E3, related sections over relations): a stack for a thing shows a section per relation: the things that point at it through a relation-backed prop or a declared kind, and (declared kinds) the things it is related to. Rows come from the relation store and the other end's `Get` (a noun with no store gets rows made from the key). `associate` relates (a list prop appends, a kind's rules hold), `unassociate` unrelates the selected or marked rows in one undo step, `new` creates a thing with the relation-backed prop filled (not offered where no prop exists). Sections read again when relations change. Setting `relations.sections` (default on) turns every relation section off. Stories `relations/list-prop-form`, `related/relation-section`; o-story's store and related specs take `relations`, a prop `relation` and `plain` nouns.
## v0.114.0
- Added (notes-n12 N1, any-noun reference): `Parameter.Noun` `"*"` (`AnyNoun`) is a reference to a thing of any noun, stored as `noun:key` (`AnyRef`, `SplitAnyRef`: split at the first colon, so a key may hold colons and slashes, a path for one). A prop that names it is Free (the thing or its noun may not exist here; the value is kept as written). `Runtime.Referrers(noun)` lists such a prop for every noun, marked `Referrer.Any` (catalog `referencedBy` `any`; `Referrer.Value(noun, key)` is what the prop holds), so related sections show it, filter `PROP = noun:key` and `new`/`associate` fill `noun:key`; a section hook (`RelatedSpec.Rows`, `Scope`) is given that stored value. The chooser for it asks for the noun, then the thing: the noun rows (every noun with a picker) open with enter (`Opens`, `NoPick`), `noun:text` lists that noun's things as `noun:key`, up goes back; `ChoicesFor` answers the same for the palette and forms (`ChoicesTakeQuery` is true). A catalog mount keeps `"*"`. Setting `anyref.nouns` (`stored`, the default: the application's nouns with a store, leaving out the framework's own and facts stores; `pickers`: every noun with a picker) says which nouns its chooser offers. Story `related/any-noun-reference`.
- Added (notes-n12 N2, related stack as a detail): `RelatedSpec.Target func() (noun, key string)` names the thing the sections show, so one stack serves rows of any noun: the sections are the referrers of the noun and are made again when the noun changes with the selection (collapse state stays by id). `RelatedSpec.Enabled` names a boolean setting that turns the stack off (nothing read or drawn). `Runtime.SelectionTarget(list func() *Collection)` is the Target that follows a list's selected row (a noun with no key prop, a mounted catalog's rows, takes the row's Data), `Runtime.AddRelatedDetail(id, list, opts...)` is the stack of a list's selected row with the settings below, `Related.Noun()` and `Related.Text(width)` (natural height, to scroll inside a detail document). Catalog detail sections gain kind `related` (main region, no field): `cli.PageView` mounts the stack following the page's followed list. Settings `detail.related` (default `true`; off leaves the detail as it was) and `detail.related-hidden` (default `[]`; NOUN.PROP ids or a `PREFIX*`, `note.about` hides notes everywhere). The stack reads on a selection change, a store change, unfold and refresh, never on a draw; a first sync no longer fills twice. Stories `related/detail-follows-selection`, `related/detail-related-off`, `related/detail-related-hidden`; o-story's related demo takes `detail`, `rowNouns`, nouns' `any` and `refs`.

## v0.113.0
- Added (pp-library O2, edit spec): `Runtime.EditSpec(noun, id) (RecordSpec, error)` is the standard edit form of one thing as a spec: the writable props as fields, loaded with `get`, written with `set` (one history group, `forms.edit-apply` deciding change or confirm), watching the noun for outside writes. The `edit` verb opens exactly that spec as a modal (one builder, `editSession.formSpec`), so an application can place the same form in a panel with `AddRecordEditor` and the two cannot drift. `RecordSpec.OnOpen(*RecordEditor)` hands a spec built away from its editor the editor once it is built. A `get` still pending (a mounted noun's) leaves the placed form empty until it lands.
- Added (pp-library O2, read-only props): the standard edit form (the modal and a placed `EditSpec`) draws the thing's read-only props (`ReadOnly` or `CreateOnly` props stored with the thing; not the key, a computed prop or a secret) muted, in prop order, with no focus stop (`RecordField.ReadOnly`, instance noun `ochazuke.form-read-only`; `RecordEditor.Values` leaves them out). Setting `forms.show-read-only` (default `true`; off draws only what can be edited; read when the form opens). `edit {field}` is unchanged. Stories `forms/read-only-props`, `forms/read-only-props-off`.

- Added (pp-library O1, list field): `Parameter.Items` (`*Parameter`, catalog `items`) is the element parameter of a list. A list prop with Items is a list field in the standard edit form and any `RecordEditor`: one focus stop (instance noun `ochazuke.form-item`) drawn as the label, one row per item and an add row; the value is a JSON array written at once on each add, edit, delete or move (one undo step, no save key). Its verbs are the form's `form.item-edit` (path item: path picker; item with choices: its picker; else a line of text in a picker whose create row takes any typed text), `item-add`, `item-delete`, `item-move-up`, `item-move-down`, `item-yank`, `item-next`, `item-previous` (spill to the neighbouring field at the ends), `item-first` and `item-last`, all under guard `form-list`; picker kind `form-item-edit`. They sit on `ochazuke.form`, as the chips' verbs do, because a command noun may not take a core verb's name (`edit`, `delete`; nouncheck), so `ochazuke.form-item` has no verbs of its own, only the key context. A list has no column edges (`SpillEdges`: a component with no horizontal motion takes them as a no-op). Keys: `enter`/`i` edit, `n` add, `d d` delete, `j`/`k`, `g g`, `G` (`bindings/form.json`); move and yank are unbound (palette, CLI, `bindings.json`). A list prop without Items keeps its JSON text field. Helpers `ParseItems`, `ItemsValue`. Story `forms/list-field-paths`.
- Added (pp-library H1, host nouns): `Parameter.HostNoun` (catalog `hostNoun`) names, beside `noun`, the noun of the program that mounts the catalog, by its bare name (`project` in mtm). `cli.MountNouns` (through the new `cli.MountParameterOn(r, p, nouns)`) makes it the mounted prop's `Noun` when the host runtime has that noun, and drops it when it has none; a list's `items` are mounted the same way, and `cli.MountParameter` (no host to ask) drops the field. So an extension's owner prop (o-lane `project`, o-questions `project_id`, o-services `origin_project`) can declare the host's project without taking the extension's own noun of that name, and `Runtime.Referrers(project)` lists it in the host. A `hostNoun` that is not a noun's name, or on a prop that is not a reference (an integer), panics at `Props` like the other prop flags (`PropSpec.HostNounError`) and is ignored with a mount warning from a catalog; `type: path` props and list `items` are kept through the mount (tests). `BIN catalog inspect` shows `hostNoun` and `items` in the props table. Consumers: a host (mtm, nabe) gets it with the next tag, defining its own nouns before it mounts; a tool's catalog declares `hostNoun` and an older host ignores the field.

## v0.112.0
- Added (FA1): `Opener` (`opener.go`), the one injectable OS opener: `Open` (macOS `open --`, Linux `xdg-open`, Windows `rundll32`) and `Reveal` (macOS `open -R --`, Linux `xdg-open` of the folder, Windows `explorer /select,`), with `Start` as the runner a test fakes. `Runtime.Opener()` / `SetOpener`. The About page's `about-row.open`, `BIN catalog inspect --open` and `markdown.link-open` call it (the copies in `about.go`, `cli/catalog_inspect.go` and `markdown/links.go` are gone); `AboutEnv.Open` and `Doc.OpenURL` still override it. macOS now passes `--` before the target.
- Added (FA2): the opener table. `Runtime.ResolveOpen(text) (OpenTarget, OpenAction, error)` says what opening text would do without doing it, and `Runtime.Open(text)` does it. Rows are data: built-in rows in `opener_rows.json` (http/https/mailto/file, `~`, `/`, `./`, `../`, common image and document extensions, and `noun:id` of any registered noun running its `open` or `show` verb) and the user's setting `open.openers` (JSON array of `{scheme|prefix|ext|noun, action, to}`; actions `os-open`, `os-reveal`, `page`, `viewer`, `verb`), tried first at the same level. Specificity: a noun's own row, any noun, scheme, extension, prefix. Setting `open.unmatched` (`os` default, or `none`) says what unmatched text does: hand it to the OS opener, or nothing. Nothing is removed; no key and no modal yet (FA3).
- Added (FA3 open modal): verbs `ochazuke.opener.show` (a one-field modal that takes any text, line breaks too, shows `→ what open would do` under it, enter opens it; setting `open.modal`, default on), `ochazuke.opener.run {text}` (opens text now; agents confirm) and `ochazuke.opener.resolve {text}` (what open would do; read). No default key. To bind one, add a row to `bindings.json`: `{"context": "app/normal", "bindings": [{"key": "O", "action": "ochazuke.opener.show"}]}` (the context of the page you want it on). Story `open/resolve-and-run`; o-story's opener is a fake that logs `start CMD`.
- Added (FA4 path-prop verbs): prop type `PropPath` (`"path"`, `PropSpec{Parameter: Parameter{Type: "path"}}`; `Parameter.IsPath()`), stored as text. A noun with a store and a path prop gets four free verbs from rows of the standard verb table (`path_verbs.go`, no noun is named): `reveal` (shows the path in the file manager), `yank-path` (the path into the active register), `open` (through the opener table) and `open-with {with}` (macOS `open -a`); `prop` picks one when the noun has several path props (default the first), the subject is the key as for `get`. `open` and `open-with` need an agent's confirmation (new `standardVerb.Agent`); `reveal` and `yank-path` are allowed. A verb the application registers under the same name wins. Setting `paths.free-verbs` (default on); off, the verbs refuse to run. A prop can no longer be named `reveal`, `yank-path`, `open` or `open-with` on a noun with a store. `Opener.OpenWith`. The verb-check probe noun gained a path prop so bindings naming these verbs check.
- Added (FA5 focused actions): verb `ochazuke.focus.act` opens a picker (kind `focus-actions`, rows from `Runtime.FocusedActions(focus)` as `Choice`s through `ChoiceRows`) of the verbs the focused thing's noun has that are available there, motions and typing left out, by name, each with the key bound to it where the focus is and its description. Enter runs the verb on the focused thing through `RunUnbound` (a verb missing arguments opens its form; agent rights and confirms apply). No default key, so the leader fall-through has nothing to mirror; to bind one add `{"context": "app/normal", "bindings": [{"key": "ctrl+.", "action": "ochazuke.focus.act"}]}` to `bindings.json`. Setting `actions.focused-picker` (default on; off, the verb refuses). Story `open/focus-actions`.
- Changed: the open verbs live on noun `ochazuke.opener` (`opener.show`, `opener.run`, `opener.resolve`), because the lexicon allows a word one part of speech and `open` is an action.

## v0.111.0
- Added (mounted results): a key run presents a verb's answer by the kind its row declares, with no tool, noun or verb named. Text, Markdown and preformatted answers already opened their viewer (`keys.text-results`); an application verb declaring `Operation.Result` `ResultRows` (`"rows"`, `NounBuilder.RowsResult()`, a mounted catalog row's `result`) now opens a list-of-objects answer in the results table the palette uses, late answers guarded like text ones (`keys.table-results`, default `true`; `false` leaves key rows answers unpresented; a binding row's `present: false` still keeps one key out). Any other answer stays the host's status line. Consumers: `mtm`, `nabe` and every `cli` host pick it up with the next tag; a mounted verb already declaring `result: "rows"` starts opening its table. Stories `mounted/key-text-result`, `mounted/key-rows-result`, `mounted/key-rows-result-off`; `cli.RunMounted` doc names the presentation.

## v0.110.2
- Changed: requires the next oden (PATH discovery is memoized per process and cached on disk, `ODEN_DISCOVER_CACHE=off` turns the disk part off). Every `cli.Mount` of an in-process group used to rescan every PATH directory (about 160 scans per `mtm` call, ~0.5-1 s on a long PATH); an app that passes its own `oden.WithFS` gets the cache by giving that FS a `DiscoverCacheKey() string` method (`oden.CachedFS`). No ochazuke code change.
## v0.110.1
- Added: tone `input.field` (`ToneInputField`) fills a text input's field, its whole width and not its label, a step off the surface around it (dark `#262C34`, light `#EEF1F5`). One place draws it (`InputBlock`), so record and args-form fields, the palette line, picker search lines, the find line, the setting value prompt and the filter save form all get it; the selection and cursor draw on top. mono and NO_COLOR draw inputs flat. To turn it off or recolour it, give the tone in `themes.json`: `"tones": {"input.field": {}}` (no bg) draws inputs flat, `{"bg": {"light": "#..", "dark": "#.."}}` recolours. No setting.
## v0.110.0
- Added (pp-r2 related sections): `AddRelated(id, RelatedSpec)` draws a stack of sections for one thing (a project): one section per `Referrers(noun)` entry, each a list of the referring noun's things whose prop holds the key, under a heading row (`▾ Tasks (3)`, a focus stop). Rows come from the noun's store (standard list filtered `PROP = key`, refreshed by its watch) or from `RelatedSpec.Rows` for a noun without one; `NounBuilder.RelatedView(id)` lets a noun keep a placed view (a tree) in its section, narrowed by `RelatedSpec.Scope`. The stack scrolls, keeps the focused row on screen and shows the heading of the section it is inside as a breadcrumb. `j`/`k` spill from section to section; `}`/`{` walk them as stops. Noun `ochazuke.related-section` (key `NOUN.PROP`; `list`, `get`) with verbs `toggle`, `open`, `close`, `open-all`, `close-all`, `next`, `previous`, `first`, `last`, `refresh`, `new`, `associate`, `unassociate`, `hide`, `show`, `move-up`, `move-down`; `new`, `associate` and `unassociate` run the referring noun's own create/set (rights, history and undo are the noun's; `d u` on marked rows is one undo step; a read-only referring prop refuses with the reason). Default rows: `z a/o/c/R/M`, `] ]`, `[ [`, and on a heading `enter`, `n`, `a`; `d u` on a thing. The application names its settings in the spec (`Order`, `Hidden` with `NOUN.PROP` or `PREFIX*` entries, `Collapsed`, `Counts`, `Empty` = `header` (default), `hide` or `open`); a hook's rows are read when the key changes, a section unfolds, `refresh` runs or the optional `Interval` setting (a duration, default off) passes, never on a draw; with none named the built-ins apply (counts on, empty sections as a heading). Stories `related/sections-basic`, `related/collapse-and-motions`, `related/associate-unassociate-undo`.
- Changed (PU2, pickers on one data mechanism): the library's hand-built picker rows are now sources returning `[]Choice`, turned into rows by `ChoiceRows` (each `Choice.Row`): the help place and view choosers, list sort, prop filter (props, values, remove, saved), the compact filter form's sort, field and value pickers, the saved-filter delete picker, the table column pickers, the settings section picker, the page picker, the pickers picker, the "(none)" row of an optional field, the chooser's create row, and the markdown insert, columns and heading pickers. `Choice` gains `Data`, `Noun` and `Props` (the row's handle, noun and typed values; not on the wire) and `RowFlags.Bare` (the id is only a handle: the row shows its label alone, no dim id, as before). Pickers look the same. Path, file and directory pickers and the command palette keep their own row building.
- Added (PU3): `TestPickerRowsAreNotHandBuilt` fails on a `PickerRow` or `ChooserRow` literal outside `params.go` and its allow list (the path-chooser files and the palette, a reason each). `ChooserRow` and `ChooserSpec` stay as aliases.
- Changed: an input never refuses text with line breaks. A single-line input given line breaks (a paste, a register put with a line or block yank, `Input.Replace`, `Input.SetText`, a form prefill or reload) becomes multi-line in place, keeps the text exactly and stays grown; a record or args-form field's height follows. `Input.Replace` no longer returns "single-line input cannot contain newlines", `p`/`P` no longer return "single-line input cannot paste multiline yank", and `SetText` no longer drops everything after the first line. Setting `input.newlines-grow` (default true; off turns each line break into a space; no error either way). `enter` in a single-line field is unchanged. `o`/`O` in a single-line input grow it and open the line instead of erroring (setting off: nothing happens, no error). The palette line, the find line, the chooser's search line, the setting prompt and the filter save form draw a grown input's rows (new `GrownRows(in, width, limit)`, at most a third of the height, the rest scrolls with the cursor). Rule R9.9; story `forms/paste-multiline-into-single-line`.
- Added (pp-r1 referrers): `Runtime.Referrers(noun)` lists the props of other nouns that name the noun's things (`Parameter.Noun`: an owner role or a plain reference) as `Referrer{Noun, Prop, Role, ReadOnly, Label}`, nouns in registration order, props in declaration order. It is computed from the declared props, never a table. The catalog publishes it per noun as `referencedBy`, and the fact noun `ochazuke.referrer` lists it (`list {noun}`, all references without one; key `NOUN.PROP`).
## v0.109.0
- Added: setting `mcp.instructions-nouns` (`all` default, `names`, `first`). MCP startup instructions now list every noun and mounted extension: `all` writes one row per noun (name, verb count, short description), falling back to names only when the text would pass the 1,800-token cap; `names` writes names only on a wrapped paragraph; `first` is the old behaviour (a few rows, then `N more: call catalog`). `cli.AgentInstructionsMode` takes the mode; `cli.AgentInstructions` uses `all`.
- Added (n04 b2): the `?` help's table view sorts and filters like any list. `s` sorts the rows by key (ascending, descending, back to the default order: own keys first, literal before key classes); `ochazuke.collection.sort-column` takes `field` to cycle one named prop. The rows now carry props (`key` as drawn, `unbound`), so the standard sort and prop filter (`z s`, `z /`) work on them.
- Added (n04 b2): the table view lists the noun's unbound verbs as rows under an `unbound` heading below the keys (a verb bound in any column is left out); `enter`, `a` and `b` act on them as in the other views. Setting `help.table-unbound` (default true; off restores the table of bound keys). `u` keeps only the unbound rows (the prop filter `unbound = true`), `U` clears it. Stories `help/table-sort-by-key`, `help/table-unbound`.
- Changed (PU1, pickers on one data mechanism): `Choice` carries the data a picker row needs: `Cells`, `Tones` and the row flags (`RowFlags`: `Opens`, `NoPick`, `Refuse`, `Trailing`, `Fixed`, embedded so a source names them as a group). `ParseChoices` reads `cells`, `tones` and the flags from a source row (a list verb's `cells` reach the picker as aligned columns); `Choice.Row()` is the one place a choice becomes a `PickerRow` and carries them across. `Choice` is no longer comparable with `==` (it holds slices): compare fields or use `reflect.DeepEqual`. `RecordField.choiceRow` no longer branches on `Noun == ""`: every field uses `Choice.Row`, so an enum with labels shows the label first, the id dim.
- Added: `Choice.Entry(order)` (a `ChoiceEntry`: text and the dim-id tone) for the palette's choice completion and a form's chips, replacing the unexported `choiceLabel`: label first, short id dim, as pickers show it. Settings `picker.show-ids` (default on; off hides the dim id, the filter still reads it), `picker.columns` (default on; off draws the label alone for rows with cells) and `palette.choice-label` (`label-first` default, `id-first` restores the earlier `id  label` look in the palette and chips). Stories `pickers/choice-label-everywhere`, `pickers/choice-label-id-first`.
- Changed (n05-esc-vs-modal-close): `esc` only leaves insert; `q` closes a modal. The default `esc` rows that closed a modal are guarded `setting:modal.esc-closes` (the modal noun's, the confirm's, the palette's, the pickers', the find line's, the setting value prompt's, the settings page's and the About page's), and a modal's text input (a prompt, the palette line, a picker's search, the find line, the setting value prompt) is no longer pinned to insert: `esc` there leaves insert for the modal's normal mode (`ochazuke.prompt/insert` esc runs `mode.switch normal`, guard `input-unpinned`), where `q` closes it and `esc` does nothing. A modal form's text field and the settings review follow the same two keys. Verbs are unchanged (`modal.close`, `about.close`, `setting.close`, `picker.close`, `palette.close`).
- Added: setting `modal.esc-closes` (boolean, default **false**, section Modals): on, `esc` closes a modal as before and a modal's text input stays pinned to insert. Rows follow it through the new general row guard `setting:<name>` (a `when` that holds while that boolean setting is on), and the guard `input-unpinned`. Set `modal.esc-closes` true to restore the old behaviour in full; the bindings alone cannot restore a pinned text input.
- `q` in the palette's normal mode runs `palette.close` (closes at once, a typed line or not); `palette.cancel` stays a verb. The insert-mode `esc` row shows a hint (`esc normal`) in the footer of the palette, pickers and other modal inputs.
- Story `modals/esc-leaves-insert-q-closes`; the stories that closed a modal with `esc` close it with `q`.
- Added: row guards, a guard written as data in its name: `row:FIELD` (the row a key acts on has the field), `row:!FIELD` (it has not), `row:FIELD=VALUE` (its text is VALUE). They read the row the way a `row:FIELD` binding source does (its Data, then its list item's props) and need no `DefineGuard`. `PortableGuard(name)` reports a guard that means the same in every runtime (the built-ins and row guards), so a host mounting an extension's catalog bindings can keep it. Tests `TestRowGuardReadsTheFocusedRow`, `TestPortableGuard`, `TestMountPageRowGuardScopesKeysToMatchingRows`.
## v0.106.0
- Changed: the binding walk, hint and footer-cell caches validate with a render stamp (`RenderStamp`: four counters for binding, guard, availability, extends, mode, noun and alias writes, mode changes, instances and the operation registry) instead of comparing every table each frame. A cache hit is a stamp, focus, mode and level comparison, not a deep walk of the rows (idle `levelRows` 79 us to 11 us, `HintCells` 97 us to 12 us, `BindingHelp` 532 us to 181 us in the fixture). The help's unbound-verb section reads the operation index instead of splitting every registered key.
- Added: `Runtime.InvalidateBindings()`. Every writer in the package calls it (guard `TestRenderInputWritersBump`); a host that edits a public `Noun`/`Instance` `Bindings`, `Guards`, `Availability` or `Extends` table in place, past `Bind` and the builders, calls it afterwards, or hints and help keep the old rows. Tests `TestRenderStampInvalidatesOnEveryInput`; benchmarks `BenchmarkBindingWalkCached`, `BenchmarkBindingHelpCached`, `BenchmarkHintCellsCached`.

## v0.105.1
- Fixed: a parameter source that reads a blank row field (`From: row:id`) refuses only when the parameter is Required; an optional target is left out instead. A mounted verb with an optional target and a collection form (o-lane `lane archive --finished-trees`) run from the CLI or MCP with no focused row now runs; before, it failed with `target field "id" is empty` before the handler ran. A binding row's own From keeps the error. Test `TestParameterSourceBlankRowLeavesOptionalTargetOut`.

## v0.105.0
- Added (LX5 och-props, lexicon plan §2): computed and collection props. `PropSpec.Read func(Ctx, Thing) (any, error)` computes a prop when it is asked for (`get --prop`, `get --props`, `BIN noun PROP`); it is read-only, not in get's default record or list's rows, not filterable, and never written to the store's thing. `PropSpec.Subject: SubjectCollection` makes a prop about all of a noun's things (read with no subject, a path reads inside it, listed after a thing's props); `CountProp()` and `NamesProp()` are shared ones. `PropSpec.Computed` and `Subject` are on the catalog wire; `cli.MountNouns` reads a mounted computed or collection prop through the extension's own `get {prop}` (pass-through, `proxyCalls.read`). `Operation.Subject` and `NounBuilder.OfCollection()` mark a collection-scope verb (the catalog verb's `subject`). `Ctx.Noun` names the noun a prop is read of. MCP `describe` lists the noun's props (`props`: name, type, readOnly, subject, computed, enum).
