A status prop's `Enum` is the states (no `states` verb); an adjective that picks rows is a saved filter row in `collection.filters` (story `lists/saved-filter-row`).
- Added (LX5): `ochazuke.snapshot.get {lists: true}` answers the rows of every store noun's list as JSON Lines (the framework's state source); `setup install {dry-run}`; `ochazuke.agent` and `ochazuke.config` are singleton things (props `rights`, `instructions`; `path`).
- Changed, breaking (LX5, one shot: no old-name aliases; the old names answer with a `removed_verbs.json` notice, `since` `v0.105.0`; every old and new name is in `docs/lexicon-renames-v0.105.tsv`). Read-only verbs became props, read as `BIN noun PROP`, `NOUN PROP` in the palette, or `get {prop}`:
  - collection props: `ochazuke.user-operation` `aliases`, `path`; `ochazuke.binding` `path`, `defaults`, `stops`; `ochazuke.history-entry` `path`; `ochazuke.hook` `path`; `ochazuke.noun` `places-path`; `ochazuke.setting` `names`, `owners`, `path`; `ochazuke.theme` `path`; `ochazuke.segment` `names`.
  - props of the singleton things: `ochazuke.config` `path`; `ochazuke.agent` `rights` (it took a `verb` filter; read `rights.N` or use `describe`) and `instructions`; `ochazuke.mcp` `config` and `instructions` (`tools` and `serve` stay verbs).
  - `ochazuke.setup plan` is `ochazuke.setup install --dry-run` (install asks no confirmation under dry-run).
  - Parameter choices sources `ochazuke.setting.names` and `ochazuke.segment.names` are `ochazuke.setting.list` and `ochazuke.segment.list`; the MCP preamble names `ochazuke.agent.get`.
  - Go API: `Operation.State` and `NounBuilder.State()` are gone; a verb that answers every list's rows has `Operation.Result` `ResultLists` (`NounBuilder.Lists()`), and `cli.CatalogDoc.State` / `speaksDelta` read that. Monogatari's `story` state verb must call `.Lists()` instead of `.State()`.
- Fixed (LX5): `cli.MountNouns` leaves out, quietly, a collection prop it cannot read through the extension's `get` (a catalog of the framework's own nouns, whose verbs a host does not mount); it no longer warns `a collection prop needs Read`.
- Changed (LX5): a regenerated standard verb keeps its `Builtin` mark; `testdata/op_traits.txt`, the catalog golden and `docs/reference/` follow.
- Not done (LX5): `note.create {thing}` (the `note` noun is mtm's, not the framework's); `segment.show` stays (its answer holds runtime state a `get` does not); the adjective verbs of qbt, gh and mtm's `extension` are those apps' catalog rows (LX6/LX8).

## v0.104.0
- Renamed, breaking (LX4 och-renames, lexicon plan §3, one shot: no old-name aliases; the old names answer with a `removed_verbs.json` notice, `since` `LX4`). One word has one part of speech: a noun's name is never an operation name, nouns are singular. `testdata/lexiconcheck.txt` is empty (23 rows before). The list of every rename, for the consumers and the owner's config rewrite, is `~/hub/o-plans/docs/lexicon-renames-ochazuke.tsv`.
  - Nouns (all `ochazuke.` names; the verbs keep their names and move with the noun): `ochazuke.actions` is `ochazuke.user-operation`; `ochazuke.bindings` is `ochazuke.binding` (noun merged into ochazuke.binding); `ochazuke.docs` is `ochazuke.docs-viewer` (noun merged into the docs viewer noun); `ochazuke.events` is `ochazuke.event`; `ochazuke.filter-save` is `ochazuke.filter-save-form`; `ochazuke.filter-values` is `ochazuke.filter-value`; `ochazuke.form-chips` is `ochazuke.form-chip`; `ochazuke.glyphs` is `ochazuke.glyph`; `ochazuke.history` is `ochazuke.history-entry`; `ochazuke.hooks` is `ochazuke.hook`; `ochazuke.instances` is `ochazuke.instance`; `ochazuke.list` is `ochazuke.collection`; `ochazuke.messages` is `ochazuke.message`; `ochazuke.modes` is `ochazuke.mode` (noun merged into ochazuke.mode); `ochazuke.nouns` is `ochazuke.noun`; `ochazuke.operations` is `ochazuke.operation`; `ochazuke.registers` is `ochazuke.register` (instance kind of the registers store); `ochazuke.state` is `ochazuke.snapshot`; `ochazuke.themes` is `ochazuke.theme`; `ochazuke.view` is `ochazuke.viewport`.
  - Verbs (noun then verb, as typed in the palette): `ochazuke.about-row` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.about` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.bindings-help` `mode` is `pick` with {what: mode}; `ochazuke.bindings-help` `noun` is `pick` with {what: noun}; `ochazuke.bindings-help` `place` is `pick` with {what: place}; `ochazuke.bindings-help` `view` is `show` with {step}; `ochazuke.filter-form` `focus` is `goto` with {part}; `ochazuke.find-line` `commit` is `accept`; `ochazuke.input` `focus` is `ochazuke.focus` `to` with {id}; `ochazuke.markdown-view` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.markdown` `link-copy` is `link-yank`; `ochazuke.markdown` `note` is `annotate`; `ochazuke.markdown` `outline` is `goto` with {title}; without it the heading picker; `ochazuke.mode` `register` is `create` with {name, pending}; `ochazuke.setting-value` `commit` is `accept`; `ochazuke.setting` `review` is `diff` with {review: true}; `ochazuke.settings-section` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.settings-tab` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.json-node` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.detail-field` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.setup` `copy` is `yank`; `ochazuke.setup` `status` is `check`.
  - Settings: the 22 `list.*` settings are `collection.*` (`list.per-page` is `collection.per-page`, `list.no-spill` is `collection.no-spill`, `list.filters` is `collection.filters`, and so on); an old key in `settings.json` is no longer read.
  - Binding contexts follow the noun: `ochazuke.list/normal` is `ochazuke.collection/normal`, `ochazuke.view/normal` is `ochazuke.viewport/normal`, `ochazuke.history/...` is `ochazuke.history-entry/...`, and so on for every renamed noun. A palette or CLI line reads the new noun: `collection move 1` (not `list move 1`); `help bindings` now opens the reference docs topic `bindings`, the keys help is `help binding`.
  - Go API: the type `List` is `Collection`; the store-kind constant `Collection` is `KindCollection` (`Singleton` and `Instances` keep their names). `AddList`, `Runtime.List(id)`, `ListItem`, `ListQuery` and the other `List` names keep theirs. `JobInfo.State`, `TickerEntry.State` and `setup.Status.State` are `Status`; their JSON key `state` is `status` (job rows, the snapshot's jobs, setup rows). The snapshot's `components[].component` says `collection` for a list and `register` for the registers component.
  - MCP and CLI: the MCP tool `state` is `snapshot` (the preamble follows); `bin setup status` is `bin setup check` (the bare `bin setup` runs it) and `bin setup copy` is `bin setup yank`.
- Changed (LX4): the framework short names `event`, `glyph`, `hook`, `instance`, `message`, `theme`, `operation`, `noun`, `snapshot`, `collection`, `viewport`, `history-entry`, `user-operation`, `filter-value`, `form-chip` and `filter-save-form` are now claimed by the framework's nouns: an application noun of one of those names declares itself with `DefineOwn` (a plain `Define` extends the framework's noun).
- Added (LX4): `ochazuke.mode.create {name, pending}` (it was the old register verb): `factsSpec.Create` lets a facts noun take the standard `create` (`needFactCreator`), and a create-only key prop is named by `create` (`createProps`). `ochazuke.viewport.scroll {by: line|page}` takes the page motion (it was `view.page`); `ochazuke.collection.goto {page}` shows a page of a paged list (it was `collection.page`); `ochazuke.focus.to {id}` focuses a component through its own focus target (`FocusTargeter`: a list lands on its selected row), which replaces the verbs `focus` of input and of the list component; `ochazuke.bindings-help.pick {what: noun|mode|place}`; `ochazuke.markdown.goto {title}` jumps to a heading by its text; `ochazuke.setting.diff {review: true}` opens the review modal.
- Changed (LX4): the help's and the title bar's noun names are longer (`collection` for `list`), so the `?` help table, the focus path and the footer hints reflow; `testdata/help-80.golden` and the o-story goldens that print a noun were re-recorded for the new text and width only.
- Deferred (LX4): `setup.plan` (it becomes `install {dry-run}` once the CLI's own `setup plan` command is reshaped), the `state` verbs and the `Operation.State` flag (LX5 `snapshot.get {lists}`), prop conversions (LX5), and the `note.create {thing}` mirror of `markdown.annotate`.

## v0.103.0
- Added (LX3 och-lexicon-guard, lexicon plan): package `lexiconcheck`, the naming guard over the registered operations. `lexiconcheck.Check(t, lexiconcheck.Config{Operations: &rt.Operations, Words, Baseline, MainNouns})` fails a test on a noun that is also an action name (whole names, so `copy-mode` is not `copy`), a singular and plural pair, a plural-only noun, a namespace that is a noun or action word (unless `MainNouns` declares it, `lane: lane`), a key of more than three parts, and, with the word list, an unclassified word or one used against its part of speech. `lexiconcheck.Scan(ops, words, mainNouns)` returns the violations for other callers. Known violations are a baseline file that only shrinks (`LEXICONCHECK_UPDATE=1` drops stale rows, `seed` writes a new one). ochazuke's own: `lexiconcheck_test.go` over `testdata/lexiconcheck.txt` and `testdata/lexicon-words.tsv` (a copy of the family word list, `~/hub/tools/lexicon-audit/words.tsv`). mtm and each tool adopt it with one test over their mounted registry.
- Changed, breaking (LX2 och-reader, lexicon plan §4): one reader for the palette, the CLI's verb-first words and completions (`reader.go`, `reader_runtime.go`, `palette_reader.go`), in place of `pair_reader.go` and the palette's first-word branches. `Operations.Lookup(line)` and `Operations.SplitLookup(word1, word2)` read the registry's own words; `Reading{Lexicon, Within, Bind, Prefer, Punctuation, WordsOnly}` with `Read`, `Candidates`, `Choose` and `Complete` is a caller's scope (`Runtime.Lexicon()` adds shown names, noun and action aliases, tails and props; `Runtime.ReadingWithin`, `MainNoun(namespace)`, `Runtime.AmbiguityOf`, `Ambiguity`, `CandidateWords`, `Operation.BindWords`). The first two words pair in both orders (`w1.nouns × w2.actions ∪ w2.nouns × w1.actions`), a missing word an omitted part matching everything; ranking is one `best` helper over data keys in two steps (what the typed words name: exact > alias > tail > prefix > omitted, an action over a prop, a whole action word over a whole noun word, how far a typed pair's rest binds; then the omitted part: the noun's default action, the rest binding, the focus path, the main noun); no branch counts candidates. `Match` gains `Via` and `Alias`; `Candidate` gains `Prop`, `Order`, `Words`, `Rest`, `Rank`. Removed: `Runtime.ReadPair`, `Pair`, `PairScope`, `choosePair`, `Palette.readPair`, the palette's `impliedNoun` and `nounReads`, cli's `ownNoun` (a name test).
- Added (LX2): `Noun.Main` (`NounBuilder.Main()`, the catalog noun's `main`): the program's main noun, which a word that leaves the noun out reads as first within its namespace (`o-lane add` is `lane add`, `mtm lane define` is `lane lane define`). This replaces the bin-name tie-break: a tool declares it (o-lane, o-questions, o-cmds: one `.Main()` each), and a host mounting the catalog keeps it.
- Added (LX2): `Noun.DefaultAction` (`NounBuilder.DefaultAction(name)`, the catalog noun's `defaultAction`): the action a noun's words run with no action word, or only flags (`review --web` is `review page --web`), on the CLI (`cli.Mount`) and the palette; a noun typed alone is never given any other action. o-questions can drop its `nounDefault` rewrite.
- Added (LX2): punctuation hints, setting `lexicon.punctuation` (default on): `a.b` reads noun.action and `a.b.c` namespace.noun.action (`json.doc.rows`), `a, b` reads action, noun (`rows, json.doc`), spaces allowed around the mark.
- Changed (LX2, cli): `cli.Mount` reads a first word that names no command exactly through the reader when every command it is a prefix of is a noun group (`o-x t r upper`; several fits print the reader's ambiguity); a framework noun's short-name group (`list` for `ochazuke.list`) is `oden.Command.Yield`, so `o-lane list --state running` runs `lane list` when the tool's nouns read the words; `BIN complete -- WORD` completes nouns and verbs through `Reading.Complete` (and a verb-first line's parameters), the list the palette's first word shows.
- Fixed (LX2, cli): an application command named like a mounted noun no longer hides the noun's verbs silently (o-questions' `review`): a command with children gains the noun's verbs it lacks; one with none is reported on stderr (`warning: ... hides the noun review and its verbs`).
- Changed (LX2): an ambiguity names the words from the first the ties read differently: `"t t" is ambiguous: text tidy, timeline toggle`, `"doc rows" is ambiguous: json.doc rows, md.doc rows`, `"c" is ambiguous: cell, column`; ties over an omitted noun list `noun verb` pairs (`"show" is ambiguous: agent show, x show`). Settings `palette.pair-words` and `cli.verb-first` keep turning the readings off. Stories `palette/both-orders`, `palette/punctuation-hint`, `palette/ambiguous-ties`; the shared table `internal/readertest` runs on both the palette (`TestReaderTablePalette`) and the CLI (`cli.TestReaderTable`).
## v0.102.0
- Changed, breaking (LX1 och-operations, lexicon plan): the operation registry is `Operations` (`Runtime.Operations`), with the three-part `Key` (`namespace.noun.name`) in place of the string fields. Breaking changes:
  - `Operation.Noun` and `Operation.Verb` are gone: `Operation.Key Key` holds the name; read it with `op.Noun()` (the registered noun, as the field held), `op.Name()` (what was the verb), `op.Namespace()`, `op.Action()`, or the parts `op.Key.Namespace()/Noun()/Name()`. `op.Key()` (the method) is `op.Key.Short()` (the same `noun.verb` string).
  - `Operation.App AppHandler` is `Operation.AppHandler`; `Operation.App App` is now the program that runs the handler and `Operation.Handler HandlerID` addresses it there (`Operations.Push` fills both).
  - `Runtime.Operations` is `Operations{All map[Key]*Operation, Order, Index, NounTable}`, not `map[string]Operation`: `r.Operations[k]` is `r.Operations.At(k)` (a copy) or `Find(k)`/`Get(k)`; writes go through `Register`/`Push`/`Set`; `delete` is `Delete`, `range` is `Entries()` (short keys and copies), `len` is `Len()`. Only `Push`, `Delete` and `Invalidate` write it (`TestOperationsWriters`).
  - Keys always have three parts once registered: a two-part key (`pane.kill`) is in `DefaultNamespace`, the program's declared namespace (`DeclareNamespace`, singular) or its binary's name without `o-` (`main` in a test binary). Two-part spellings in bindings, typed lines, history and code still read; `Short()` is that spelling. A name holding a dot is refused (`KeyOf` panics): `dirs.base.add` as a dotted verb no longer registers.
  - `Noun.Verbs` and `NounBuilder.Verbs` are removed: a noun's actions are `r.Operations.ActionsOnNoun(n)` (`ActionNames(n)` for the words).
  - The Go type `Action` (an actions.json entry) is `UserOperation`; `Action` is now the cross-noun object of an operation's name.
- Added: `Operations` lookups over a memoized `Index` (built in one pass on the first lookup after `Invalidate`; `Hold` defers invalidation so a mount of many verbs invalidates once): `Namespaces`, `Nouns`, `Actions`, `List`, `Get`, `Namespace`, `Noun`, `Action`, `OperationFor`, `NounsWithAction`, `NamespacesWithAction`, `OperationsOfAction`, `ActionsOnNoun`, `NounsInNamespace`, `ActionsInNamespace`, `NamespacesMatching`/`NounsMatching`/`ActionsMatching` (typed `Match{OriginalValue, PartOfSpeech, Namespace, Noun, Action, Exact}`); `Candidate` for the reader (LX2). `Namespace`, `Noun` and `Action` are plain data.
- Added: the `ochazuke.action` facts noun (one row per action: name, nouns, operations) with `list {noun}`, mirrored by `ochazuke.nouns list {action}`; `ochazuke.operations` rows gain `name` and `namespace`; Operation JSON keeps `noun` and `verb` and adds `key`, `name` and `namespace`; a catalog carries its declared `namespace`, which hosts mount under (`cli.CatalogNamespace`, `cli.OwnNamespace`).
- Migration: run the codemod before bumping, against the ochazuke you build with today: `go run ofam.dev/ochazuke/cmd/opname@<this release> ./...` (add `-tags explore` for tagged files and run it on `testdata` programs by path), then bump, build, and fix the uses it lists as `manual:` (the registry passed on as a map, a multiple assignment of `Noun`/`Verb`) and every `Noun.Verbs` read. `-n` reports without writing; a second run changes nothing.
## v0.101.0

- Added (questions-review-in-mtm): `Operation.OpensPage` / `NounBuilder.OpensPage(id)` and the catalog field `opensPage`: a verb that opens a page of its own tool. A host that mounts the catalog opens that page instead of running the tool (mtm's extension proxy); run standalone the tool ignores it.
- Added (och-drawn-bindings): bindings read at a drawn surface. New `Runtime.ListBindingsAt(id)` returns the rows the surface holding `id` is drawn with (its drawn focus and drawn base mode, bounded by the modals open at it; `PresentationAt`'s rule), built on the pure resolution context of MO-07, with no cache read or fill and no Runtime write; `HintCellsAt(r, id)` is the hint line the same way. A page beneath a modal gets its own bindings, a modal beneath another the ones the modal above it saved; the top surface and an unknown id are live. `ListBindings(focus)` and `HintCells` stay the live readings. Visible change: a modal covered by another draws its hint line in its own mode (help under the palette keeps its normal-mode hints; it used to resolve them in the palette's mode and show none). Apps: replace a memo of covered-surface keys with `ListBindingsAt(pageID)`. Tests `drawn_bindings_test.go`; story `modals/covered-hint-line`.

## v0.100.0

- Fixed (och-filter-keeps-children): the `/` filter hid a matching row's child rows (the Settings page's "2 items" row showed no items) and a matching child's parent. `ListItem.Under` names a flat-list row's parent row; the filter keeps a matched row's children and a matched child's parent, in list order, and "N of M" counts them. The Settings page sets `Under` on a list setting's item rows. Setting `list.filter-keeps-children` (default on) turns it off. Trees already keep a match's ancestors through `TreeNode.Parent` (a matching tree node does not keep its descendants).
## v0.99.0

- Added (och-pair-reader): one pair reader for the first two words. An ambiguous first word of a palette line is settled by the second: every verb and noun it could be reads the second word (a verb's noun, a noun's verb, prop or instance) and the readings where it resolves survive, one taking the line (`t r up` is `row transform`, `t tid` is `text tidy`); several that fit are an error listing the noun verb pairs (`"t t" is ambiguous: text tidy, timeline toggle`), the one whose whole line resolves further winning a tie. This replaces the exactly-one-verb-and-one-noun special block; a first word with no second word that fits stays `"X" is ambiguous`. Completions after an ambiguous first word offer the words fitting any of its readings. Setting `palette.pair-words` (default on) turns it off. `choosePair` (`pair_reader.go`) is the rule; `Palette.readPair` feeds it parsed lines.
- Added (och-pair-reader): verb-first words in the CLI. `Runtime.ReadPair(words, PairScope)` reads `VERB [NOUN]` over a tool's nouns (the framework's own verbs, `Operation.Builtin`, aside); `cli.Mount` runs a first word that names no command but is a verb of the tool's nouns as that verb (`o-lane add`, `o-lane define --project P`): the verb's only noun, else the second word's, else the noun named like the tool's namespace (`ExtensionNamespace`), else an ambiguity listing the noun verb pairs. A namespace group does the same for its nouns (`mtm lane state` is `mtm lane lane state`). It becomes the long spelling's argv, so flags, positionals and `--help` are the long form's. A machine builtin's name (`state`) gives way to a noun's verb of that name through oden's `Claims`. `cli.Shell.VerbFirstDefault(app, next)` chains a tool's own `Default` set after `Mount`. Setting `cli.verb-first` (default on) turns it off. Needs oden after abd2fdb (`WithClaims`, `Command.Yield`).
- Changed (och-pair-reader): requires oden v0.3.0 (`WithClaims`, `Command.Yield`) in the root module, `cmd/o-story` and `examples`.
- Fixed (och-pair-reader): `BIN NOUN VERB --target ID TEXT` dropped its text when the verb declares a parameter called `target`: the flag set `Request.Target` while the first bare word filled the parameter, and the text went to `target`, leaving the next parameter empty with exit 0. The flag now fills a declared parameter of that name, as the bare word does.
## v0.98.0

- Added (och-motions, clean-slate pocket 2): a motion table. Every input motion is a row of `motions` (`motions.go`), `Motion{Linewise, Inclusive, KeepsGoal, SelectEdge, Change, To}` over the new exported `TextBuffer` interface (`Runes()`, `ScreenLines()`); `Input` implements it and moves, counts, extends visual selections and builds every operator range from the row, so `Input.move`'s three switches, `operatorEnd` and the motion compares in `operatorSelect` are gone (34 namecheck rows removed; `input.kill`'s spans are the `killMotions` table). New rows and keys in `bindings/editor.json` (normal, visual, operator-pending): `W`/`B`/`E` (`WORD-forward`/`WORD-backward`/`WORD-end`, `cW` changes to the WORD's end), `}`/`{` (`paragraph-forward`/`paragraph-backward`, guarded `multiline`, capture rows in normal so a multiline input's paragraph motions win over `focus.next`; a single-line input still walks the stops), `H`/`M`/`L` (`screen-top`/`screen-middle`/`screen-bottom`, within the lines the last frame drew) and `%` (`match`, brackets). Unbinding a row turns its key off. New exported: `TextBuffer`, `Motion`, `MotionArgs`, `MotionTable` (a namecheck declaration type), `TextMotion`, `MotionNames`, `TextLineBounds`, `ClampOnChar`, `InputScreen`, `Input.Runes`, `Input.ScreenLines`, `Placement.Screen` (the lines an `InputBlock` showed). `NameVocab` adds the table's motion names. Existing motions unchanged. Tests `motions_test.go`; stories `editor/word-motions`, `editor/paragraph-motions`, `editor/percent`, `editor/screen-motions`.
- Changed (och-presentation, mode-ownership MO-10): rendering borrows no interaction. `Model.beneath` is gone: no frame assigns `Runtime.Focus` or `Runtime.Mode` (their `TestStateWriteOwnership` exception lists are empty). Each surface draws in its presentation by `PresentationAt`'s one rule (now shared by every drawing read): new `Runtime.DrawnFocus(id)`, `Runtime.DrawnBaseMode(id)`, `List.DrawnFocused()`, `Grid.DrawnFocused()`, `Terminal.DrawnFocused()` and `Model.StylesAt(id)` (mode chrome of the surface; `Styles()` stays live). Lists, tables, timelines, grids, terminals, choosers, forms, the filter form, diff views, Markdown documents, the Settings and About pages and `cli.PageView` panels draw their highlights, cursors, faded rows and focused borders from them; `Model.follow` follows the page's drawn focus. `Focused()` stays live. Visible change, nested modals only: a modal covered by another keeps the state the modal above saved (help under the palette keeps its selected row marked, dimmed; story `modals/nested-presentation-state` updated); a saved return without a mode draws in the mode its focus declares (what closing lands in), not the modal's live mode. Apps: a page renderer that read `Focused()`/`Runtime.Focus` beneath a modal now gets the live focus; switch it to the drawn reads. Tests `TestRenderDoesNotBorrowInteraction`, `TestRenderRepeatedAndReentrantInspection`, `TestDrawnModeOfARepairedReturnIsTheLanding`; stories `modals/under-modal-*` (visual list, table cell, completion, hidden stop, narrow and wide), goldens identical to v0.97.0.
- Changed (och-modal-owner, mode-ownership MO-09): the modal stack has one owner, `modal_stack.go`. Each open modal's saved return is one value, `ModalReturnState{Focus, Mode}`, in the new exported `Runtime.ModalReturns` (by modal id), with the new detached `Runtime.ModalFrames()` (`ModalFrame{ID, Return, Created}`); `ModalReturn(id)` keeps its shape. An open modal's `Payload.Return` is now a compatibility snapshot of the saved focus, kept in step, not the authority: a write to it through a `Modals()` pointer no longer changes what Pop restores. Push, Pop, OpenModal/ReplaceOpen, RetargetFocus, subtree removal, PinInstance and the end of a visual selection call the owner's methods; `TestStateWriteOwnership` now also guards `modals`, `modalInstances`, `ModalReturns`, `Instance.Payload.Return`, `borrowed` and `lentParent` (index writes, delete/clear/copy and in-place slices/sort calls count as writes). Nothing was unexported; no behaviour change.

## v0.97.0

- Changed (och-focus-owner, mode-ownership MO-08): `Runtime.Focus` has one writer, `changeFocus` (`focus_change.go`): `setFocus` (so `SetFocus`, `RetargetFocus`, `Push` and focus jumps), `Pop`, `OpenPage`, a page's `OnOpen` re-landing (About), the repair after `RemoveInstance` and the constructor all go through it. Each move names its landing (`focusLanding` applies the focus mode rule, `focusRestore` the saved modal or page mode, `focusSettle` no mode rule, `focusAssign` nothing more), so the writer selects the focused row, remembers the path being left and commits exactly one mode transition (no default landing before a saved restore). The field stays exported and readable; nothing was unexported. `TestStateWriteOwnership` now guards `Runtime.Focus` as it guards `Runtime.Mode` (one subtest per field); its only Focus exception is the render borrow in `Model.beneath` (MO-10). Tests that set a focus no public move reaches use `forceFocus`, through the writer. No behaviour change.
- Changed (md-link-urls): a rendered Markdown link draws only its text, underlined, as an OSC 8 hyperlink; the focused link's URL is a faint `→ URL` line under the document. Setting `markdown.link-urls` (`focused` default, `always` the old layout with URLs after the text, `never`), steppable from a `bindings.json` row through `ochazuke.setting.cycle`; tables follow it; links keep their focus stops and `link-open`/`link-copy` use the URL. New: tone `markdown.link` (`markdown.LinkTone`, no built-in row) colours link text; `markdown.HideLinkURLs()` for `Block` callers.
- Changed (och-mode-owner, mode-ownership MO-06): `Runtime.Mode` has one writer, `changeMode`: `mode.switch` and `Runtime.SetMode` share one named-mode request, and pending entry/leave, `count.append`, focus landing, restores, a focused input's mirror and the constructor all go through it, advancing the mode generation in that one place (an explicit same-name `SetMode` now counts as a mode change, as `mode.switch` always did). The field stays exported and readable; the AST guard `TestStateWriteOwnership` fails on any other write to it (assignment, member, address, Runtime copy or literal) across the root module, tests and nested modules, except five listed legacy query/render borrows (StopBindings, TypingShadows, help refresh, remote replay, Model.beneath) left for MO-07 and MO-10. No behaviour change otherwise.
- Changed (och-pure-queries, mode-ownership MO-07): the binding queries resolve "as if" without touching the runtime. `bindings.stops` (`Runtime.StopBindings`), the typing check (`Runtime.TypingShadows`) and the bindings help refreshed at the place it opened over each read their hypothetical focus, mode and modal chain from an internal resolution context passed down the walk (`walkIn`, `stackIn`) instead of swapping `Runtime.Mode`/`Runtime.Focus` or copying the Runtime, and read and fill no binding or help cache. Guards judged by such a query see it on the new `KeyEvent.Within` field; `Runtime.EventFocus(ev)`, `Runtime.EventModals(ev)` and `Runtime.EventTopModal(ev)` give the event's focus and modal chain (the query's, else the live one), and the built-in guards that compared with the live focus or modals (`no-modal`, the detail and pane guards, `catalog-detail-fields`, `filter-form-text`, `timeline-on-card`, the markdown editor's) read them; the runtime's own fields and accessors always describe the live interaction. Three of the guard's legacy exceptions are gone; `remoteRun.pass` (a replay of handler code, not a query) and `Model.beneath` stay. Output unchanged: a differential run of the old and new queries over the root tests and all o-story stories found no difference.
- Changed (och-request-context, G13): what one request carries is its own `RequestContext` on the new `Context.Request` field (not in JSON): the instance it acts for (`ActsFor`), the focus it was asked at (`AskedAt`), its subjects (`Subjects`), whether a binding row made it (`FromRow`) and the mode it started in (`Mode`). `execute` builds one per request and hands it to the handler; a nested request builds its own. The runtime fields `origin`, `askedAt`, `rowRequest` and `subjects` and their save/restore pairs are gone; the instance a running request or a completion scope acts for (the modal top-level rule) is read from the innermost of the running request contexts, and `Runtime.RunningRequests()` lists them for inspection. A remote store's replayed standard rows read the mode their request started in through the request context instead of assigning `Runtime.Mode`, so `remoteRun.pass` leaves the mode-write guard's exceptions (only `Model.beneath` remains, MO-10). A handler's `Context` now compares unequal to `r.Context()` when it carries a request context. No behaviour change otherwise; nothing was unexported.

## v0.96.0

- Added (och-base-modes-nested): `Runtime.BaseModeNames()` and `Runtime.PendingModeNames()` list the defined base and pending modes, sorted; prefer them to reading `Runtime.Modes`. The nested modules (`cmd/o-story`, `examples`) require monogatari v0.5.0.
- Changed (och-picker-names, pickers PK2): one term, "picker". Choosers and the path finder are one noun, `ochazuke.picker`, whose shared verbs every open picker answers (a chooser through `OpenPicker`, a registered picker, the path picker, the path finder): `pick` (new `current` argument: the path finder's folder), `cancel`, `move {delta, to}`, `into`, `up`, `enter`, `complete`, `next-source`, `previous-source`, `clear-props`, `normal`, beside PK1's `open`, `list`, `get` and `watch`. `bindings/chooser.json` and the finder's rows merge into `bindings/picker.json` (loaded by every runtime; choosers gain `ctrl+s` pick and `ctrl+t` cycle-match, the finder gains the chooser's motion keys, `alt+enter` and `ctrl+l`). Renamed, each old name kept one release (since v0.96.0, removed after v0.97.0) with one notice: noun `ochazuke.chooser` (and `chooser`) → `ochazuke.picker` (a deprecated noun alias: requests, binding rows and contexts spelled by it still run; `bindings check` notes them); verb `ochazuke.chooser.open` → `ochazuke.picker.into` (an alias spelled under the old noun, so it never reaches the picker noun's `open`); path finder verbs `ochazuke.picker.choose` → `pick`, `close` → `cancel`, `descend` → `into`, `parent` → `up` (action aliases; `enter`, `complete` and `move` keep their names); settings `chooser.match`, `chooser.up-from-none`, `chooser.prop-filters`, `chooser.remember-source`, `chooser.claim-source`, `chooser.stable-height`, `chooser.hidden-count`, `chooser.enter` → `picker.*` and `edit.field-chooser` → `edit.field-picker` (`Setting.Deprecated`); guards `chooser-sources`, `chooser-opens`, `chooser-enter-opens`, `chooser-up`, `chooser-up-empty`, `chooser-normal-mode`, `chooser-prop-controls` → `picker-*` (old names still defined); settings section `choosers` → `pickers`; `forms.single-path` value `chooser` → `picker` (a stale `chooser` reads the default, which behaves the same); the source load job `ochazuke.chooser.load` → `ochazuke.picker.load` (a story's `{"job": "chooser.load"}` waits become `picker.load`). The picker thing's prop `pick` is now `pick-verb` (it clashed with the `pick` verb). Kept as is: edit's `chooser` argument (a plumbing parameter; renaming it could clash with an application prop named picker), modal kinds (`path-chooser`) and story fields. Go: `PickerSpec`, `PickerRow`, `PickerSource`, `OpenPicker` and `PathFinder` are the names; `ChooserSpec`, `ChooserRow`, `ChooserSource` (type aliases), `OpenChooser` (a forwarding method) and `Picker` (alias of `PathFinder`) stay one release. New general mechanism: `Runtime.RenameNoun(NounRename{From, To, Since, RemoveAfter, Note, Verbs})` renames a noun for one release, and `AddNounAlias` takes a dotted alias when it is deprecated (a renamed noun's old canonical name). Story `pickers/old-names-alias`.
- Changed (och-path-find-source, pickers PK3): the path finder is the path picker's `find` source (`path_find.go`): its Git-aware recursive index (shallow rows first, the recursive index replacing them, run once per root between keystrokes; title says `indexing…` or `index incomplete: …`), its ranking in the picker's match mode (`ctrl+t` cycles and ranks again), size and modified cells (the first 256 rows statted off the loop, cached) and its preview (a job per highlight). `ochazuke.picker.open` with no name opens the path picker on `find` for the input in focus (path field: replaced whole; paths: the cursor's line; other: inserted at the cursor; none: the pick is the answer), with or without `NewPicker`; the named `path` picker opens on it too. New setting `path.find` (default on) offers `find` in every path picker; off, only where asked for by name. Visible change: the finder is drawn as a picker (title with the sources, `› query` relative to the folder searched, `ctrl+o` to browse/recent/typed, the preview beside the rows from 60 columns) rather than its own `Path [filter]:` modal with a results table header and help lines; a pick refused because the field changed or a newline name in a paths field now closes the picker with the error (the field is untouched). No verb or key row changes; the finder's old verbs (`choose`, `close`, `descend`, `parent`) stay aliases (v0.96.0, RemoveAfter v0.97.0). Go: `NewPicker`/`PathFinder` are a deprecated shim (kept for one release; `PathFinder` keeps its exported fields, filled by `Refresh` and every method from the open find, and `ViewBlock` draws the path picker); `PickerSource` gains `LoadMode`, `Preview`, `Complete`, `Current`, `Close`; `ChooserBatch.Replace`; `Chooser.LoadedMode`, `Chooser.Preview` (`ChooserPreview`); `PathChooserSpec.Query`; `PathAny` and `PathPick.Files`; `PathSourceFind`, `PathFindSetting`.

## v0.95.0

- Changed (och-mode-traits): one mode table of `ModeSpec` traits replaces the runtime's five per-mode maps (pending, passthrough, selection, extends, outside); `ModeSpec` gains `Typing` (insert) and `CursorOnChar` (normal), which the built-in modes declare as data and a `ModeOption` to `DefineMode` can set. Framework code asks a mode's traits instead of comparing its name to "normal" or "insert" (the editor's motions and erase, the record editor, trees, markdown, help, the shadow-idle guard, completion); behaviour is unchanged, and the traits stay out of a bindings file's "modes". New `Runtime.ModeTraits(name)` and `Runtime.IsBaseMode(name)`; `Runtime.Modes` stays as a read-only view of the base modes (read it through `IsBaseMode`; writing it no longer defines a mode).
- Added (och-chooser-match): `ChooserSpec.Match` is the match mode a chooser opens in (fuzzy, substring, regex); it wins over `chooser.match`, empty is the setting, and tab still steps it. `ChooserSource.Matched` lets a Live source's rows be narrowed by the chooser's match mode, so tab (`search.cycle-match`) cycles on live sources that ask for it; a Live source without it keeps ranking its own rows and has no mode, as before. Story `choosers/live-tab-cycles`.
- Added (och-path-start): `Parameter.Start` (so `PropSpec` and `RecordField` too) names the path chooser source a path field opens on (`browse`, `recent`, `typed`, `walk` or an `AddPathSource` name), in the args form and the record editor; `PathChooserSpec.Source` still wins, an unknown name opens as before. `Runtime.PathStartSource` reads it. Story `forms/path-start-declared`.
- Added (och-interaction-accessors, MO-01): detached interaction reads. `Runtime.CurrentMode()` is the live mode with its payload copied (stored request, arguments, subjects, actor, macro shadow), `Runtime.FocusID()` the live focus, and `Runtime.PresentationAt(id)` a `Presentation` (surface, focus, mode, context, visible modals, live) of the page or modal that holds id: the top surface is live, a covered modal reads the return saved by the modal directly above it, the current page beneath modals the outermost return, another page what it remembered. Changing a result never changes the runtime and asking never moves focus or mode. `Runtime.Mode` and `Runtime.Focus` still compile; prefer the accessors (the fields become private in a later release). Stories `modals/nested-presentation-state`, `modes/explicit-set-versus-focus`.
- Added (och-picker-registry, pickers PK1): pickers as data. `PickerDef` (name, title, about, noun, source, sources, preview, pick, verb, match) registered with `Runtime.AddPicker`, in a catalog's `pickers` array (`cli.CatalogPickers`, `cli.MountCatalogPickers`), or derived: every noun with a list verb gets a picker named after it (setting `picker.derive`, default on; `NounBuilder.NoPicker` and the catalog's `noPicker` opt a noun out; a declared picker naming the noun replaces it). `ochazuke.picker` is now a thing noun whose things are the pickers (key `name`; standard `get`, `list`, `watch`), with `ochazuke.picker.open {name, query}` (no name: the path picker, or the path finder's open as before with `filter`/`path`); the framework declares `pickers` (the picker of pickers, pick opens the one picked; `picker.picker-list` `all`|`declared`, default `all`), `pages` (runs `page.pick`), `path` and, with the palette, `commands`. A form field whose parameter names a noun opens that noun's picker (its source and match mode; setting `picker.params`, default on). No default key; `ochazuke.picker.open` is now registered by every runtime (the path finder's `NewPicker` no longer registers its own `open`). Go API: `OpenNamedPicker`, `PickerDefs`, `PickerDef`, `PickerFor`, `DeclaredPickers`, `CatalogPickerDefs`.

## v0.94.2

- Changed (och-copy-kept-notice): a copy store whose source refuses a Through prop keeps the write and says so as a plain notice naming the thing by its label (its key when it has none), not an error.

## v0.94.1

- Added (och-prop-cell): `PropSpec.Cell func(Ctx, Thing) string` says how a prop reads in a table cell of a `From` list (a project's name for its id, an argv joined by spaces, a `~` path): derived on each fill, never stored; without it the cell is the value as text. `get`, `list` and CLI output keep the store's values.

## v0.94.0 (2026-11-07)

Clean-slate Pocket 1, the rest of the library half: name tables and switches become data (och-edit-subject, och-record-outbox, och-param-kinds, och-small-merges, och-register-tables, och-ext-keys, och-op-traits, och-arg-and-action-rules; och-ext-modal was already on main). Behaviour is unchanged except where a line says so.

- Added (och-param-kinds): parameter type `number` is accepted everywhere a parameter is read: the palette (`x must be a number`), the CLI (parsed to a float64) and the runtime's argument check (int or float64). It is not an action parameter type. One `paramKinds` table holds each type's form, palette, CLI, record-editor and runtime readings and its example; `ParseFlagValue(type, text)` is the CLI's reading. Today's per-route differences (boolean and integer spacing) are kept and pinned by `param_kinds_test.go`.
- Changed (och-edit-subject): `Result.also` and `job.alsoThen` chain a callback after one already set; the edit verb's handler is an `editSession` (subjects, writer, form binding) instead of one 178-line closure.
- Changed (och-record-outbox): the record editor's write state (written, baseline, dirty, in-flight, error, owner) lives in one `recordOutbox`, the only code that touches it; `send` is split into next, take and dispatch.
- Changed (och-small-merges): component getters are one-liners over a generic `componentAt`/`nearestComponent`; one SGR scanner (`rewriteSGR`) serves the modal foreground and backdrop un-highlight.
- Changed (och-register-tables): the editor operations, table cells, settings editor and markdown verbs register from verb tables (`verbSpec` rows with named handlers, guards and traits) instead of four functions over the complexity ceiling; the catalog is byte-identical.
- Changed (och-arg-and-action-rules): `Runtime.argument` reads binding `from` sources from one table (`argSourceRows`, parsed by `parseSource`; `validSource` checks against the same table), and `Action.check` evaluates ordered rule rows (`actionRules`: name and test, first failing message wins). Behaviour and error texts are unchanged (pinned by `rules_pin_test.go`); 22 namecheck baseline rows dropped.
- Changed (och-op-traits): the framework's operation name tables (`motionOps`, `focusBoundOps`, `typingOps`, `frameworkHistoryKinds`, `stayingRequests`, `repeatableOperations`, `unpersistedNouns`) are gone. Each operation carries its classification as fields set where it registers: `Motion`, `FocusBound`, `Typing`, `Repeatable`, `HistoryKind`, and the new `Operation.Staying` (the verb manages the pending mode itself); `Noun.Unpersisted` keeps a noun's history out of the history file. `NounBuilder` gains `Typing()`, `Staying()`, `Repeatable()`, `History(kind)`, `Unpersisted()` and `Params(map)` (the last verb's parameters, so an app need not write `Runtime.Operations`); its modifiers share one `lastOp`. `testdata/op_traits.txt` pins every built-in classification. The catalog carries `motion`, `typing` and `staying` per verb and `unpersisted` per noun, and `cli.MountNouns` keeps them (and `focusBound`, already kept), so a mounted verb is classified as its extension declared it. Dead table entries for `ochazuke.surface.scroll` now live on the registration (the verb exists); no behaviour changes.
- Added (SC-06, SC-07): `Noun.Fallback` (`NounBuilder.Fallback`) names the table a focus on the noun reads last, after app; the editor noun names itself (`EditorNoun`). A component declares one through `FallbackKeys` (every `Input` names the editor) and owns its keys through `KeyOwner` (a read-only `Input`; a custom viewer the same way: its keymap answers right after it and consumes the keys it binds that do not run there). The resolver no longer compares a noun's name; `TestBindingRowsAreAtomic` fails on a noun-name comparison in `resolve.go`.
- Removed: the editor's implicit `input` guard (`ImplicitGuard`) and its exception to row identity. Editor rows carry no framework guard: the table is read only for a focus that declares it its fallback. A user row on the editor noun with `"when": "input"` is now a guarded row of its own that answers before the default, like any guarded row; a row without `when` replaces or unbinds the default as before. Dot-repeat reads `Operation.Requires` (the input verbs need an input focused) where it read the implicit guard.
- Changed: a tree and a timeline declare their list their child table (`holdsList`, and the tree's `yieldsEdge`) instead of the list and the table rows recognising them by type.

## v0.93.1 (2026-11-07)

Fixes from mtm's v0.93.0 UX review.

- Fixed (R5.16): `esc` on a chooser a verb opened over a visual selection (the edit chooser, an application's move or status chooser) resumes `visual-line` over the same rows, as the palette's close does; it used to end the selection (`V j m esc` landed in NORMAL). A pick still runs the verb and ends the selection (R5.15). One mechanism for every chooser: a chooser modal says when it is accepted (`Instance.accepts`), and `list.apply` waits for that answer before ending the selection (`untilAccepted`). Stories `verbs/edit-field-chooser-visual-esc-resumes.json`, `verbs/chooser-visual-esc-resumes.json`; test `TestVisualSelectionChooserEscResumes`.
- Fixed (R5.15): `edit {field}`'s chooser over several rows (a visual selection or marks) counts them in its title, "Edit 2 tasks project", as "Move 3 rows to" does; over one row it still names the thing. Story `verbs/edit-field-chooser-visual-title-count.json`.
- Changed: a verb that replaces the standard verb of its name on purpose declares it (`Operation.ShadowsStandard`, `NounBuilder.ShadowsStandard`), and `bindings|actions|hooks check` no longer print its `info: ... shadows the standard ...` line. `ochazuke.catalog.list` and `ochazuke.setup.list` declare it, so every tool's checks are quiet again; an application's undeclared shadow keeps its info line. A tool whose test was loosened for the v0.93.0 line (o-koe) can restore its exact check.

## v0.93.0 (2026-11-07)

Standard-verbs follow-ups: catalog and setup as facts nouns, edit {field} opens a choice field's chooser, Runtime.DefineListFacts, target-as-key, and fixes from three UX rounds.

- Added (och-facts-from-list): `Runtime.DefineListFacts(specs ...ListFacts)` makes a noun with its own list verb a thing noun over that verb: a read-only, Remote facts store keyed by the row id (with or without the `noun/` prefix), labelled by the row label, its other props the fields of `Sample` (or one `data` object prop without one), with `Rename`, `Docs`, `ListParams` and `Limit`. `get` and the standard list run the verb's work off the loop through `Ctx.Await` and ignore its `Then`. It replaces the `thing_store.go` (`listStore`) each tool copied: `listStore{...}.define(r)` becomes `r.DefineListFacts(ochazuke.ListFacts{...})`.
- Changed: `ochazuke.catalog` and `ochazuke.setup` are thing nouns with a facts store (`Store.Facts`), so no tool's nouncheck baseline holds `command-core-verb ochazuke.catalog list` or `ochazuke.setup list` any more (remove the stale row: `NOUNCHECK_UPDATE=1`). A catalog thing is a noun of the catalog (`name`, `about`, `kind`, `verbs`): `catalog get ochazuke.register`; a setup thing is a declared item with its state (`id`, `name`, `description`, `kind`, `group`, `path`, `state`, `reason`, `next`, `opt_in`): `setup get rules`. `create`, `set`, `delete`, `archive` and `edit` are on both, unavailable (a system fact). Every existing verb is kept as it was: `catalog list` and `describe` (the agent discovery search) and `setup list`, `status`, `show`, `plan`, `install`, `uninstall`, `copy` are the application verbs under those names and answer as before.
- Fixed: a request target that names no instance names a thing by its key (or human key) on a verb that takes its noun's thing by key, when the request gives no key: `register set --target a text=x` (and MCP `run` with `target: "a"`) writes register a whether or not it exists yet, as `--name a` does, instead of failing with `unknown target "a"`; `get`, `set`, `edit`, `delete` and `archive` read it the same way, and the history records the key. An instance id, or a target the application's `ResolveTarget` maps, is still that instance; a key argument beside such a target still refuses it.
- Fixed: an alias named like a prop of its noun may share the prop's name when it presets the prop's own set in either form, `{prop: read, value: true}` or `{props: {read: true}}` (`propSetAlias` read only the first); a props object that writes another prop, or more than that one, still clashes.
- Changed (och-one-field-chooser): `edit {field}` on a field that takes a choice (an enum, a choice source, a noun reference, a boolean) opens that field's chooser in place of the one-field form. The pick is the write (through `set`, on the subject and any selection or marks, one undo step) and closes the chooser, the focus back on the row; picking the value it holds writes nothing; `esc` closes it with nothing written. Works on catalog-mounted nouns. New setting `edit.field-chooser` (default on; off, or `edit.field-only` off, keeps the form) and a new `chooser` argument on edit for a key row to choose per key (`{"field": "@owner", "chooser": false}` keeps the form). The chooser's kind is `EditChooserKind` (`edit-choose`). Free text fields keep their form. Apps: `m` bound to `edit {field: "@owner"}` now opens the owner chooser directly.
- Fixed (UX R1 F1): the edit chooser over a visual selection or marks writes the pick to every other selected row when the focused row already holds it; only the row that holds it is skipped. Stories `verbs/edit-field-chooser-visual-same-value`, `verbs/edit-field-chooser-marked-same-value`.
- Fixed (UX R1 F2): an edit over several rows (the chooser or the form) no longer stops when the focused row refuses its write: that row is counted with the others, the rest are written, one notice says `parcel.edit: 1 done, 1 failed (t1: ...)`, history.undo takes back what applied in one step, and the edit fails only when no row took it.
- Fixed (UX R1 F3): the edit chooser opens with the value the thing holds highlighted, as the form's choice list does, so a reflexive enter writes nothing new. New `ChooserSpec.Start`: the row the highlight starts on while the query is empty. Story `verbs/edit-field-chooser-opens-on-current`.
- Fixed (UX R1 F4): on a noun whose get answers later (a mounted noun), edit {field}'s chooser opens at once in its loading state, so the keys typed before the get lands reach its query and never the page's list; the get's answer then highlights the current value and titles it. The one-field form still opens when the get lands.
- Fixed (UX R1 F7): `edit.field-chooser`'s description and this file no longer claim a path field: no prop type is a path, so edit's chooser has no path branch.
- Fixed (UX R1 F9): a confirmation never asks about a thing that does not exist. A request naming its noun's thing by key (`task delete --target typo`, `--id typo`) on a store that reads things where it runs fails with the store's answer (`unknown task "typo"`) before any prompt; the read is shared with the prompt's label, so the store is not asked twice. A remote store is not probed.
- Fixed (UX R2 N1): edit {field}'s chooser opened before the thing's get lands says so in its title (`· loading current value…`) and no longer writes the top row on a reflexive enter: an enter with nothing typed and the highlight not moved is held until the get lands and then takes the value the thing holds (no write unless other selected rows differ). A typed query or a moved highlight is a choice and writes at once. New `ChooserSpec.Hold` and `Chooser.Release`: hold such an enter while a value the highlight starts on is loading.
- Fixed (UX R2 N2): when edit {field}'s get fails after the chooser picked or closed, the failure is no longer the verb's result; the pick's write is what is shown. A get that fails while the chooser is still open closes it and is reported as before.
- Fixed (UX R2 N3): the edit form over a selection marks its field `not written: <reason>` when the focused row refuses the write, as over one row, while the other rows are written and counted in the notice. form.undo over a selection puts each row back to its own earlier value (as history.undo does) instead of writing the focused row's to every row, and never tries a row that refused; form.redo writes again only the rows the undo reached. A row whose earlier value its store did not record is left as it is, with a notice. New `RecordWrite.Walk` (-1 form.undo, +1 form.redo, 0 an edit).
- Fixed (UX R2 N6): a confirmation on a verb that takes its noun's thing never asks when the request has no thing to act for: `task delete` with no target, or a target that is not a task (`--target app`), fails first with the verb's own error (`id is required: no task is focused`) instead of asking `Delete task app?`.
- Fixed (UX R2 N4): palette argument completion never waits on a choices source that answers later. A parameter whose source verb returns `Pending` (every list-facts noun's list, a mounted noun) loaded synchronously once per key, so `:widget get xy` over a 3 s list echoed each key seconds late; the source now runs through `ExecuteAt` as before but its work launches as one job of the palette (a newer one replaces it, a stale answer is dropped), its answer is kept for the line until the palette opens again, the list shows `loading widgets…` meanwhile, and a word typed before it lands is taken as typed. New internal `Runtime.startChoices`.
- Fixed (UX R2 N5): a parameter whose choices come from a list verb offers its rows. `ParseChoices` reads `[]ListItem` (a row whose `Data` is a plain string names that thing, else its id) and any slice whose items encode as choice maps (a `Thing`, an application's row struct, `*ListItem`), so a field whose prop refers to a list-facts noun lists its things; a failed source's chooser says why in the one format, named by the noun (`no widgets: widget backend down`), even with the fixed `(none)` row under it (a chooser draws its empty line while it has only fixed rows). A source answer that failed to parse before (the check skipped) is now checked: `command run nope` says `name must be one of greet, echo`.
- Fixed (UX R2 N7): the palette reads a boolean given by name: `task edit field=status chooser=false` and `app quit force=true` (a bare `NAME=VALUE` naming a parameter of the verb, as the line spells a request back, like `--NAME=VALUE`); a positional word fills a boolean only when it is `true` or `false`, any other word passing the boolean over to the next parameter instead of failing with `chooser must be one of true, false`.
- Fixed (UX R3 F1): an enter held in a chooser (for a load, or for edit {field}'s get) is dropped when the highlight moves, as typing already dropped it, so `enter down` before the get lands no longer writes the moved-to row when the get lands; the next enter takes the row.
- Fixed (UX R3 F2): the edit form over a selection whose focused row refuses the write no longer fails the save when other rows took it: the field keeps its `not written: <reason>` mark, the `parcel.edit: N done, 1 failed (...)` notice stays on the status line, and the form shows no `! not saved` line. The save fails only when no row applied.
- Fixed (UX R3 F3): edit's bare words are its key, `field` and `chooser`, in that order (edit declares `Positional`); a prop is given only as `prop=value` or `--prop`, so `task edit t2 hello` reads `field=hello` (refused: no such prop) instead of writing `dir=hello`, `task edit t2 status false` and `task edit status false` read field and chooser in the palette and the CLI, and a word past them is refused. New `Parameter.Named` (the catalog's `named`): a parameter no bare word fills; the palette and the CLI pass over it, and the palette spells it back as `NAME=VALUE`.
- Changed: the form's choice and suggestion fields and edit's chooser share one chooser opener (`openChoiceChooser`) and one field-kind test (`editKindOf`); the form's behaviour is unchanged.

## v0.92.1 (2026-11-06)

Fixes from mtm's R1 UX review.

- Fixed: `edit` over a visual selection or marks no longer writes one typed name to every row. The noun's human key (`PropSpec.Human`, else the label) names one thing, so edit's writes go to the focused row alone for it: `edit {field: "@label"}` (`V j r`) opens on the focused row and says "left N as they were: PROP names one row"; in the full form the other fields still reach every row. Other fields (`edit {field: "@owner"}`) fan out as before, one undo step, and now post "NOUN.edit: done on N rows" (a failure on another row is counted, as a per-row run's is). Story `verbs/rename-visual-edits-focused-row`; `verbs/move-visual-writes-every-row` checks the notice.
- Added: `Operation.Summary` names the field of a verb's answer that holds the one line a person reads. From the palette or the help's run that line goes to the status line instead of the answer's JSON; programs still get the whole answer. `history.undo` declares it: `:history undo` shows "undid task.set on Groceries" (each thing by the label its reverted change holds, else its key), no longer a notice of `{"things":[...],"undone":13,...}`.
- Fixed: `--format table` (and markdown, toon) prints the rows of a list answer that crossed JSON: `AnswerRows` unwraps a page object of `items`, `page`, `per_page` and `total` given as a map, a `Thing` or raw JSON bytes (a mounted host's or a daemon's answer), as well as a `ListAnswer`. An object that is not a page (a thing with a prop named items) is left as it is.
- Fixed: in the palette a word that names a thing exactly (its key, or a human key one thing holds) wins over a prefix of other keys, whether or not the thing is listed: `task set t1 ...` on an archived t1 beside live t10, t11, t12 is t1, not "ambiguous: t10, t11, t12" (and a live t1 never reads as another key). A remote store is not probed while a line is read.
- Fixed: a palette line that the removed list answers (a removed noun.verb) is that answer for every reader of the line: the parameter heading and completions no longer describe a misread of its words (`note associate` read as another noun's verb `note`).
- Fixed: a mounted catalog alias named like a core verb the host generates for its noun (o-questions `review.archive` for `question.archive` once review has a key) is dropped without a warning when it would run that same core verb; an alias over a core name that runs another verb, or carries presets, still warns.
- Tests: `Runtime.Execute` refuses an argument the operation does not declare, naming it, on every core verb and on `register set` (o-git's old `{name, text}` fails with `unknown argument "text"`; the silence was the caller discarding the error).

## v0.92.0 (2026-11-06)

R1, DESIGN-001 standard verbs. Every thing noun (a noun with a store) has the seven core verbs create, get, set, delete, archive, list and edit; rename, move, restore, duplicate and patch are removed with no aliases (use `edit {field: "@label"}`, `edit {field: "@owner"}`, `set {props: {"@archive": false}}`, `create {from}`, `set`); command verbs that used a core name are renamed with no aliases; `list(noun, opts)` filters, sorts, groups and pages and answers `{items, page, per_page, total}`; mounted extension nouns get proxy stores that run as jobs; copy stores and `refresh`; store-driven lists. The removed list (`removed_verbs.json`) names every replacement, and `bindings check` reports user rows that still use a removed name.


- Added (DESIGN-001 malleable copies, och-copy-store): `CopyStore(source Store, local LocalStore) Store`, a store of copies kept by the application (`LocalStore`: get, put, list, delete of `Thing`; `NewMemoryLocal` for tests and demos). The first read of a thing (get, or a list that meets it) copies it in once and stamps its own `created`/`updated`; after that the copy is the thing. `set`, `create` and `archive` write the copy alone; a prop declared `PropSpec.Through` is also sent to the source's `Patch` (before the copy's write; a source that refuses it keeps the copy's write and says so). `delete` removes the copy and keeps a tombstone (`CopyTombstone`) so the next list does not copy it in again; setting `copies.delete` (`local`, default, or `source`) deletes at the source too. `Store.CopySource` gives a noun's explicit source verbs the source. A copy over a remote source is remote, and its plan runs make the same calls whatever earlier runs wrote. A copy noun gets `refresh {key?}`: it reads the source (a job for a remote one), opens a modal with a required `local`/`source` pick per differing prop (each showing what the copy holds, local at first) under the focused pick's plain diff (`- local`, `+ source`); a pick writes through `set`, so `form.undo` and `history.undo` both put the copy's value back. A list row whose copy differs from what the last refresh read ends with `≠` (`copies.marks`, default on). `Runtime.SetDiffRenderer` draws plain diffs; `diffview.Register` sets `diffview.RenderText`. o-story stores take `copy`, story `copies/refresh-diff`.
- Added (och-list-from-store): `AddList(id, nil, From(noun))` makes a list the view of the noun's store: it reads the things through the store's `Query`/`List` (every thing, archived ones included, hidden or shown by `list.hide-archived` as any list), shows one row each (`LIST/KEY`, the label prop as label, the thing as `Data`, `Cells` from `Noun.Columns`, which a From list now applies on its own unless `Columns(...)` is given) and fills again after any change of that noun: a request of the change kind, a job of one, `Runtime.Changed(noun)` and a store's `Watch` events, through one shared watch per noun that ends with its last list. The selection and marks follow row ids. New: `List.From`, `List.Refill`. Row-fed lists (`SetItems`, `Patch`) are unchanged. No setting: only a list built with `From` behaves so. o-story pages get `lists[].from`; new story `lists/store-driven-refills`.
- Changed (DESIGN-001, och-list-query): `list(noun, opts)` is the one query. `ListQuery` is now `{Filters []PropFilter, Sort []SortKey, GroupBy []string, GroupSort []SortKey, Page, PerPage int, Archived bool}` plus `Text`, `Saved` (a widget's text query and saved-filter name) and `Args` (the store's own `ListParams`, passed through: a store needs them to read by project, say); `Where` and `Limit` are gone (a where is equality `Filters`, a limit the first page of that size), `Runtime.archiveHiding` takes the query (`Archived` folds in what its `explicit` flag did). The standard `list` verb takes `--filter` (words `prop=value`, `prop!=value`, `prop~value`, `prop!~value`, `a|b` alternatives, or a saved filter's name), `--sort prop[:desc]`, `--group-by`, `--group-sort`, `--page`, `--per-page`, `--archived`; filter is not its own verb. Its answer is `ListAnswer{items, page, per_page, total}` with `prev` and `next` page numbers when paged (and each row a `group` when grouped) instead of a bare array; `AnswerRows` unwraps it for callers that want rows (the palette, `--format table`, choices); `cli.DecodeRows` reads either shape. A page counts rows, never groups.
- Added: `Store.Query`, an optional capability that runs the whole query and answers `ListPage{Items, Total, Groups}`. Any other store is run by one generic in-memory `queryAll` over `Store.List` (filter, sort, group, page); its `List` is handed the conditions and `Args` only.
- Changed (F9): a `List`'s query text, prop conditions, sort and saved-filter name are one `ListQuery` value (`List.ListView()`), and the widget sorts, groups and pages through the same functions the verb runs (`orderRows`, `arrangeRows`). New: `List.Page`, `SetPage`, `SetGroupBy`, verbs `list.page`, `page-next`, `page-prev`, `page-first`, `page-last`, `group`, `group-clear` (no default keys), a group heading line over each group in plain lists (tables use their section labels), and a `page N/M` indicator in the list's filter line. Settings: `list.per-page` (default 0: one page of every row, as before; a number pages the lists a widget draws) and `list.group-by` (default empty: no groups). With the defaults every frame is unchanged. A tree and a chooser's rows are neither grouped nor paged.
- Changed (DESIGN-001, och-command-nouns): no command noun has a verb named like a core verb any more. The colliding command verbs are renamed, and, by the owner's decision, the old names are purged with no alias: each is a row of `removed_verbs.json` with its replacement, so the verb check fails on a reference and `bindings check` / `actions check` tell a user's `bindings.json` and `actions.json` what to write. `ochazuke focus set {id}` is `ochazuke.focus.to {id}`; `ochazuke mode set {mode}` is `ochazuke.mode.switch {mode}`; `ochazuke palette set {text}` is `ochazuke.palette.fill {text}`; `ochazuke confirm set {operation, prompt}` is `ochazuke.confirm.require {operation, prompt}` (it sets an operation's prompt, so not the plan's `confirm.answer`: `confirm.accept` answers); `ochazuke input delete` is `ochazuke.input.erase`; `ochazuke markdown delete` is `ochazuke.markdown.cut` (it removes the block into a register). The framework's binding files keep every key with the new verb names.
- Changed: the plural nouns merge into the singular thing nouns, with no noun alias (owner decision): `ochazuke pages list` is `ochazuke.page.list` (the standard list: rows with `id`, `label`, `title`, `hidden`, `active`); every `ochazuke.settings` verb is on `ochazuke.setting`: `apply` (`:set`), `all`, `close`, `complete`, `diff`, `names`, `owners`, `path`, `reload`, `reset-all`, `review`, `sections`, `undo` keep their names; `settings edit` is `setting.open` (the Settings page; `:options` and `:opt` run it), `settings write` is `setting.write-all` (alias `setting.save-all`, `mksettings`), `settings revert` is `setting.revert-all`, `settings section` is `setting.show-section` (a prop is named `section`), `settings tab` is `setting.show-tab`, and `settings get`, `settings list`, `settings set` and `settings reset` are the setting noun's own `get`, `list`, `set {name, value}` and `reset {name, scope: file}`. `settings set` wrote settings.json; `setting set` writes the session by default in a running app, so a caller that relied on the file write passes `scope: file` (the removed row says so; the demo's theme and density cycles do). Removed rows cover both spellings (`settings set`, `ochazuke settings set`). `:set all` answers the standard list's rows. The value verbs of a setting (`set`, `append`, ...) keep their value in the history file (`PersistArgs`), as the plural `set` did.
- Added: `Runtime.RemovedWords(words)`: a command line whose leading words name a removed verb (NOUN VERB, NOUN.VERB, or a two-word noun: `ochazuke settings list`) that nothing answers says what to write instead before the words resolve, so a merged plural noun or a renamed command verb names its replacement in the palette (`:settings edit`, `:jobs list`, `:focus set ID`) and the CLI (`bin settings list`) instead of failing as an unknown or ambiguous word. An extension's peer row naming one says so too, and `bindings check` reports a removed row once (its note), not again as a skipped row.
- Changed: `register`, `appearance`, `state`, `job` and `history` are thing nouns. `ochazuke.register` is a collection keyed by `name` (`text`, `shape`, `width`, read-only `keys`): `register set a text=shared` (an uppercase name is the same register, `_` discards; `shape` is char, line or block), undone by `history.undo`; `register.append {name, text}` adds to one, as an uppercase yank register does; `register get`/`list` answer props. `ochazuke.appearance` is a singleton (`theme`, `density`, read-only `dark`): `appearance set theme dark`, or both at once with `props`; a write that fails changes nothing, and `history.undo` restores the old values (so `history.undo` is offered in every application: the framework's register and appearance stores undo). `ochazuke.state` is a read-only singleton (a system fact) whose props are the snapshot's fields. `ochazuke jobs` is `ochazuke.job` (a system fact keyed by `job`; `job.cancel {job}`): finished jobs are kept after they end, up to 32, instead of being dropped by the first read (Q2). `ochazuke.history` is a system fact keyed by `seq` whose `list` is the standard one with `last`, `actor` and `kind` as its own parameters (`history get {seq}` reads one entry). Answers are props, not the old Go values (`Appearance`, `[]JobInfo`, `[]HistoryEntry`, `Register`).
- Added: `get {prop}` on every thing noun takes a dotted path into an object or list prop (`state get instances.editor.noun`, `state get lists.side.items.0.id`): at an object the longest run of segments that is a key wins, so a key holding dots is reached (`nouns.ochazuke.list.verbs`); a number indexes a list. `state.get {path}` (a JSON Pointer) is gone: write `state get {prop: "a.b.c"}`.
- Changed: `ochazuke.outline` and `ochazuke.setting-item` are thing nouns (their rows: `id`, `label`/`text`, ...), so `get` and `list` read them; their own `delete` and `edit` stay the core verbs (they act through the tree's undo and children rule and the Settings page's prompt), so `d d` and `i` behave as before.
- Changed: `ValidateNouns` is a hard failure for ochazuke: `testdata/command-noun-collisions.txt` is gone and `TestNounsAreThingsOrCommands` runs with no baseline.
- Fixed: `Model.Init` reads `settings.json` again when it changed since a setting was read while the runtime was being built (a store's verbs ask `forms.edit` at registration), so a file written before the Model starts is never missed.
- Added (och-registry-things): modes, operations, nouns, themes, glyphs, hooks, events, actions, bindings, association, notice and segment are thing nouns with read-only facts stores (`Store.Facts`) over the registry. Their `list` is the standard `list` (rows with `id` and `label`, `where`, `props`, `limit`; the old extra arguments `event`, `all` and `noun` stay as list parameters) and `get` reads one by its key. Names are unchanged. create, set, delete and archive are on these nouns but unavailable with the reason "a system fact" (the core table's `needWriter`: a store that is not `Store.Facts`). Row shapes changed: `modes`, `themes` and `notice` list rows (not strings), `glyphs` keys by `name` (was `set`), `nouns` counts `seen` (was `places`), `bindings` carries `helptext` (was `help`, a verb of that noun), and `hooks`, `bindings`, `association`, `notice` rows have an `id`; `Runtime.AllBindings` is exported for hosts that read every stored row.
- Changed: choices come from the noun's list verb through the standard verb table (`standardOperation`), not the literal `NOUN.list`; an application's own `list` still wins.
- Changed: every thing noun whose store writes (Create or Patch) or declares `Stamps` has the read-only `created` and `updated` props, and create and set stamp them unless the store declares `Stamps`; a facts store has none. `Store.Times` is gone (the base times are on by default). The framework's stamped times never make a history write unsimple.
- Changed (DESIGN-001, och-core-verb-table): one core verb table (`core_verbs.go`) holds `create`, `get`, `set`, `delete`, `archive`, `list` and `edit`, and every thing noun (a noun with a `Store`) has all seven, always. Where the store lacks what a verb needs (a handler, a writable prop, `forms.edit`), the verb is listed but unavailable with the reason: `Operation.Unavailable`, the catalog's `unavailable` (and `disabled`), the reference's per-noun verbs; running it fails with the reason, and the palette, hints and help leave it out as before. CLI help keeps unavailable verbs out of a noun's summary; `NOUN VERB --help` says why. Rows are data: capability fields (`TakesSubject`, `Many`, `Rows`, `Record`, `Creates`, `WritesProps`, `Form`), the undo shape (`Undo`: create is undone by delete, set and archive restore, delete re-creates), `Aliases` and `ParamNames`. No code compares a standard verb's name any more (namecheck baseline: 23 rows fewer); `reservedStandardVerbs`, `standardParamNames`, `standardNeeds`, `standardAliases` and the two `init` mutations are gone. `CoreVerbs()` lists the seven; `StandardVerbs()` lists the core, then the legacy rows.
- Changed: `watch`, `rename`, `move`, `duplicate` and `restore` are rows of a second table, `legacyVerbs`, offered only where the store meets them, as before (the purge lane `och-purge-verbs` removes all but `watch`). `duplicate` is now `Many` and records its change as a new thing, so its undo deletes the copy (G1, G7).
- Changed (G16): a visual selection fans a standard verb out only when its row takes a subject and many (`set`, `delete`, `archive`, `move`, `duplicate`, `restore`). `create`, `get`, `list`, `edit`, `rename` and `watch` run once, on the focus, and answer as themselves instead of a `list.apply` report.
- Added: `Runtime.ValidateNouns()` (`NounProblem`: a thing noun without a core verb or a key prop, a command noun with a verb named like a core verb) and package `nouncheck` (`Check`, `CheckRows`, `Rows`) to hold an app's bindings test to it with a shrink-only baseline (`NOUNCHECK_UPDATE=1` removes rows). ochazuke's own baseline, `testdata/command-noun-collisions.txt`, lists the 35 command-noun collisions `och-registry-things` and `och-command-nouns` remove.
- Changed (G10): the standard verbs' default keys are data in `bindings/standard.json`: `i` (`ochazuke.nouns.edit`) and visual-line `d` (`list.apply {verb: delete}`), moved from `app.json` and `list-modes.json`. Same keys, tables, guards and hints; in the generated bindings reference `i` now lists among the app noun's NewRuntime rows and `d` before `esc` in visual-line.
- Removed (DESIGN-001, och-purge-verbs; owner decision: purged with no aliases, overriding the one-release alias of "Keep actions"): the standard verbs `rename`, `move`, `duplicate` and `restore`, and set's alias `patch`. A user's `bindings.json` or `actions.json` row naming one stops working; `bindings check` and the verb check name each with its replacement (`removed_verbs.json`): `rename` → `edit {field: "@label"}` (or `set {props: {"@label": value}}`), `move` → `edit {field: "@owner"}` (or `set {props: {"@owner": id}}`), `restore` → `set {props: {"@archive": false}}`, `duplicate` → `create {from: ID}`, `patch` → `set`. A noun's own verb of one of these names (a motion named move, pane's restore, an app's own rename) is unchanged. `rename`, `move`, `duplicate`, `restore` and `patch` are no longer reserved names: a prop may take one again.
- Changed: `set {target, props: {...}}` is set's one shape: `props` is an object of prop to value, a subset or all of them, and `@label`, `@owner` and `@archive` name a prop by its role. The palette's and CLI's `prop=value` words fill it; `toggle` with `prop` flips one boolean. The old parameters `prop` with `value`, `changes` and `pairs` still work for one release: `removed_verbs.json` lists them as deprecated parameters (rows with `param`, and `with`), the verb check reports a JSON or Go line that gives one (state `deprecated`, not a failure; ochazuke's own test fails on one outside tests), and `bindings check` warns of a row or alias that uses one, naming `props`. A prop-shaped request (`{noun, prop, value}`) now runs as `set {props: {prop: value}}`, the standard edit form writes `props`, and `page.hide`/`page.show` preset `props`.
- Added: `edit {target, field?}`. `field` is a prop name or `@role`; with the new setting `edit.field-only` (default on) the form holds only that field, opened as a rename was (insert, its value selected under `rename.select`, the title `Edit NOUN PROP: LABEL`); off, the full form opens with that field focused. Every change writes through `set`. An empty prop with a `Fallback` shows the fallback's value as its placeholder in any edit form.
- Added: `create {from}` copies a thing: `from` is an id, a human key, or a row's Data holding the key (a binding row's `"from": {"from": "row"}`); every prop create writes is read from it, the props given replace their values, the store gives the new key, and undo deletes the copy. New `Parameter.Covers`: a parameter that, given, stands in for the verb's required ones (from), so a copy never opens the args form for them.
- Changed (UX round 1 on the purge): one edit form's writes (every debounced save, every subject) are one history group (`HistoryEntry.Group`, `newHistoryGroup`/`inHistoryGroup`): `history.undo` takes the form back in one step. In a form of one shown field that writes each change, enter writes and closes it (guard `form-one-field`, a row of `bindings/form.json`). `edit` is `Many`: `m` over a visual selection (or `list.apply {verb: edit, args: {field: "@owner"}}` over marks) opens the one-field form once and writes the pick to every row, one undo step. `edit {field}` reaches the one-field form on a noun whose application registers its own `edit` (the row's `FieldParam` is adopted, `adoptFieldParam`) and with `forms.edit` off (`storeNeed.WaivedBy`: the setting turns off only the full form). A request or a bindings row naming a removed verb says what to write instead (`parcel rename was removed in R1: use edit {field: "@label"}`), from the palette, a program and a user's `bindings.json`; a request addressing a missing role says which role, not "not applicable". Story stores take `undo`.
- Changed: a request addressing a role the noun has no writable prop for (`set {props: {"@owner": x}}`, `edit {field: "@label"}` on a read-only label) is unavailable there, as the purged verb was, so a key bound to it is not offered; a prop named by its name that is missing or read-only stays an error. The standard verb table's `FieldParam` names edit's `field` for that check.
- Changed: the verb check (`namecheck.Verbs`) treats every CHANGELOG section but Unreleased as history (it read only dated or versioned ones); `cmd/verbcheck` prints deprecated parameters. Stories: new `verbs/rename-is-edit-label`, `verbs/rename-is-edit-label-full-form`, `verbs/unarchive-is-set`, `verbs/create-from-copies`; `palette/rename-chooser`, `palette/move-chooser`, `palette/archive-list`, `focus/association-standard-subject`, `focus/modal-return-focus-on-close` and `terminal/leader-association-rename-return` now bind or type the role-addressed requests; the palette form stories' own verbs are `demo.retitle` and `demo.relocate`.

- Changed: `cli.MountNouns` gives a mounted noun that has catalog props a proxy store (`cli/proxy_store.go`) whose get, list, create, set (Patch) and delete call the extension's own verbs through `HostMount.Proxy`. The standard verbs run in the host over it as for a local noun: undo (a mounted `set` or `edit` records its inverse), confirm, the edit form, history and targets by id or human key. The store is `Store.Remote`: a standard verb over it plans on the loop, makes each store call off the loop as one job (queued per thing by the editor outbox, cancellable from the jobs list, never blocking quit; `HostMount.CallTimeout` bounds a call, default 30s) and records the change and its undo entry when the calls are answered, so mounted writes and reads stay jobs. A call the extension lacks is `Store.Missing[verb]`: the core verb is present but unavailable with that reason. Removes the edit-handler swap in `host_mount.go`, the invented history change for a store-less proxy write and the store re-check in the standard verb handler. New: `Store.Remote`, `Store.Missing`, `Ctx.Await`, `Result.Await`, `Result.Continue` (a job that runs in steps under one ticket), `Result.Map` (transform a result's final value; code that wraps a handler's result uses it, not `Result.work`), `Result.Launched`, `Runtime.StoreServes`. A mounted noun without props, or of live instances, mounts its verbs as proxies as before. `cli.Settle` reports a job's value only at its last step. Consumers (mtm, nabe, o-* tools) pick it up with the next tag; no code change.

## v0.89.0 (2026-11-04)

- Added chrome roles for active/inactive tabs and overflow counts, header/footer rules, error status and running/done/failed job entries. `chrome.color=rich` (default) fills the active tab, makes rules quiet without faint, and colours jobs and errors; `subtle` colours only job state words. `off` restores the original rendering byte for byte. Keys, focus, modes, selection, geometry and timers are unchanged.
- Added `TickerEntry`, `TickerEntries`, `Model.TickerEntries`, `Footer.JobEntries` and `Layout.TickerEntriesRule`; existing string ticker APIs remain unchanged.
