- y, Y, V and the width keys are hidden from the footer; `?` help lists them.
- cli: the groups rail's `h`/`left` back rows take the new guard `list-first-column`, so in a table `h` moves left and goes back to the rail from the first column. Story `catalog/groups-table-h`.

### grid

- Per-column widths: `z >`/`z <`/`z =` on the grid noun (`grid.resize-column`, `grid.reset-columns`; `Grid.SetColumnWidth`, `ColumnWidth`, `ColumnWidths`, `ResetColumnWidths`). A given width replaces `MaxCellWidth` for its column and is remembered under the grid's id.

### o-story

- Goldens read `C|` (`c|` dimmed) for a highlighted row that holds a table's current cell; the expect field `cell` names the current cell's text.

## v0.11.0 (2026-10-26)

The terminal leader can wait for the next key indefinitely.

- `terminal-leader-timeout` (`TerminalLeaderTimeoutSetting`, duration): how long `leader_pending` waits for the next key before it sends the leader alone. `0` never expires, so the leader waits like tmux's prefix; a positive value is the leader's own timeout. `EnsureTerminalLeader` declares it beside `terminal-leader` unless the app declared it first (with its own default); the framework default is `DefaultBindingTimeout`, and until settings.json holds a value the leader follows `BindingTimeout`. Other key sequences keep `BindingTimeout`. Story `terminal/leader-timeout-zero-waits`.

## v0.10.0 (2026-10-26)

An unfocused list keeps its selected row, faded; the dims are settings. A list can clear its filter when enter picks a row.

### Unfocused lists and dims

- A page list without the focus keeps its selected row in reverse video and is drawn faded as a whole, so the row an unfocused list is on (a tree beside a live terminal) stays visible and the focused row is the one highlight at full strength. A list in a modal stays plain until focused; a tree editing a line is not faded. Replaces the old rule "an unfocused list draws every row plain" (UX rule R5.1). Stories `lists/unfocused-dimmed`, `lists/beside-terminal-keeps-row`.
- Two framework settings, declared by `EnsureConfigSettings`: `dim-unfocused` (percent an unfocused list fades toward the background, default 20) and `modal-dim` (the page behind a modal, default 35, the backdrop's look until now). Both 0 to 90 (`MaxDim`); 0 is off.
- `Styles.Fade(percent)` blends the scheme's text colour toward its surface (`lipgloss.Blend1D`, perceptual); in mono or NO_COLOR any fade is the faint attribute. `Styles.WithDims(modal, unfocused)` sets the new `Styles.Backdrop` and `Styles.Unfocused`; `Model.Styles()` applies the settings. `NewStyles` uses the defaults.
- o-story: `highlighted` counts full-strength highlights only; the new `dimmed` counts highlights under faint; goldens mark a dimmed-only row `r|`.

### Lists

- `ClearFilterOnEnter()` (an `InstanceOption` for `AddList` and `AddTree`): enter in the list's filter line chooses the selected match (`list.filter-choose`, guard `list-chooses`): the filter clears, the selection stays on that row and on screen, the mode is normal. With no match enter keeps the filter, as on every list; other lists are unchanged. Story `lists/filter-enter-clears`.

### Fixes

- Custom components' explicit `Block.Cursor` now reaches `Model.View().Cursor` without a focused input, using the normal block or visual underline shape. Blocks without a cursor remain hidden; input and terminal cursor shapes are unchanged.

## v0.9.0 (2026-10-26)

### List motions

- Every list answers Vim's paging motions in normal mode: `g g` and `G` go to the first and last row, and with a count to that row (`5G`, `5gg`, `list.goto {position, row}`); `ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u` move half a page (a count is rows) and `ctrl+f`/`ctrl+b` a page (a count is pages) (`list.scroll {delta, half, steps}`). A page is the list's own window, else the page body's height; the window moves with the selection. They clamp at the list's edges, never spill, and are motions (off the footer, under `movement` in `?`). A row noun that binds the same key keeps it. Story `lists/page-motions`.
- A table with more columns than fit scrolls them (`list.scroll-columns {to, delta, steps}`, `List.ColumnOffset`): `$` brings the last column into view, `^` (and `0` without a count) goes back, `z l`/`z h` move one column. The first column stays; the unscrolled view still drops columns by `Priority`. The keys answer only on a table that hides a column or is scrolled. Story `tables/column-motions`.
- A key-sequence prefix whose only remaining row is its generated `<prefix> esc` cancel (every sequence on it guarded off) no longer waits for a second key.

## v0.8.0 (2026-10-26)

The Settings page is redesigned after tuios's: a list of fields, not a table.

A state verb answers what changed since the host's last poll instead of every row, and the catalog tells a tool's own verbs from the framework's. Every list filters with `/`, and an Update no longer visits list rows.

### Settings page

- Sections are tabs across the top instead of a rail, so the rows get the whole width. `tab` and `]` show the next section, `shift+tab` and `[` the previous, wrapping (`settings.section {step}`, or `{name}`); each section keeps its selected row. With one section there is no tab row.
- One row per setting: its name on the left, its value on the right. There is no header and no Value/Default/Source columns. A `•` after the name marks a value that is not the default. `‹ value ›` marks a value `h`/`l` steps.
- The description under the rows says what the default is and where the value comes from: not in settings.json, set in settings.json, or settings.json holds an invalid value (with the reason) so the default applies.
- List settings: a list setting's row shows its item count (`(none)` when empty), and each item is a row directly beneath it with the name column blank. On a list's row `enter` or `n` adds an item at the end. On an item row `enter` or `i` edits it, `n` adds one after it, `d d` deletes it and `r` resets the whole list. An empty item is refused.
- `u` (or `ctrl+z`) takes back the last change made on the page and shows its row. A value that was invalid in settings.json comes back as it was. `u` is offered only when there is a change to take back.
- `backspace` and `delete` reset a setting, as `r` does.
- The page reopens on the section and row it was left on.

### State cache and deltas

- A state verb run through `cli.Shell` takes `--since LIST=HASH,...` and `--since-cache ID` (`cli.StateFlags`, `FormatSince`/`ParseSince`; `--since=` asks for every list in full, with hashes) and answers JSON Lines: a header `{"state":1,"cache":ID}`, then per list `unchanged`, a `delta` (the changed and added rows whole at their indexes, the removed ids) or the list in `full`, each with its content hash (`RecordHash`, `ListHash`). An unknown hash, another cache id or a pruned version answers full, never wrong. The catalog declares `stateDelta: 1`.
- The extension side caches each list's last versions (`Shell.StateCache`): `(id, record hash)` files under `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/<bin>/state/<scope>/lists/<list>/`, written temp + rename and content-addressed, `Keep` (2) versions per list and `Scopes` (8) scopes, the rest deleted. `StateCache.Scope(args)` adds to the scope (a TUIOS server); `StateCache.Fingerprint(args)` (`StatFingerprint(paths...)`) answers an unchanged poll without running the verb, except under `--fresh`; `StateCache.Off` turns it all off.
- `MountPage` uses it: with `PageConfig.FetchFlags` a page's `Reload` sends its list hashes and applies the answer (`PageView.ApplyState`): unchanged touches nothing and skips `OnRows`, a delta goes through `List.Patch`, full through `SetRows`; a list whose hash does not match after applying is fetched again in full, once. `PageView.Refresh` forgets the hashes. An older extension (no `stateDelta`) keeps full answers through `Fetch`.
- `List.Patch(ListPatch{Upsert, At, Remove})` (and `PatchItems`) is `SetItems` over the patched rows: kept rows keep their instances, selection and marks, and only removed rows close. `SetItems` keeps a table's measured widths when every row draws the same cells.
- `cli.DecodeState` and `RowReader.Answer()` read a delta answer. Benchmarks `BenchmarkStatePoll` and `BenchmarkStateApply` cover an 8,000-row poll, unchanged and with one row changed.

### Builtin verbs

- Every ochazuke tool's catalog lists the framework's verbs beside the tool's, and some are application verbs (the outline's 14, the chooser's 3), so hosts mounted them as the tool's (`ai-outline`, `qbt-outline`). `Operation.Builtin` now marks every operation the framework registers: everything `NewRuntime` and `NewModel` register, and what a framework constructor registers later (`NewPicker`, `NewPalette`, `NewAppearance`, `grid.Register`, `markdown.Register`, `cli.AddCommands`). An application's verb is never builtin, even on a framework noun (`history.capture-hook`).
- The catalog's verbs carry `builtin` (`cli.CatalogVerb.Builtin`). `CatalogDoc.AppNouns()` is what a host mounting a tool's verbs mounts: each noun with only its non-builtin verbs (`CatalogNoun.AppVerbs()`), nouns left with none dropped. A `MountPage` list of a noun's verbs lists only the tool's.

### Every list filters

- `/` on a focused list (page lists, tables, catalog lists, the Settings page's section, a list in a modal) opens a query line above the rows; typing keeps the rows whose label or cells hold every word, ignoring case, in list order, `N of M` on the right. In the `list_filter` mode up/down and `ctrl+n`/`ctrl+p` move among the matches (no spill), enter keeps the filter (`/` edits it, `esc` clears it), esc clears it and selects the row `/` found, backspace on an empty query leaves, `ctrl+u` empties it.
- Rows are hidden, never removed: `SetItems` and `Patch` filter the new rows and keep the selection by id, marks survive, `list.apply` acts on the marked rows shown, focus landing on a hidden row clears the filter.
- An application's own `/` wins at any level: the framework's is an `app` row (guard `list-filterable`), its `/ filter` hint last on the footer.

### Faster updates

- `Runtime.components()` (every `Deliver` and `PendingCommands`) walks an index of the instances that are not list rows instead of sorting every instance, and the list verbs no longer copy every row into their result. `BenchmarkUpdateKey` at 16,000 rows: 818 µs to 11 µs a key; nabe's ai page 295 µs to 32 µs.

### API changes

- `SettingList` (`"list"`): a setting whose value is `[]string`. settings.json keeps it as a JSON array, `settings set NAME '["a","b"]'` sets it, and `Settings.Strings(name)` reads a copy. A value of another shape is refused, as are an `Enum` and a default that is not a list of strings.
- `settings.section {step, name}` and `settings.undo`. The nouns are `setting-item` (`edit`, `add`, `delete`, `reset`) and `setting.add`. The guards are `setting-list` and `settings-undo`.
- A table whose columns have no titles draws no header (`List.View`).
- The page's lists are `__settings/list[/SECTION]` (were `__settings/table[/SECTION]`); the `__settings/sections` rail and the `settings-section` noun are gone. Item rows are `__settings/item/NAME/INDEX`.

- `Operation.Builtin`, `Runtime.Builtin(register func())` (marks the operations `register` added; for framework packages), `cli.CatalogVerb.Builtin`, `CatalogDoc.AppNouns()`, `CatalogNoun.AppVerbs()`.
- `list filter --target LIST --query Q` (empty clears), `list.filter-open|type|erase|keep|clear`, `list.move {clamp}`; `List.SetFilter`, `Filter`, `Filtering`, `Shown`, `Height` (`Len` plus the query line); `ListFilterMode`. `Items`/`Len` stay every row; a filtered list's state has `filter` and `total` and its `items` are the rows shown.
- `cli.StateDeltaVersion`, `StateFlags`, `FormatSince`, `ParseSince`, `StateList`, `RecordHash`, `ListHash`, `StateCache`, `StatFingerprint`, `DecodeState`, `RowReader.Answer()`, `PageConfig.FetchFlags`, `PageView.ApplyState`, `PageView.Refresh`, `PageView.SinceFlags()`; `ochazuke.ListPatch`, `List.Patch`, `PatchItems`.

### Migrating

- Stories or tests that read the old columns (`Setting`, `Value`, `Default`, `Source`) or the rail (`Sections`, `__settings/section…`) read the tabs, the rows and the description instead.
- A host that mounts another tool's catalog verbs (nabe, mtm) mounts `doc.AppNouns()` instead of comparing keys against a fresh `NewRuntime()`+`NewModel()`. A catalog from a tool built before this release has no `builtin`, so every verb reads as the tool's; keep the old check as a fallback until the tools are rebuilt.

## v0.7.0 (2026-10-26)

A Settings page instead of the settings modal, one action history whose entries are small and typed, and two per-key costs that grew with the rows gone: `j` on an 8,000-row list in nabe's ai page went from about 3.7 ms to 2.4 ms a key (the keypress itself from 1.4 ms to 0.3 ms), and `BenchmarkPageFrame` at 8,000 rows from 2.1 ms to 0.6 ms.

### Settings page

- Settings get a page of their own instead of the cramped modal. `settings.edit` opens (or switches to) the framework's Settings page: a rail of sections on the left (the settings without a section first, as General, then each section in declaration order), the section's table on the right (setting, value, default, source: `default`, `settings.json`, or `default (file value invalid)`), and below it a detail of the selected setting (description, type, allowed values; `J`/`K` scroll it, `>`/`<` move the divider). Editing is as before: every change is saved as it is made, `enter` flips or steps or opens the value prompt, `h`/`l`/`space` step, `r` resets. `q` or `esc` (`settings.close`) goes back to the page it was opened from with its focus and mode. The page is registered when it opens and removed when it closes, so it is a tab only while open.

### History

- Every history entry has a `kind`: `action` (the user's interaction: motions, focus, page and modal moves, modes, the palette's own keys), `change` (edits and verbs that change something; dot-repeat reads only these) or `fetch` (state, list and show verbs, reloads, a verb declaring `Rows` or `State`, a host calling an extension). `system` is reserved for what the runtime does on its own; nothing records it yet.
- The kind comes from the operation (`Operation.HistoryKindOf`): `Operation.HistoryKind` when set, else `fetch` for `Rows`/`State`, `change` for `Repeatable`, `action` for `Motion`, `change` for any other application verb (an extension verb that declares nothing is kept with the user's entries), `action` for any other interaction verb. The framework's reads and reloads (`state.get`, `history.list`, `jobs.list`, `bindings.list`, `operations.list`, `settings.list`, `*.reload`, ...) are fetches; its interaction verbs that change a setting, a register or a pane (`settings.set`, `input.undo`, `register.set`, `terminal.send`, ...) are changes.
- `history.list {last, kind}` and `state get /history` show actions and changes by default; `kind` takes a comma list or `all`. `history.list` and `state.get` are now recorded, as fetches.
- Fetches the user did not type into the palette keep their own ring, the last `HistoryFetchLimit` (100), so a host polling a state verb never pushes the user's entries out of the `history-limit` ring. A fetch typed into the palette stays with the user's entries, and palette Up/Down recalls it.
- `history.jsonl` keeps fetches only while the new `history-fetches` setting is on (default off: a poller would fill the file, and nothing reads a fetch back after a restart). Lines from older files get their kind when they are read.
- An entry never holds the value its operation returned: `j` over a list encoded the list's snapshot into the ring on every key (about 1 ms at 8,000 rows). A request that moved the focus, page or mode records `moved {from, to}` (`HistoryMove`, `HistoryPlace`: ids, the page and the mode), so `j` is "row A to row B".
- `PageList` reads app's page order (kept by `RegisterPage` on the app instance, ordered as `Children("app")` is) instead of scanning every instance; it runs about three times a frame. `BenchmarkPageList` is flat in the row count.

### API changes

- `HistoryEntry.Kind`, `HistoryEntry.Moved`; `HistoryMove`, `HistoryPlace`; `HistoryResult.Value` is gone (nothing read it; the file already dropped it). A reference to a response (a hash into a content-addressed store) would go in `HistoryResult` beside `ok`.
- `Operation.HistoryKind`, `Operation.HistoryKindOf()`, `NounBuilder.Fetch()`; constants `HistoryAction`, `HistoryChange`, `HistoryFetch`, `HistorySystem`, `HistoryFetchLimit`, `HistoryFetchesSetting`.
- `Runtime.HistoryOf(n, kinds...)`; `Runtime.History(n)` returns every kind.
- The catalog's verbs carry `history` (`cli.CatalogVerb.History`, the verb's `HistoryKindOf`), and actions.json entries take `history` (`Action.History`: `action`, `change` or `fetch`).
- `Setting.Section` (JSON `section`) files a setting under a section of the Settings page; without it the section is the name before its last dot (`ext.ai.poll-every` is in `ext.ai`), `Setting.SectionName()`.
- `settings.close`; `Runtime.AddSettingsPage()` registers the Settings page as a permanent tab; `SettingsPageID` (`__settings`), `SettingsGeneral`.
- The `settings-editor` modal and its `ModalView` are gone; the value prompt (`setting-prompt`) stays. Row ids are `__settings/row/NAME` (were `__settings/NAME`). The `l`, `right`, `space` and `left` step rows are `Hide`, so the footer shows one `h/l setting.cycle`.

### Migrating

- A host that mounts another CLI's catalog verbs (nabe) should copy each verb's `history` to its operation (`Operation.HistoryKind`, or `NounBuilder.Fetch()` for `fetch`), so the extension's fetches stay out of the user's history there too; without it a mounted verb records as a change.
- An application verb that only reads should call `.Fetch()` (or declare `Rows`/`State`).
- Code that read `HistoryResult.Value` reads the operation's return value instead.
- An application that binds `settings.edit` keeps its key; it now opens the page. Code or stories that expected `TopModal() == "settings-editor"` check `Runtime.Page == SettingsPageID` instead. With no page of its own to go back to, `settings.close` refuses.

## v0.6.1 (2026-10-26)

A patch release: a page with many rows no longer lags on every key.

### Fixed

- Finding the current page's detail (the `detail-scrolls` guard of the `J`/`K` rows, which the footer hints evaluate every frame and dispatch evaluates on every `J`/`K`, and `detail.scroll` without an `id`) walked the page's tree asking `Children` at every node, and `Children` scans every instance: quadratic in the rows. A page of ~8,300 rows (nabe's `ai` page, mounted with `cli.MountPage`) spent ~620 ms a key there, with or without a detail; it now takes ~3.5 ms. A page records the details beneath it (`AddDetail`, and `RegisterPage` for details added before the page), on the `Page` component; the first one depth first still wins, and a removed detail drops out.
- `BenchmarkPageFrame` and `TestPageFrameScalesLinearly` (keypress, hints and frame at 1,000 and 4,000 rows must stay near x4, not x16) guard it.

## v0.6.0 (2026-10-26)

### Highlights

- `config export [DIR]` writes a complete reference of an application's user configuration, by default into `<config dir>/reference/`: `settings.json` (every declared setting at its current value), `settings.reference.json` (type, default, enum, description and value per setting), `bindings.reference.json` (every key row, `bindings list --all`), `actions.example.json` and `themes.example.json` (every field, valid as they are) and a `README.md` explaining each file, where the live files are, and that copying defaults pins them. Files are written atomically; the live config files are never written without `--force`.
- `config path` prints the config, state and default reference directories.
- A binding row's guard is part of its identity: identity is the noun or instance, the mode, the keys or pattern, and `when`. A row with `when: X` replaces only the row with the same keys and guard `X`; a row without `when` replaces only the unguarded one; an unbind (`"request": null`) removes only the row with its own `when`. This holds for `Runtime.Bind`, the builder (`Bind`, `Pattern`, `When`, `Unbind`), `bindings.load`, the user `bindings.json` and `bindings.reload`'s undo, so a guarded row and its fallback on the same keys can be overridden or unbound one at a time, and `config export`'s `bindings.reference.json` loads back exactly. A replaced row keeps its place in the table (it used to move to the end), a new guarded row goes before the unguarded row on its keys so it answers while its guard holds, and reload puts a replaced row back where it stood. The `input` guard the framework puts on the editor's `input.*` rows (`ImplicitGuard`) is not part of the identity: a row without `when` replaces or unbinds those defaults as before, and a user row running an `input.*` operation on the editor noun gets the guard too.
- The user `bindings.json` loader reports a row that a guarded default on its keys still answers before, as a notice (`bindings.json: row N KEYS: the default NOUN.VERB row (when "G") answers first while its guard holds; to replace it, add "when": "G"`): an added row behind a guarded row, any row behind a guarded capture row on its keys (`ctrl+c` and its `no-modal` capture row), and an unbind without `when` that leaves a guarded row in place.

### API changes

- `ModeBuilder.UnbindGuarded(keysOrPattern, guard)` removes the row with that guard; `ModeBuilder.Unbind` now removes only the unguarded row. `ModeBuilder.When` after `Bind` re-places the row under its guard (an unguarded row it replaced comes back).
- `Runtime.ExportConfig(dir, force) (ConfigExport, error)`, `ConfigExport{Dir, Files, Config, State}`; `Config.Paths() ConfigPaths`, `ConfigPaths{Config, State, Reference}`.
- The `config` noun: `config.export {dir, force}` (`dir` positional), `config.path`.
- Constants `ReferenceDir`, `ReferenceReadme`, `SettingsReferenceFile`, `BindingsReferenceFile`, `ActionsExampleFile`, `ThemesExampleFile`.

### Migrating

- An unguarded row no longer replaces or unbinds a guarded row with the same keys; it adds an unguarded row beside it (which answers only where the guarded row's guard is false), and the loader says so in a notice. To replace or unbind a guarded default, give the row the default's `when` (`bindings list --all` and `bindings.reference.json` show it). The editor's implicit `input` guard is the exception: `{"noun": "editor", "mode": "normal", "keys": "x", "request": null}` still unbinds `x`. The defaults with a guard of their own are few: `0` in `operator_pending` (`when: count`), the `ctrl+c` capture row (`when: no-modal`), and the rows an application guards. The same goes for code: `Bind("k", row)` without a guard leaves a guarded `k` in place, and `Unbind("k")` leaves it too (use `UnbindGuarded("k", guard)`).
- An application that defined its own `config` noun with an `export` or `path` verb now collides with the framework's (`Register` panics on a duplicate operation); rename its verb.

## v0.5.0 (2026-10-26)

A minor release: inline completion for inputs, a preserved-newlines option for Markdown, and `Runtime.Bind` no longer cancels a key sequence it does not touch.

### Highlights

- Inline completion: an application gives an input triggers such as `@`, `$` or `[[`, each with an asynchronous search. Typing a trigger at the start of a token offers the search's suggestions for the rest of the token in a list drawn under it, while typing keeps editing the same input.
- The token runs from the trigger to the next whitespace (`/` never splits it); the query is the whole token after the trigger, wherever the cursor is inside it. Of overlapping triggers (`[` and `[[`) the longest wins.
- Accepting replaces the whole token and keeps the trigger (`@src/ma` becomes `@src/main.go`) as one undoable edit.
- Searches run off the loop after a 150 ms pause in typing. An answer to a request older than the one on screen is dropped, so a slow, late answer never replaces a newer one; a search error is shown in the list.
- Keys while the list is open: `ctrl+n`/`ctrl+p` or `down`/`up` move, `tab` takes the highlighted suggestion (else the first), `enter` takes it only once one is highlighted (so enter after a typed token still submits or breaks the line), `esc` hides the list and stays in insert. `tab` on a token with the list closed opens it at once, unless the application binds `tab` itself.
- The demo's notes field completes `@` library entries, `$` skills and `[[` pages. o-story stories `completion/*` (typing-opens, look, accept-undo, escape-stays-insert, error-row, mid-token-tab, longest-trigger); a story input takes `completions: [{trigger, items, fail}]`.
- Markdown keeps line breaks on request: `markdown.PreserveNewlines()` makes every newline inside a paragraph a line break (Glamour's `WithPreservedNewLines`), for notes written as plain lines. Long lines still wrap; code blocks and tables draw the same. A Doc with the option keeps its items, highlight and byte-exact toggles. o-story stories `markdown/preserve-newlines` and `markdown/render-preserve-80`.
- `Runtime.Bind` and the builder's `Define(noun).Mode(m).Bind` while a key sequence is pending (`d` of `d d`) keep the sequence unless a row they add or replace is on it: in its mode, with keys equal to, extending or extended by the keys typed so far. Before, any new row cancelled it, so an application binding rows mid-sequence broke `d d`.

### API changes

- `Completions(triggers...)` (an `InstanceOption` read by `AddInput`) and `NounBuilder.Completions(triggers...)`; `Input.Completions`; `Runtime.CompletionTriggers(id)` (the input's own, else its noun's). A trigger must be non-empty, hold no whitespace and have a search.
- `CompletionTrigger{Trigger, Search}`, `CompletionSearch func(ctx, query) ([]Suggestion, error)`, `Suggestion{Text, Label, Detail}`, `SearchStrings(func(ctx, query) ([]string, error))`.
- `FindCompletionToken(text, cursor, triggers) (CompletionToken, int, bool)` and `CompletionToken{Start, End, Trigger, Query}`.
- `Runtime.Completer() *Completer`: `Open`, `Input`, `Token`, `Suggestions`, `Err`, `Highlighted`, `Searching`, `State`, `Debounce`, `DebounceTick`, `DebounceCurrent`; `CompletionDebounceMsg{Seq}`, `DefaultCompletionDebounce`, `CompletionNoun`.
- The `completion` noun: `completion.open` (guard `completion-available`), `completion.move {delta}` (a motion), `completion.accept`, `completion.dismiss`; rows in insert mode on the noun (`esc`, `tab`, `enter`, `ctrl+n`, `ctrl+p`, `down`, `up`) and `tab` → `completion.open` on the editor noun. Guards `completion-rows` and `completion-highlighted`.
- The stack walk reads a focused instance's inner level first (the open suggestion list); nothing else sets one.
- `markdown.Option` and `markdown.PreserveNewlines()`; `markdown.Block(text, width, appearance, opts...)` and `markdown.New(r, id, parent, src, opts...)` take options (existing calls compile unchanged). Renderings are cached per option. A story's markdown spec takes `preserveNewlines`.

### Migrating

- The Markdown option needs no change. An application that relied on `Bind` clearing a pending sequence after binding an unrelated row now keeps the sequence; one that binds a row on the sequence still has it cancelled.
- Completion needs no change: `tab` in insert mode on an input with no triggers is unchanged (the new editor row's verb is guarded off there). An application that binds `tab`, `enter`, `esc`, `up`/`down` or `ctrl+n`/`ctrl+p` on an input with triggers keeps them while the list is closed; while it is open the list's rows answer first.

## v0.4.0 (2026-10-26)

Requires oden v0.1.1. A minor release: UI services on `Ctx` for telling the user something from a handler, resizable list/detail panes, and a new `markdown` package. Two behavior changes to check when upgrading: `Model.Notify` now returns a `NoticeID`, and a key clears `Model.Status` before it runs rather than after.

### Highlights

- UI services on `Ctx`: handlers say what to tell the user (`ctx.Notify`, `ctx.ClearNotice`, `ctx.Status`) and each host decides how to show it: the footer in the TUI, stderr on the CLI, `Response.Messages` on a socket or API call. No `Runtime.Model`.
- Notices and status lines now print on the CLI: `cli.Shell` writes `notice: TEXT` and `status: TEXT` on stderr beside its `warning:` lines, and `ochazuke NOUN VERB` prints a call's messages the same way.
- Resizable panes: in normal mode `>` grows a page's list pane, `<` shrinks it, `=` resets the divider; counts repeat (`3>`). Steps of 5% of the split, at most 30 points either way, each side keeps 16 cells (3 rows stacked), so neither pane collapses; stacked (narrow) layouts resize the lists over the detail the same way. The keys answer, and the footer shows `>/< resize`, only on a page whose last frame drew a split (guard `pane-split`).
- The divider is remembered per page in `panes.json` in the state directory (`Config.PanesPath()`, atomic writes); nothing is written when the config is disabled. A file that does not parse, or a write that fails, is a footer notice.
- Splits read it: `Layout.Split`, `SplitBlock` and `SplitFocusBlock` divide by `Layout.Shift`, which `Runtime.PaneLayout(page, layout)` sets from the page; `Layout.Divide` does the same for a split drawn by hand. Catalog pages with a detail (`cli.MountPage`) and the demo's Library get it with no application code.
- Markdown, in a new package `markdown`: `markdown.Block` renders with Glamour v2 in the appearance's style, and `markdown.Doc` is a document whose task items you move between with `j`/`k`, toggle with `x` and undo or redo with `u`/`ctrl+r`. Each change is saved back to its source by rewriting only the box character. A toggle of an item that is gone or changed in the source is refused with an error and nothing is written. The demo has a Checklist page.
- o-story: `"state": true` gives a story its own state directory, `{"restart": true}` rebuilds the session into it, and the `shift` expectation reads the page's divider; stories `panes/*` (grow, shrink, clamp, stacked, remembered, no-split) and `markdown/*` (render, toggle, refind, refused, moves, spills).

### API changes

- `ctx.Notify(text) NoticeID`, `ctx.ClearNotice(id)`, `ctx.Status(text)`; the same on `Runtime.UI()` (`*UI`) for code without a `Ctx` (setup, daemon events).
- `UISink` (`Notify(id, text)`, `ClearNotice(id)`, `Status(text)`) and `UI.Attach(sink) UISink` (returns the previous sink; nil detaches), `UI.Sink()`. With no sink messages are discarded, except that the last 32 posted before the first attach (the startup ring) are replayed into that sink, then dropped.
- `UIMessage{Kind, ID, Text}` (kinds `UINotice`, `UIClearNotice`, `UIStatus`, `UIMessage.Apply(sink)`) and `RecordingSink` (`Messages`, `Notices`, `LastStatus`) for tests.
- `NewModel` attaches the `Model` as the sink: notices go to the footer and the status line as before. `Model.Notify(text)` now posts through the UI and returns the `NoticeID` (it returned nothing).
- A key clears `Model.Status` before it runs, so a status its handler sets (`ctx.Status`) stands until the next key; before, a successful key cleared it afterwards. A key's error still replaces it.
- `Response.Messages` (`messages`, omitempty): what a `HandleCall` (socket or API) call posted while it ran, also sent to the runtime's sink. `CallResponse(ctx, path, q)` returns the whole `Response`; `Call` is unchanged.
- The `UI` component is an instance (`__ui`) under `app`; `Runtime` gains no field.
- Panes: `Layout.Shift`, `Layout.Divide(first, total, least)`, `Runtime.PaneLayout`, `Runtime.PaneShift`, `Runtime.SetPaneShift`, `Config.PanesPath`, `PanesFile` and the constants `PaneStep`, `PaneShiftLimit`, `PaneMinWidth`, `PaneMinHeight`; operations `pane.grow`, `pane.shrink {steps, page}` and `pane.reset {page}`, rows `>` `<` `=` on `app` in normal mode.
- `markdown.Block(text, width, appearance)` and `markdown.StyleOf(appearance)`: Glamour's standard `dark` or `light` style from the appearance's background, `notty` under mono and NO_COLOR. Renderings are cached by text, width and style.
- `markdown.New(r, id, parent, src)` returns a `*markdown.Doc` (`View`, `Toggle`, `Undo`, `Redo`, `Reload`, `Tasks`, `Selected`, `Select`, `Focus`, `ListID`). It is on the `markdown` noun (`markdown.toggle`, `markdown.undo`, `markdown.redo`, `markdown.reload`; `x`, `u`, `ctrl+r` in normal mode), with `markdown-task` rows.
- `markdown.Source{Load, Save}` and `markdown.File(path)`, which writes atomically. Before a toggle the source is loaded again and the item found again by its text; when it is gone, changed or ambiguous the toggle, undo or redo fails with an error wrapping `markdown.ErrRefused` (the CLI exits non-zero; the TUI shows it as the key's error in the footer status), and the view shows the source as it is now.
- `markdown.Tasks(text)` lists task items with the byte offset of each box (goldmark with GFM, as Glamour parses).
- `Appearance.Background()` reports the resolved background (`light` or `dark`) and whether the appearance is plain.
- Dependencies: `charm.land/glamour/v2` v2.0.1 and `github.com/yuin/goldmark` v1.7.8 (with chroma, bluemonday and goldmark-emoji indirect). Lip Gloss stays v2.0.6 and Bubble Tea v2.1.0.

### Binary size

- `markdown` is a package of its own, so only a binary that imports it carries Glamour, goldmark and chroma. The demo binary (`cmd/ochazuke`, which imports it for the Checklist page) grows from 10.2 MB to 20.0 MB, mostly chroma's lexers; `examples/hello`, which does not, is 9.4 MB as before (+37 KB for the UI and panes code). Measured with `-trimpath` on darwin/arm64.

### Migrating

- In a handler, `a.m.Notify(x)` becomes `ctx.Notify(x)` and `a.m.Status = x` becomes `ctx.Status(x)`; keep the returned `NoticeID` to `ctx.ClearNotice(id)` instead of editing `Model.Notices`.
- Without a `Ctx` (setup, a daemon event), `a.r.UI().Notify(x)` / `a.r.UI().Status(x)`.
- A caller of `Model.Notify` that used it as a statement keeps compiling; one that passed it as a `func(string)` needs a wrapper.
- Tests that asserted on `m.Notices` keep working with a `Model`; without one, attach a `RecordingSink`.
- `>`, `<` and `=` are new default rows on `app` in normal mode (guarded by `pane-split`); an application with its own rows on those keys should check `bindings list` on its split pages.

## v0.3.0 (2026-10-26)

Requires oden v0.1.1. A minor release: new public API (`Column.Wrap`, `Column.Max`, `ListItem.Section`, `Binding.FromRow`).

### Highlights

- Tables wrap long cells (`Column.Wrap`), cap flexible columns (`Column.Max`) and draw section labels (`ListItem.Section`).
- Binding rows can pass the acted-on row's data as an argument (`Binding.FromRow`, `"row:<field>"` in binding files).
- Confirm prompts for CLI calls without a target read the request's arguments instead of naming `app`.
- The `?` help is a list table (`helpColumns`: key, operation, arguments, description) instead of its own column layout: a header line, the selected row as the reverse highlight alone (no `> ` marker), section labels and wrapping from the table. Content and order are unchanged.

### API changes

- `Column.Wrap`: in `List.View` a long cell wraps onto further lines of its row instead of being cut (`TableText` still cuts). `Column.Max` caps a flexible column's growth. Both are in the catalog's `columns` (`wrap`, `max`).
- `ListItem.Section`: a table draws a label line above each run of rows in one section, repeated at the top of a window that starts inside it.
- Confirm prompts without a target instance: a request that addresses no instance of the verb's noun (a CLI call with positional words) expands `{{noun}}` to the verb's noun and `{{id}}`, `{{target}}`, `{{label}}` to the argument of that name, else its first argument in `ParameterOrder`, instead of naming `app` (o-git gap).
- `Binding.FromRow(arg, field)` (`SourceRow`; `"row:<field>"` or `"row"` in a binding file's `from`): a binding row passes the acted-on row's `Data` as an argument (a map key or struct JSON field, dotted for nested values; the whole value JSON-shaped), reading the focused row also when the row is bound on the list; a missing field leaves the argument out, and `ValidateBindings` rejects a malformed field (o-git gap).

## v0.2.0 (2026-10-26)

Requires oden v0.1.1. A minor release: `Response.Result` changes type, and the shell, the `Model` and the default keys gain behaviour hosts should know about.

### Highlights

- User configuration for every app: `bindings.json`, typed `settings.json` with an in-place editor, `actions.json` (external commands as verbs), user colour schemes (`themes.json`) and a persisted action history, under `~/.config/<app>` and `~/.local/state/<app>`.
- New framework components: the chooser (`Runtime.OpenChooser`) and the `'` page picker (recent pages first), schema-driven record editors (forms written as you type, with suggestions), scrollable page details, and catalog-declared data pages with widgets that a host mounts without the extension's Go code.
- The oden TUI seam: `cli.TUI(app)` gives any oden tool a Commands page whose commands run in an attached pseudo-terminal (`cli.StartPty`).
- CLI output formats: `--format json|table|markdown|toon` on every shell command.
- `?` help shows each row's arguments; palette commands that return a list of objects open a read-only Results table.
- Counts on `list.move`, the jobs ticker on the footer rule, a configurable terminal leader, linear-time list and tree updates.

### API changes

- `HandleCall` returns `Response.Result` as a `json.RawMessage` (the handler's value encoded once, so object keys keep the order the value marshals in; `Lines` stays `Lines`; a nil or null result is nil). Code that type-switched on the decoded `map[string]any`/`[]any` must unmarshal the bytes itself (`fix/cli-gaps`).
- `Layout.BodySizeWith(f)` now equals `Layout.BodySize()`: the jobs ticker takes no row, it rides the rule line above the footer (`Layout.TickerRule`). Kept for callers; new code can call `BodySize` (`fix/uxr2-batch`).
- `Model.Init` now loads the user bindings file and the settings and batches the settings poll with `tea.RequestBackgroundColor`. A host that drives the `Model` without calling `Init` (headless tests) gets neither.
- New default row: `'` on `app` in normal mode runs `page.pick` (only with no modal open). An app that bound `'` keeps its own row.
- `HelpRow` gains `Args` (the row's arguments, resolved when help opens), so code comparing whole `HelpRow` values sees the new field.
- The framework now registers the `chooser` noun: an app that registers its own (mtm's `cmd/mtm/chooser.go`) must drop it, or registration panics.

### The oden TUI seam (`feat/oden-seam`)

- `cli.StartPty` / `cli.PtyStream`: a `TerminalStream` for a local process, run in a pseudo-terminal (`charmbracelet/x/xpty`) and emulated with `charmbracelet/x/vt`; frames carry the cursor cell, shape and visibility; `Resize`, keys and bracketed paste reach the program; `Release` hangs up and kills the process group and closes the pty, once. New dependencies: `x/xpty` v0.1.4 (with `creack/pty`), `x/vt` v0.1.0.
- `cli.AddCommands` / `CommandSource` / `OdenCommands`: the `command` noun and a Commands page over an oden tool's catalog (inline commands and `<bin>-<name>` on PATH, with shadowing); `command run` opens the command in a terminal under its row, attached (`ctrl+a d` detaches), `command help` shows its help in the detail (an external's `--help` as a job), `which`, `stop` (confirmed).
- `cli.TUI(app)` as an oden `Default` gives any oden tool that page; `cli.NewCommandsModel` for hosts and smoke runs. `examples/hello` uses it.
- o-story: a page with `commands` is the Commands page; stories `commands/*`.

### Lists, help and the ticker (`fix/uxr2-batch`)

- Counts on `list.move` (`3j`, `5k`): moves that many rows, stops at the edge, spills as one move only from the edge.
- A focused empty list draws `(empty list)` reversed, so a spill into it shows where the focus went.
- `?` help hides a row whose guard is false at the focus, as the footer and the walk do; `bindings list` keeps it.
- The jobs ticker rides the rule line above the footer (`── noun.verb running · …`) instead of taking rows, so a job never moves or hides the body. Story `ticker/keeps-body`.

### CLI (`fix/cli-gaps`)

- A `.Quiet()` verb's job prints no `job #N` line unless it fails.
- Immediate results keep the handler's key order (the `Response.Result` change above); o-csv no longer needs Pending verbs for header order.
- A bare `--json` (last, or before another flag) asks for the JSON output the shell always prints and is ignored, instead of eating the next word as arguments.

### CLI output formats (`feat/cli-format`)

- `--format json|table|markdown|toon` on every shell command (`cli.Formats`; json stays the default, byte for byte). A list or state verb's rows (an array of objects, `Lines` of `Row` included) print one table per noun: the noun's declared columns over the rows' `cells`, else the records' fields (id and label first, data's fields last).
- `table` cuts with `…` and drops columns by `Priority` only when stdout is a terminal (`Shell.Width` overrides); `markdown` is a GitHub table; `toon` is `NOUN[N]{col,...}:` and a line per row, quoting only values with a comma, a quote or a line break (the o-qbt rule). A non-tabular result prints JSON with a stderr note.
- `--format` combines with `--json '{...}'`; a bare `--json` with another format is an error; a verb with its own `format` parameter (o-qbt, o-ai) keeps `--format` as that argument.
- `ochazuke.TableText(cols, rows, width)`: the list's column layout as plain text lines (width 0: no cut).

### Page details (`feat/detail-scroll`)

- `Runtime.AddDetail(id, item func() string, Parent(page))`, `Runtime.Detail(id)`, `Detail.Window(text, width, height)` (wraps, windows, returns a `12–40 / 300` marker), `Detail.Scroll`/`Offset`/`Overflows`/`Marker`/`Item`.
- `detail.scroll` on `J`/`K` (app rows, counts work, guarded by `detail-scrolls`): the detail scrolls while the list keeps the focus; an inner `J`/`K` (the outline's moves) answers first; a new item starts at the top. The footer shows one `J/K detail` hint only while the detail overflows (a motion row with its own `Hint` now shows). Demo: a Library page.

### Many pages (`feat/many-pages`)

- The tab row windows around the active tab with `‹N` and `N›` counts for the pages hidden on each side.
- The framework chooser, lifted from mtm: `Runtime.OpenChooser(ChooserSpec{Kind, Title, Rows, Filter, Choose, Create, CreateLabel, CreateRow, Empty})`, `Runtime.Chooser(kind)`, `ChooserRow`, `Chooser.SetRows`/`Matches`/`Highlighted`/`Query`/`Moved`; keys are rows on the `chooser` noun (insert pinned). mtm must delete `cmd/mtm/chooser.go` when it adopts this version: both register the `chooser` noun, and a second registration panics.
- `ChooserSpec.Keep []int` (from mtm, UX review 2 F22): the cell columns a narrow chooser cuts last; the other columns narrow to their minimum (6) first. mtm's directory chooser passes `Keep: []int{1}` (the path).
- `ChooserSpec.Preview func(ChooserRow) string` and `ChooserPreviewMin` (60), from mtm's context picker: from that width the highlighted row's preview is drawn on the right (three fifths rows, the rest preview, a fixed-width column so the panel does not resize as the highlight moves; wrapped, cut with `…`); narrower, no preview.
- The page picker: `page.pick`, `Runtime.OpenPagePicker()`, on `'`; every page, the ones visited before most recent first, then the never-visited in registration order, then the current page last, marked `current`, so `'` enter goes back to the page last left; type to filter, enter opens, esc closes. Story `picker/page-picker-recent-first`.

### User configuration (`feat/user-config`)

- Every app loads `~/.config/<app>/bindings.json` (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/<app>`) at start: `Runtime.Config()` (`Config{App, Dir}`, `Path`, `Directory`, `Disable`, `BindingProblems`), `Runtime.EnsureUserBindings`/`ReloadUserBindings`/`CheckUserBindings`, verbs `bindings.reload|path|check`; a bad row becomes a notice, never a crash. The CLI applies the same file. Constants `BindingsFile`, `SettingsFile`.
- Declared typed settings in `settings.json`: `Runtime.DeclareSetting(Setting{Name, Type, Default, Description, Enum})` (string enums, integer, number, boolean, duration), `Runtime.Settings()` with `Int`/`String`/`Bool`/`Float`/`Duration`/`Value`/`Get`/`Set`/`Reset`/`List`/`Observe`/`Reload`, `settings.*` verbs, atomic writes, a 1 s poll (`SettingsPoll`, `SettingsPollMsg`) only when settings are declared.
- `settings.edit`: an in-place settings editor modal (a table of `setting` rows, every key saves at once; no default key). The demo's theme and density are settings.

### User actions (`feat/config-actions`)

- `actions.json` beside `bindings.json` (`ActionsFile`): each entry adds a verb to an existing noun that runs an external command, an application verb like any other (binding rows, the palette, the CLI, `?` help, the catalog). `Action{Noun, Verb, Description, Command, Base, Call, Target, JSON, Label, Confirm, Parameters, Positional, Dir, Timeout, Quiet}`, `Argv`; `Runtime.EnsureUserActions`/`ReloadUserActions`/`UserActions`, `Config.ActionProblems`; verbs `actions.list|reload|path`. `Model.Init`, `cli.Mount` and `cli.Shell.Run` install the file before the user bindings.
- `command` is an argv template run without a shell and without stdin; `base` is the JSON convention `BASE NOUN VERB --json ARGS [--target T]` (`call` renames the noun and verb). Templates: `{{target}}`/`{{id}}`, `{{label}}`, `{{noun}}`, `{{verb}}`, `{{data}}`, `{{data.KEY}}`, `{{args}}`, a parameter; an empty lone placeholder word is dropped. The work is a `Pending` job labelled `label` (default the program); stdout is the result (JSON, JSON Lines as a list, else text), a non-zero exit fails with stderr's last line, `timeout` kills it.
- A malformed entry is skipped and is a notice (a CLI `warning:`). Commands come only from the user's own file, never from a catalog, a row or a call; the catalog never carries a command (README "User actions").
- A confirm prompt's `{{target}}` now expands to the instance id, as `{{id}}` does.

### Terminal leader setting (`feat/config-actions`)

- `terminal-leader` (`TerminalLeaderSetting`, default `DefaultTerminalLeader` `ctrl+a`): the terminal leader is a declared setting, one `ctrl+` or `alt+` key. `Runtime.EnsureTerminalLeader()` declares it unless the app did and applies settings.json's value and every later change; an app with terminals calls it at setup (`cli.AddCommands` and the full-project example do), so an app without terminals never lists it. `EnsureConfigSettings` (Init) applies an app-declared one. `Runtime.SetTerminalLeader(key)` rewrites the rows (every noun's single-key `leader_pending` entry row with its prefix and timeout request, the self-send row, hints naming the old leader); application `leader_pending` rows stay. `Runtime.TerminalLeader()`. `AddTerminal` applies it, so leader rows defined after `Init` move too. The old leader is ordinary input to the pane.
- `Setting.Validate` (code only): a further check after the type; `Set` refuses a value it rejects, a file value it rejects reads the default.
- o-story: the attached pane's line names the current leader; stories `terminal/leader-twice-sends-one`, `leader-unbound-forwards-both`, `leader-timeout-sends-leader`, `leader-setting-footer`, `leader-setting-detach`.
- mtm: its leader row moves with the setting, but `terminal.keys`' modal labels still print `ctrl+a` literally; it should read `Runtime.TerminalLeader()`.

### Record editor (`feat/record-editor`)

- Schema-driven forms written as you type: `RecordSpec{Title, Fields, Load, Save, Debounce, Insert, OnLeave}`, `RecordField{Name, Label, Parameter, Multiline, Validate}`, `RecordFields(op)` from a verb's parameters, `Runtime.AddRecordEditor` (placed on a page) and `Runtime.OpenRecordEditor` (a modal), `Runtime.RecordEditor(id)`, `Record`, `RecordWrite`, `RecordDebounceMsg`, `DefaultRecordDebounce`. Invalid values are never written; enum, sourced and boolean fields are chosen from a list; `u`/`ctrl+r` undo and redo as writes; no save key. Nouns `form`, `form-field`, `form-text`, `form-choice`, `form-option`.
- `ModalBody` (`ModalTitle`, `ModalBlock`): a modal whose component draws itself needs no `ModalView`. The record editor, its choice list and the framework `Chooser` draw through it.
- `Appearance.Accent` (code only) overrides the theme's accent colour, e.g. for a pin state; mono and `NO_COLOR` stay colourless.

### Form suggestions and page row hooks (`feat/form-hooks`)

- Record editor: a field whose `Parameter` is `Free` with `Enum` or `Source` is a text field with suggestions (noun `FormSuggestNoun`, `form-suggest`): typing stays free text and a value outside the suggestions is written as typed; `enter` (normal) or `ctrl+space` runs the new verb `form.suggest`, which opens the framework chooser (`FormSuggestionsKind`, `form-suggestions`) over the Enum then the Source verb's result, loaded off the loop as a job of the chooser when the Source is Pending; a pick (or the typed query, `+ use "…"`) fills the field and is written at once. Before, such a field was plain text with no suggestions (mtm's tool forms lost their pick lists). Such a field is one line high. Story `forms/suggestions`.
- `cli.PageConfig.OnRows func(list string, rows []cli.Row)`: runs on the loop after a list's rows land (a reload's answer, a state answer filed under each list, an empty one included, or the host's `SetRows`/`SetState`), with the rows as filed; a failed load does not call it. A host follows a list's rows without wrapping `Reload` (nabe's `followReload`).

### List scaling (`fix/list-scaling`)

- `SetItems` removes dropped rows in one batch (one child index for the batch, not a tree scan per row), trees index node positions, depths and child counts, and tables measure their cells once per item set and draw only the window. Benchmarks in `scaling_test.go` (`SetItemsReplace`, `TreeBuild`, `TreeExpand`, `TableView`) now scale linearly.

### Integration fixes

- `'` (`page.pick`) answers only with no modal open (guard `no-modal`). Without the guard it opened the page picker over `?` help, the settings editor and a record editor, and choosing a page then failed. Story `picker/page-picker-not-in-modal`.
- The catalog's `free` flag is now pinned by a test and documented on `CatalogVerb`. A free sourced parameter (mtm's `--model`) is published as `"free": true`, so a host (nabe) can tell it from a closed one. The field was already written through `ochazuke.Parameter`'s JSON tag; nothing tested or documented it.

### Small gaps (`fix/small-gaps`)

- `Result.Label(label)` names one job of a verb (`Pending(work).Label("claude")`): `JobInfo.Label` in `jobs.list`, the ticker entry reads `noun.verb label state`, the shell's stderr line `job #N noun.verb label STATE`. Story `ticker/job-label`.
- `cli.CatalogDoc.ListColumns(noun)` and `CatalogDoc.RowSource(noun)`: the columns a host's list of a catalog noun's rows draws, following the row source verb (`rows`, else `list`) to the noun its rows answer to. A host mounts with `ochazuke.Columns(doc.ListColumns(noun)...)`; this is nabe's `columnsOf`/`rowSource`, which nabe can now drop.
- `bindings stops` (`Runtime.StopBindings`): the effective rows of every focus stop, app-wide, each read at the focus a jump lands on and in the mode it would have there: pages in tab order, stops in drawing order, open modals, then `app`. `bindings list --all` stays the export of every stored row; `stops` is what answers where.
- Fixed: focusing a list row instance (`SetFocus`/`focus.set`, `RetargetFocus`, a `FocusOnClose` landing, a page starting on a row, `Pop`) left the list's selection, and its highlight, on the old row while keys answered for the new one. The focused row is now selected. Story `lists/focus-set-selects-row`; `lists/one-highlight` checks the highlighted row is the focused one.
- Fixed: the confirm modal drew its keys twice, a hand-written `y yes · n no` under the prompt and the derived hint line (with `<any> key.ignore`). The body is the prompt alone and the swallowing row is hidden, so the hint line reads `y confirm.accept · n modal.pop`. Story `modals/confirm-one-hint`.
- `Page.OnOpen func(r, from)`: a page-opened hook, run on the loop each time `OpenPage` (page.open, page.next/previous, the picker) makes the page current from another page, after its focus and mode are restored; reopening the current page does not run it. Set it on the `*Page` `AddPage` returns.
- Fixed: a job launched from a chooser (`Choose` or `Create` returning `Pending`) failed with `unknown instance <kind>/query`, because picking closes the modal and its input before the callback runs. An application verb whose handler removed its own instance now launches on the nearest surviving instance of its old ancestry: the focus the chooser opened over.
- Changed: once a page has drawn a frame, a focus stop the last frame did not draw (a hidden panel) is skipped by `focus.next`/`previous` and list spills, instead of following the drawn stops; mtm no longer needs to mark a hidden tree `Unfocusable`. Before the first frame (the CLI) the walk is still tree order; a modal's stops are never skipped. An application view that draws a stop must call `Runtime.Placed` (the framework's views do). Story `motions/undrawn-stop-skipped`.
- `Layout.SplitFocusBlock(side, …)` with `SplitLeft`/`SplitRight`/`SplitNone`: a split whose focused panel is named (accent title and border; its cursor wins), for a view whose focus moves between panes (o-json). `Split`/`SplitBlock` keep the left focused. Story `chrome/split-focused-side`; o-story gains `layout: "split"` and the cell-exact `styled` expectation.
- `NoMarks()` (an `InstanceOption` for `AddList` and `AddTree`): a read-only view whose rows take no marks; `space` no longer answers with `list.mark` there (the verb is guarded by `list-marks`, so the footer and `?` leave it out) and `list.mark`/`List.ToggleMark` refuse. Replaces o-json's hidden `key.ignore` row. Story `lists/no-marks`.

### Persisted history and colour schemes (`feat/history-themes`)

- The action history persists per app: `history.jsonl` in the state dir (`Config.StateDirectory()`: `Config.StateDir`, else `$XDG_STATE_HOME/<app>`, else `~/.local/state/<app>`; `Config.HistoryPath()`, `history.path`). `Model.Init` restores the last entries (`HistoryEntry.Restored`, before the session's own, so palette up/down recalls them and dot-repeat skips them) through `Runtime.EnsureHistory`; one writer goroutine appends off the loop and compacts at twice the limit (temp + rename); `Config.Flush` drains it on quit. Unparsable lines are one notice and are dropped. Constants `HistoryFile`, `HistorySetting`.
- `history-limit` is a framework setting (`Runtime.EnsureConfigSettings`, called by `Model.Init` and the CLI shell, declares it unless the app did; 0 keeps none; it also bounds the ring). `Config.NoHistoryFile` opts an app out; a disabled config (stories, test binaries) keeps no file.
- The file keeps palette lines (failed ones too) and other completed operations, never text editing or its plumbing (the `input`, `editor`, `key`, `count`, `register`, `macro`, `yank-history`, `mode`, `palette` and `terminal` nouns), and never result values. Text-like arguments (`text`, `value`, `keys`, `args`, `password`, `secret`, `token`) are dropped unless the operation sets `Operation.PersistArgs` (`NounBuilder.PersistArgs()`; `settings.set` does), so a palette `command run deploy --token=…` does not write the token.
- Colour schemes: `Scheme` (roles `text`, `muted`, `accent`, `border`, `surface`, `error` as `SchemeColor{Light, Dark}`, plus `background`), `SchemeSet`/`NewSchemeSet`, `ParseSchemes`, `Appearance.Schemes`; `Appearance.Theme` names a built-in or a scheme. User schemes are `themes.json` beside `settings.json` (`ThemesFile`); a scheme that does not validate is skipped with a notice. The framework declares the `theme` setting (`ThemeSetting`; choices: the built-ins, then themes.json's) unless the app declared one (`Config.FrameworkTheme()`), and the Model applies it. `Runtime.ReloadThemes`, `Config.Schemes`, verbs `themes.reload`, `themes.path`, `themes.list`. `Appearance.Accent` still goes on top; mono and `NO_COLOR` stay colourless.
- o-story: a story's `themes` field; stories `chrome/scheme-light`, `scheme-user`, `scheme-user-accent`, `scheme-malformed`.

### Catalog pages, widgets and the target resolver (`feat/catalog-pages`)

- Data pages: `cli.CatalogPage` gains `layout` (`stack`, `split`, `groups`), `lists` (`{id, noun, source, title}`) and `detail` (`{title, fields}`). An extension declares one with `cli.DeclarePage(r, p)` and `cli.Catalog` publishes it whole; a Go-drawn page stays `{id, title}`.
- A host builds it with no Go code of the extension's: `cli.MountPage(m, doc, p, cli.PageConfig{Noun, Fetch})` / `cli.MountPages(m, doc, cfg)` return a `*cli.PageView` (`ListsRender`, `Reload`, `SetRows`, `SetState`, `SetError`, `SetStatus`, `Row`, `List`). Loads are quiet `catalog-load` jobs; rows are `<list>/<row id>` instances of the host's noun with `Data` the row's id. `cli.DecodeRows` reads a list answer (array or JSON Lines). This is nabe's hand-built mount page as framework code.
- Widgets: `pages[].widgets` (`cli.CatalogWidget{id, kind, title, source, fields, columns, rows}`), drawn by the framework above a data page's lists: `kv` (a key/value panel), `status` (a line with a progress bar and state), `table` (columns over cells or fields, capped rows). Data from the widget's `source` on `Reload` or `PageView.SetWidget`. The plan's reserved top-level `widgets` lives on the page it draws on.
- `Runtime.ResolveTarget func(target string) (string, error)` and `Runtime.TargetInstance(target)`: a request whose target is not an instance asks the resolver for the instance standing for it before `ExecuteAt` refuses it (`unknown target`), so a foreign id reaches its row from keys, the palette, the CLI and the API; a resolver error is the refusal, `target "X": …`. A confirm stores the resolved target. `cli.Shell.Reveal` now runs only for a target the runtime cannot resolve yet, and the resolver maps it after Reveal loaded the rows.
- o-story: a page with `catalog` is a mounted data page over canned `answers`; stories `catalog/*`.

### Also since v0.1.1

- Framework `bindings list --all` lists every stored noun and instance binding table as configuration JSON, including inactive modes and contexts. The default listing remains focus- and mode-specific. The CSV example now exposes framework operations through `csv NOUN VERB`, including `csv bindings list --all`; the socket CLI also accepts `--all`.
- Keyboard help shows bold, underlined binding-owner groups from inner to outer, with movement last. Scrolling within a group keeps its owner heading visible, even when its bindings invoke another noun's operations.
- Keyboard help adds an arguments column: values resolved when help opens, unknown pattern-key sources shown as placeholders, and long values truncated with `…`. Full previews are available in `HelpRow.Args`.
- Palette commands returning JSON object arrays open read-only result tables, including pending completions. Columns rank by field presence (universal first, then most common; alphabetical ties); sparse cells, nulls, nested JSON, empty lists, and exact integers are preserved. Bindings, APIs, background refreshes, and existing modals stay unchanged.

## 2026-10-26 04:06

### Root and libraries
- changed: `ochazuke` commit `681be7f`; was `792010d`

## v0.1.1 (2026-10-25)

Requires oden v0.1.1. Fixes from mtm's test pass:

- `Tree.Record(TreeAction{ID, Label, Undo, Redo})`: an application change kept outside the nodes (a task status) goes on an outline's undo stack in order with the tree's edits. Story `tree/undo-app-action`.
- The footer shows only the newest notice, cut to half the width with `(+N more)`, so notices never hide the hints. Story `notices/footer-keeps-hints`.
- An argument error (`ArgumentError`, `Response.Argument`) adds no `pass --target, one of: …` hint in the CLI.
- `?` help: a row's `Help` replaces its operation's description (the `"`, `q`, `@` and count rows now say what they do); a generated prefix cancel (`d esc`) is listed only while a sequence on its prefix answers.

## v0.1.0 (2026-10-25)

First tagged release. Requires Go 1.26 and oden v0.1.0.

- Bubble Tea v2 application framework: retained pages, nouns and instances, one focus field and one global mode, one stack walk for actions and bindings, binding rows (`Define`, bindings files, capture rows, verb guards), `ValidateBindings`.
- Components: lists and tables (lists with columns), the tree and outline editor (`AddTree`), modal text inputs with an undo tree, registers and macros, the file picker, terminal instances, partial-screen modals and confirm prompts.
- Chrome: the ex-mode command line and palette with parameter choices, footer hints as `key noun.verb`, `?` bindings help, a notice line and the two-row jobs ticker.
- Runtime: operations with a delivery contract for off-loop jobs, action history with dot-repeat, tree queries, and live control over a Unix socket.
- `cli`: the generic NOUN VERB shell, the catalog answer and `cli.Mount` into oden command groups.
- `story` and `o-story`: stories as data played headless against the real chrome, and session recordings.
